Hecke L L -values, definite Shimura sets and Mod ℓ \ell non-vanishing
Ashay A. Burungale, Wei He, Shinichi Kobayashi and Kazuto Ota

Abstract
. Let λ \lambda be a self-dual Hecke character over an imaginary quadratic field K K of infinity type ( 1 , 0 ) (1,0) . Let ℓ \ell and p p be primes coprime to 6 ​ N K / ℚ ​ ( cond r ​ λ ) 6{\rm N}_{K/{\mathbb{Q}}}(\mathrm{cond^{r}}{\lambda}) . We determine the ℓ \ell -adic valuation of Hecke L L -values L ⁡ ( 1 , λ ​ χ ) / Ω K L(1,\lambda\chi)/\Omega_{K} as χ \chi varies over p p -power order anticyclotomic characters over K K . As an application, for p p inert in K K , we prove vanishing of the μ \mu -invariant of Rubin’s p p -adic L L -function, leading to the first result on the classical problem of determination of the μ \mu -invariant of imaginary quadratic fields at non-split primes. Our results and approach complement the prior work, primarily due to Hida and Finis. The approach is rooted in the arithmetic of a CM form on a definite Shimura set, relying on Ratner’s ergodicity of unipotent flows and a new local result on test vectors for supercuspidal representations. The application to Rubin’s p p -adic L L -function builds on recent progress in CM Iwasawa theory initiated by resolution of Rubin’s conjecture. Along the way, we present an automorphic view on Rubin’s supersingular Iwasawa theory.



1.
Introduction

Special values of
L
-functions mysteriously encode arithmetic.
As underlying motives vary in a family,
a basic problem is whether the
L
-values are generically non-zero.
If so, a finer problem: mod
\ell
non-vanishing of algebraic part of the
L
-values for a fixed prime
\ell
.
The main results of
this paper establish the mod
\ell
non-vanishing
of central Hecke
L
-values in self-dual families over imaginary quadratic fields (cf. Theorems
1.1
,
1.3
and
1.5
).
They lead to the first result on the classical problem of determination of the
\mu
-invariant of imaginary quadratic fields at non-split primes (cf. Corollary
1.7
).

The study of mod
\ell
non-vanishing of Hecke
L
-values goes back to the 80’s.
The first results are independently due to Gillard
[
30
]
and Schneps
[
79
]
, who
proved the non-vanishing for
p
-adic deformation of a CM elliptic curve arising from the Coates–Wiles
{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
-extension of the CM field
K
for primes
p

split
in
K
.
(Throughout the introduction,
\ell
and
p
denote primes, not necessarily distinct.)
These results build on the pioneering work of Ferrero–Washington
[
29
]
, Washington
[
90
]
and Sinnott
[
81
]
on Dirichlet
L
-values, and are based on Zariski density of torsion points on self-products of the CM elliptic curve modulo
\ell
.
A couple of decades later, Hida initiated and extensively studied
[
39
,
40
,
41
,
42
]

the mod
\ell
non-vanishing of Hecke
L
-values for anticyclotomic deformation of Hecke characters and primes
\ell

split
in
K
.
In contrast to the prior work it relies on the arithmetic of
{\mathrm{GL}}_{2}
-Eisenstein series, studied via
geometry of modular curves and mod
\ell
analogue of the André–Oort conjecture
[
20
]
(Chai–Oort rigidity principle).
A few years later, Finis established
[
27
,
28
]
the mod
\ell
non-vanishing of central Hecke
L
-values in self-dual families arising from the
{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
-anticyclotomic deformation of a self-dual Hecke character
1
1

1

This includes the case of a CM elliptic curve.
for
split
primes
p
.
His notably different approach relies on the arithmetic of
U(1)
-theta functions with complex multiplication, being rooted in Mumford’s theory of theta functions and a variant of the Manin–Mumford conjecture.

The aim of this paper is to treat self-dual cases excluded by methods of Hida and Finis, indicated by
*
below
(cf. Theorems
1.1
and
1.3
).

Table 1.
Mod
\ell
non-vanishing of
p
-anticyclotomic Hecke
L
-values

(\ell,p)

p
split in
K

p
inert in
K

\ell
split in
K

Hida, Finis

*

\ell
inert in
K

Finis

*

Our results have application to CM Iwasawa theory: vanishing of the

\mu
-invariant of Rubin’s
p
-adic
L
-function and that of the imaginary quadratic field
K
at inert primes
p
(cf. Theorem
1.5
, Corollary
1.7
and Remark
1.8
).

This paper introduces a new approach to mod
\ell
non-vanishing of Hecke
L
-values
via arithmetic of CM modular form on a
definite Shimura set. It relies on Ratner’s fundamental ergodicity of unipotent flows
[
74
]
.
To link arithmetic of the definite Shimura set with that of the imaginary quadratic field, a key is
an
\ell
-integral comparison of quaterionic and CM periods (cf. Theorem
1.11
).
We approach the period comparison indirectly via a synthesis of local and global tools, the primary local tool being an explicit construction of
\ell
-optimal test vectors for supercuspidal representations (cf. Theorem
1.12
), which may be of independent interest.
In the
\ell=p
split case our approach gives a different proof of the results of Hida and Finis.

The vanishing of the
\mu
-invariant of Rubin’s
p
-adic
L
-function is a continuation of our study of Rubin’s integral Iwasawa theory of CM elliptic curves at supersingular primes initiated by our resolution of of Rubin’s conjecture in 2021 (cf.
[
14
,
15
,
16
,
17
]
).
While Rubin’s construction of his
p
-adic
L
-function is motivic, relying on elliptic units and his local conjecture encoding epsilon factors, we first construct its automorphic counterpart via the definite Shimura set. This link allows access to automorphic and representation theoretic tools.
The automorphic perspective on Rubin’s theory is at the heart of this paper.

1.1.
Main results

1.1.1.
Setting

Let
K
be an imaginary quadratic field.
Let
\eta_{K}
be the associated quadratic character over
{\mathbb{Q}}
and
h_{K}
the class number.
Let
\lambda
be a (conjugate) self-dual Hecke character over
K
of infinity type
(1,0)
, that is,

\lambda_{\infty}(z)=z^{-1}\text{ and $\lambda^{*}:=\lambda|\cdot|_{{\mathbb{A}}_{K}^{\times}}^{1/2}$ satisfies {$\lambda^{*}|_{{\mathbb{A}}_{{\mathbb{Q}}}^{\times}}=\eta_{K}$. }}

(1.1)

For a prime
p
, let
K_{\infty}
be the anticyclotomic
{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
-extension of
K
. Put
\Gamma={\mathrm{Gal}}(K_{\infty}/K)
and let
\Xi_{p}
denote the set of finite order characters of
\Gamma
.
For
\nu\in\Xi_{p}
, note that
\lambda\nu
is also self-dual. Put

\Xi_{\lambda,p}^{\pm}=\{\nu\in\Xi_{p}|\,\epsilon(\lambda\nu)=\pm 1\},

where
\epsilon(\lambda\nu)
denotes the root number of the Hecke
L
-function
L(s,\lambda\nu)
. We normalise the latter so that
s=1
is the center of the functional equation. If
\nu\in\Xi_{\lambda,p}^{-}
, note that
L(1,\lambda\nu)=0
.

In this paper we consider divisibility properties of algebraic part of the central
L
-values
L(1,\lambda\nu)
for

\nu\in\Xi_{\lambda,p}^{+}
and
p\nmid D_{K}
.
If
p
splits in
K
, then
\epsilon(\lambda\nu)=\epsilon(\lambda)
. On the other hand, for inert
p\nmid 2N_{K/{\mathbb{Q}}}(\mathrm{cond^{r}}{\lambda})
, Greenberg observed an interesting variation

\epsilon(\lambda\nu)=(-1)^{t_{p}+1}\epsilon(\lambda),

where the associated local character
\nu_{p}
is of conductor
p^{t_{p}+1}>1
(cf.
[
32
]
).
(If
p\nmid h_{K}
, then
\mathrm{cond^{r}}{\nu_{p}}=p^{t_{p}+1}
if and only if
{\rm ord}(\nu)=p^{t_{p}}
.)

Let
\ell
be a prime and
v_{\ell}
the
\ell
-adic valuation on
{\mathbb{C}}_{\ell}
so that
v_{\ell}(\ell)=1
.
Fix embeddings
\iota_{\infty}:\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}\hookrightarrow{\mathbb{C}}
and
\iota_{\ell}:\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}\hookrightarrow{\mathbb{C}}_{\ell}
.

To introduce CM period, consider an elliptic curve
E
with complex multiplication by
O_{K}
, defined over a number field
M\subset\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}
, and a non-vanishing invariant differential
\omega
on
E
.
We may extend the field of definition to
{\mathbb{C}}
via
\iota_{\infty}
, and possibly replacing
E
by a Galois conjugate,
obtain

\Omega_{K}\in{\mathbb{C}}^{\times},

uniquely determined up to units in
K
, such that the period lattice of
\omega
on
E
is given by
\Omega_{K}O_{K}
.
For a given prime
\ell
, we normalise the pair
(E,\omega)
so that
E
has good reduction at the
\ell
-adic place
{\mathfrak{l}}
of
M
determined via
\iota_{\ell}
,
and
\omega
reduces modulo
{\mathfrak{l}}
to a non-vanishing invariant differential on the reduced curve
\tilde{E}
. Fix the pair
(E,\omega)
and the resulting period
\Omega_{K}
.

Hurwitz proved that

\frac{L(1,\lambda\nu)}{\Omega_{K}}\in\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}.

A basic problem:

How does
v_{\ell}\left(\frac{L(1,\lambda\nu)}{\Omega_{K}}\right)
vary with
\nu\in\Xi_{\lambda,p}^{+}
?

(Q)

The following local invariants appear in our non-vanishing results. For a prime
q
, put
\mu_{\ell}(\lambda_{q})=\min_{x\in O_{K_{q}}^{\times}}v_{\ell}(\lambda_{q}(x)-1)
and

\mu_{\ell}(\lambda)=\sum_{\text{$q|{\rm{N}}_{K/{\mathbb{Q}}}(\mathrm{cond^{r}}{\lambda})$ inert in $K$}}\mu_{\ell}(\lambda_{q}).

(1.2)

The latter invariant
is closely related to the
\ell
-part of the Tamagawa number associated to
\lambda
.
Its relevance to the problem (
Q
) is due to the lower bound

v_{\ell}\left(\frac{L(1,\lambda\nu)}{\Omega_{K}}\right)\geq\mu_{\ell}(\lambda),

(1.3)

as predicted by the Bloch–Kato conjecture (cf.
[
27
]
).

1.1.2.
(\ell,p)
non-vanishing

Our first main result is the following.

Theorem 1.1
.

Let
\lambda
be a self-dual Hecke character over an imaginary quadratic field
K
of infinity type
(1,0)
.
Let
\ell
and
p
be two different primes which are coprime to
2{\rm{N}}_{K/{\mathbb{Q}}}(\mathrm{cond^{r}}{\lambda})
.
If
\epsilon(\lambda)=-1
, suppose that
\ell\geq 5
.
Then for all but finitely many
\nu\in\Xi_{\lambda,p}^{+}
we have

v_{\ell}\left(\frac{L(1,\lambda\nu)}{\Omega_{K}}\right)=\mu_{\ell}(\lambda).

In view of the Birch and Swinnerton-Dyer formula for rank zero CM abelian varieties
[
77
,
8
]
, Theorem
1.1
has the following application.

Corollary 1.2
.

Let
\lambda
be a self-dual Hecke character over an imaginary quadratic field
K
of infinity type
(1,0)

and
A_{\lambda}
an associated CM abelian variety over
K
.
Let
\ell
and
p
be two different primes which are coprime to
2{\rm{N}}_{K/{\mathbb{Q}}}(\mathrm{cond^{r}}{\lambda})
.
For the
{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
-anticyclotomic extension
K_{\infty}
and a non-negative integer
n
, let
K_{n}
denote its
n
-th layer.

(a)

Suppose that
p
splits in
K
and
\epsilon(\lambda)=+1
. If the Tate–Shafarevich groups
\hbox{\cyr X}(A_{\lambda}/K_{n})[\ell^{\infty}]
are finite for each
n
, then there exists a constant
c
such for any sufficiently large
n
, we have

\#\hbox{\cyr X}(A_{\lambda}/K_{n})[\ell^{\infty}]<c.

(b)

Suppose that
p
is inert in
K
and
2
2

2

The hypothesis
p\nmid h_{K}
is inessential, it only leads to a cleaner formulation of the result.

p\nmid h_{K}
.
Then there exists a constant
c
such that for any sufficiently large
n
with
(-1)^{n+1}=\epsilon(\lambda)
, we have

\#{\mathrm{coker}}(\hbox{\cyr X}(A_{\lambda}/K_{n-1})[\ell^{\infty}]\rightarrow\hbox{\cyr X}(A_{\lambda}/K_{n})[\ell^{\infty}])<c.

1.1.3.

In the
\ell=p
case our main result:

Theorem 1.3
.

Let
\lambda
be a self-dual Hecke character over an imaginary quadratic field
K
of infinity type
(1,0)
.
Let
p\nmid 2{\rm{N}}_{K/{\mathbb{Q}}}(\mathrm{cond^{r}}{\lambda})
be a prime and
\mu_{p}(\lambda)
be as in (
1.2
).

(a)

Suppose that
p
splits in
K
and
{\mathfrak{p}}
the prime of
K
above
p
determined via the embedding
\iota_{p}
. Suppose
that
\epsilon(\lambda)=+1
.
Then there exists an integer
c_{\lambda}\geq 0
such that for any
\nu\in\Xi_{p}
of order
p^{t}\gg 1
and the local character
\nu_{{\mathfrak{p}}}
of conductor
p^{t_{{\mathfrak{p}}}+1}
, we have

v_{p}\left(\frac{L(1,\lambda\nu)}{\Omega_{K}}\right)=\mu_{p}(\lambda)+\frac{c_{\lambda}}{p^{t-1}(p-1)}-\frac{t_{{\mathfrak{p}}}+1}{2}.

(b)

Suppose that
p
is inert in
K
.
If
\epsilon(\lambda)=-1
, suppose also that
p\geq 5
.
Then there exists an integer
c_{\lambda}\geq 0
such that for any
\nu\in\Xi_{\lambda,p}^{+}
of order
p^{t}\gg 1
and the local character
\nu_{p}
of conductor
p^{t_{p}+1}
, we have

v_{p}\left(\frac{L(1,\lambda\nu)}{\Omega_{K}}\right)=\mu_{p}(\lambda)+\frac{c_{\lambda}}{p^{t-1}(p-1)}-\frac{t_{p}+1}{2}+\frac{1}{p^{t-1}(p-1)}\left(\frac{1-\epsilon(\lambda)}{2}+\sum_{k\equiv t-1\ \mathrm{mod}\ {2}}(p^{k}-p^{k-1})\right),

where
1\leq k\leq t-1
.

Theorem
1.3
is a consequence of the existence of certain
p
-adic
L
-functions, and determination of their
\mu
-invariants. Our principal result is that the latter equals

\mu_{p}(\lambda)
.

The above asymptotic formulas for
p
-adic valuation of Hecke
L
-values have an application to the variation of Tate–Shafarevich groups analogous to Corollary
1.2
(see Corollary
1.9
).
The variation reflects the underlying Iwasawa theory. While the split case illustrates a typical phenomenon at an ordinary prime, the inert case echoes a peculiar non-ordinary phenomenon.
While the shape of the asymptotic formula in the former case goes back to Katz
[
55
]
, the latter case recently appeared in
[
16
]
. (In these works an abstract invariant
\mu\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 0}
instead of the above
\mu_{p}(\lambda)
appears.)

Remark 1.4
.

For Theorem
1.3
(a), the hypothesis
\epsilon(\lambda)=+1
is essential as otherwise

L(1,\lambda\nu)=0
for any
\nu\in\Xi_{p}
.

1.1.4.
Application to CM Iwasawa theory at inert primes

We describe an application to Rubin’s supersingular CM Iwasawa theory
[
76
]
.

Let
p\nmid 6h_{K}
be a prime inert in the imaginary quadratic field
K
.
Let
\Phi
denote the completion of
K
at the prime ideal generated by
p
.
Let
H
denote the Hilbert class field of
K
.
For
\lambda
as above, suppose that
the Hecke character
\lambda\circ{\rm{N}}_{H/K}

is associated to a
{\mathbb{Q}}
-curve
E
over
H
with good reduction at primes of
H
above
p
(cf.
[
33
]
).
Without loss of generality, we assume that
E
has CM by
O_{K}
.
Let
T
denote the
p
-adic Tate module of
E
, which is naturally endowed with an
O_{\Phi}
-action.

Let
\Psi_{\infty}
be
the anticyclotomic
{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
-extension of
\Phi

and
\Psi_{n}
the
n
-th layer.
Denote the Iwasawa cohomology
\varprojlim_{n}H^{1}(\Psi_{n},T^{\otimes-1}(1))

by
\mathcal{H}^{1}
, where
T^{\otimes-1}
is the
O_{\Phi}
-dual of
T
.
Since
p\nmid h_{K}
, we may identify
\Xi_{p}
with the set of finite order characters of
{\mathrm{Gal}}(\Psi_{\infty}/\Phi)
.
For
\nu\in\Xi_{p}
factoring through
{\mathrm{Gal}}(\Psi_{m}/\Phi)
, the dual exponential map for
\nu\otimes T^{\otimes-1}(1)

defines
a map

\delta_{\nu}:\mathcal{H}^{1}\longrightarrow\Psi_{m}(\mathrm{Im}\,\nu),

dependent on a choice of Néron differential.
Following Rubin
[
76
]
, define

\mathcal{H}^{1}_{\pm}:={\{}h\in\mathcal{H}^{1}\text{ $|$}\text{ }\delta_{\nu}(h)=0\quad\text{for any }\nu\in\Xi_{p}\text{ of order $p^{t}$ with $t$ odd/even}{\}}.

Rubin
[
76
]
showed that
\mathcal{H}^{1}_{\pm}
is a free
\Lambda
-module of rank one for
\Lambda:=O_{\Psi}[\![{\mathrm{Gal}}(\Psi_{\infty}/\Phi)]\!]
.

Fix a generator
h_{\pm}
of the
\Lambda
-module

\mathcal{H}^{1}_{\pm}
.
Let
\varepsilon\in\{+,-\}
denote the sign of
\epsilon(\lambda)

and

\mathscr{L}_{p}(\lambda):=\mathscr{L}_{p}(\lambda,\Omega_{K},h_{\varepsilon})\in\Lambda

(1.4)

the associated Rubin
p
-adic
L
-function
[
76
, §10]
.
Let
\nu(\mathscr{L}_{p}(\lambda))
denote its evaluation at an anticyclotomic character
\nu
.
An interpolation property of the Rubin
p
-adic
L
-function is given by

\nu(\mathscr{L}_{p}(\lambda))=\frac{1}{\delta_{\nu^{-1}}(h_{\varepsilon})}\cdot\frac{L(1,\overline{\lambda\nu})}{\Omega_{K}}

(
\nu\in\Xi_{\lambda,p}^{+}\setminus\{1\}
),

(1.5)

where
the non-vanishing of
\delta_{\nu^{-1}}(h_{\varepsilon})

is a consequence of Rubin’s conjecture
[
14
]
.

The Iwasawa
\mu
-invariant of Rubin’s
p
-adic
L
-function is given by the following.

Theorem 1.5
.

Let
\lambda
be a Hecke character over an imaginary quadratic field
K
of infinity type
(1,0)

such that

\lambda\circ{\rm{N}}_{H/K}

is associated to a
{\mathbb{Q}}
-curve
E
over
H
with good reduction at a prime
p\nmid 6h_{K}
inert in
K
.
Let
\mathscr{L}_{p}(\lambda)
be an associated Rubin
p
-adic
L
-function.
Then

\mu(\mathscr{L}_{p}(\lambda))=0.

The above mod
p
non-vanishing
is based on Theorem
1.3
and the main result of
[
16
]
.

Remark 1.6
.

In the setting of Theorem
1.5
the invariant
\mu_{p}(\lambda)
vanishes.

Corollary 1.7
.

For
\lambda
as in Theorem
1.5
, let
\mathscr{X}_{\varepsilon}(\lambda)
denote the associated signed anticyclotomic Selmer group
[
14
]
and

\mathscr{X}_{\rm st}(\lambda)
the two-variable strict Selmer group.
Then

\mu(\mathscr{X}_{\varepsilon}(\lambda))=\mu(\mathscr{X}_{\rm st}(\lambda))=0.

Proof.

The assertion for
\mathscr{X}_{\varepsilon}(\lambda)
just follows from Theorem
1.5
and signed Iwasawa main conjecture
[
14
, Thm. 6.1]
.
In particular, the
\mu
-invariant
\mu(\mathscr{X}_{\rm st}^{\rm ac}(\lambda))
of the anticyclotomic strict Selmer group
vanishes. Therefore, control theorem implies the same for
\mathscr{X}_{\rm st}(\lambda)
.
∎

Remark 1.8
.

The above vanishing of
\mu
-invariants has an application to classical Iwasawa theory. Indeed,
the Selmer group
\mathscr{X}_{\rm st}(\lambda)
is a classical Iwasawa module over imaginary quadratic fields: the projective limit of
p
-part of class groups along the
{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}^{2}
-extension of
K
with tame action arising from
\lambda
. It is extensively studied since the 1970’s by Coates–Wiles
[
25
]
and Rubin
[
77
]
, among others, yet determination of its
\mu
-invariant at inert primes remained elusive.

In combination with
[
17
, Thm. 1.1]
we obtain the following.

Corollary 1.9
.

Let
E
be a CM elliptic curve over
\mathbb{Q}
, and

K
the associated CM field.
Let
p\geq 5
be a prime of good supersingular reduction for
E
, and
K_{\infty}
the anticyclotomic
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-extension of
K
.
Then
there exists an integer
\lambda_{E,p}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
such that for any sufficiently large
n\geq 0
with
(-1)^{n+1}=\epsilon(E)
,
the cokernel of the restriction map

\hbox{\cyr X}(E/K_{n-1})[p^{\infty}]\to\hbox{\cyr X}(E/K_{n})[p^{\infty}]

is finite, and

{\rm length}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\left({\mathrm{Coker}}\left(\hbox{\cyr X}(E/K_{n-1})[p^{\infty}]\to\hbox{\cyr X}(E/K_{n})[p^{\infty}]\right)\right)=\lambda_{E,p}.

1.1.5.
Relation to prior work

The characteristic zero non-vanishing of Hecke
L
-values as in Theorem
1.1
goes back to Greenberg
[
32
]
and Rohrlich
[
75
]
.

As for
(\ell,p)
non-vanishing in the case
\ell\neq p
,
Theorem
1.1
complements the existing results if
p
remains inert in
K
.
It is due to Finis
[
27
]
if
p
splits in
K
. In the
\ell=p
case,
Theorem
1.3
is a new result for inert primes
p
.
The split case is again due to Finis
[
28
]
, of which our method gives a different proof.

If
\ell
and
p
are both split in
K
, then
the problem has been studied by Hsieh
[
44
,
45
]
, Ohta
[
64
]
and the second-named author
[
38
]
, based on Hida’s idea
[
39
,
40
,
41
]
. However, in the
\ell\neq p
case, the non-vanishing is established
3
3

3

The results were originally announced for all but finitely many
\nu\in\Xi_{\lambda,p}^{+}
. However, a few years back, an issue was found in Hida’s strategy
[
39
,
40
]
. His present fix
[
42
]
only allows infinitely many
\nu\in\Xi_{\lambda,p}^{+}
.
only for
infinitely
many
\nu\in\Xi_{\lambda,p}^{+}
(cf.
[
42
]
).

1.2.
Strategy

The mod
\ell
non-vanishing (
Q
) concerns the arithmetic of
U(1)
over
K
.
Via theta correspondence, we recast it as a problem on a definite unitary group
U(2)
. Since the associated Shimura set is finite, the framework turns out to be amenable to various tools, as outlined below.

Some of the following notation differs from the rest of the paper.

1.2.1.
Definite Shimura set

Given an imaginary quadratic field
K
, we have a naturally associated quaternion algebra
B
over
{\mathbb{Q}}
such that

\epsilon(B_{q})=\eta_{K_{q}}(-1)

for any prime
q
. Note that
K
embeds into
B
as a
{\mathbb{Q}}
-algebra, and we often fix such an embedding.

In our study,
B
is foundational to the arithmetic of
K

in the guise of definite Shimura set.
Though the association is natural, the arithmetic seems largely unexplored. As far as we know, the only other examples are Tian’s work
[
83
,
22
,
84
]
on the congruent number and cube sum problems, jointly with Cai–Shu and Yuan–Zhang respectively (see also
[
48
]
).

1.2.2.
Ancillary results

The mod
\ell
non-vanishing is based on Theorems
1.10
and
1.11
below, which concern the arithmetic of the definite Shimura set.

For a self-dual Hecke character
\lambda
of infinity type
(1,0)
, let

\phi_{\lambda}
be the associated
{\mathrm{GL}}_{2}
-theta series of weight two.

Let
\pi_{\lambda}
be the cuspidal automorphic representation of
B^{\times}_{{\mathbb{A}}}
arising from Jacquet–Langlands transfer of

\phi_{\lambda}
to
B^{\times}
.
Let
\ell\nmid 2N_{K/{\mathbb{Q}}}(\mathrm{cond^{r}}{\lambda})
be a prime.
Let

\varphi_{\lambda}\in\pi_{\lambda}

be
a CM form as in Definition
3.4
.
It is a test vector in the sense of Gross and Prasad
[
35
]
, which is
\ell
-primitive and
K_{q}^{\times}
-invariant for all primes
q|{\rm{N}}_{K/{\mathbb{Q}}}(\mathrm{cond^{r}}{\lambda})
non-split in
K
.
We emphasise that
\varphi_{\lambda}
is not a newform
4
4

4

In this setting the finite part of the discriminant of
B
divides
D_{K}
. In turn, at primes dividing
D_{K}
, newform is not a test vector under an optimal embedding
K\hookrightarrow B
.
.

The period

\Omega_{\lambda}=\frac{8\pi^{2}(\phi_{\lambda},\phi_{\lambda})}{\langle\varphi_{\lambda},\varphi_{\lambda}\rangle}

naturally arises while studying Rankin–Selberg
L
-values
L(1/2,\pi_{\lambda,K}\otimes\nu)
for
\nu\in\Xi_{p}
,
Here
(\ ,\ )
and
\langle\ ,\ \rangle
are Hermitian pairings on the space of
{\mathrm{GL}}_{2}
and
B^{\times}
-modular forms, and
L(s,\pi_{\lambda,K}\otimes\nu)
denotes the Rankin–Selberg
L
-function associated to the self-dual pair
(\pi_{\lambda},\nu)
.
We normalise the latter so that
s=1/2
is the center of the functional equation.
In view of explicit Waldspurger formula due to Cai–Shu–Tian
[
21
]
the normalised
L
-value
L(1/2,\pi_{\lambda,K}\otimes\nu)/\Omega_{\lambda}
is
\ell
-integral.

We first prove the following
(\ell,p)
non-vanishing.

Theorem 1.10
.

Let
\lambda
be a self-dual Hecke character over
K
of infinity type
(1,0)
and
\pi_{\lambda}
the associated cuspidal automorphic representation.
Let
\ell
and
p
be two different primes which are coprime to
2{\rm{N}}_{K/{\mathbb{Q}}}(\mathrm{cond^{r}}{\lambda})
.
Then for all but finitely many
\nu\in\Xi_{\lambda,p}^{+}
, we have

v_{\ell}\left(\frac{L(1/2,\pi_{\lambda,K}\otimes\nu)}{\Omega_{\lambda}}\right)=0.

As for the period
\Omega_{\lambda}
, note that

\Omega_{\lambda}/\Omega_{K}^{2}\in\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}^{\times}

since
the normalised
L
-values
L(1,\lambda/\lambda^{c})/\Omega_{\lambda}
and
L(1,\lambda/\lambda^{c})/\Omega_{K}^{2}
are algebraic and non-zero, where
\lambda^{c}:=\lambda\circ c
for
c\in{\mathrm{Gal}}(K/{\mathbb{Q}})
the non-trivial element.

Theorem 1.11
.

Let
\lambda
be a self-dual Hecke character over an imaginary quadratic field
K
of infinity type
(1,0)
. Let
\ell\nmid 2{\rm{N}}_{K/{\mathbb{Q}}}(\mathrm{cond^{r}}{\lambda})
be a prime. If
\epsilon(\lambda)=-1
, suppose that
\ell\geq 5
.
Then

v_{\ell}\left(\frac{\Omega_{\lambda}}{\Omega_{K}^{2}}\right)=2\mu_{\ell}(\lambda).

The above results yield Theorem
1.1

in light of the factorisation

L(1/2,\pi_{\lambda,K}\otimes\nu)=L(1,\lambda\nu)L(1,\lambda\nu^{-1})

(1.6)

of
L
-values, and the lower bound (
1.3
).

1.2.3.
About Theorem
1.10

The non-vanishing is based on explicit Waldspurger formula and an equidistribution of special points.

Let
X_{U}
be the definite Shimura set associated to
B^{\times}
and an open subgroup
U\subset B^{\times}
corresponding to the test vector
\varphi_{\lambda}
. An apt choice of embedding
\iota:K\rightarrow B
leads to special points

x_{n}(a)\in X_{U}

for
[a]\in{{\rm G}_{n}}:={\mathrm{Gal}}(H_{p^{n}}/K)
and
H_{m}
the ring class field of conductor
m\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 1}
. By the explicit Waldspurger formula of Cai–Shu–Tian
[
21
]
, the mod
\ell
non-vanishing of
L
-values in Theorem
1.10
is equivalent to
\ell
-indivisibility of toric periods

P_{\varphi_{\lambda}}(\nu):=\sum_{[a]\in{{\rm G}_{n}}}\nu(a)\varphi_{\lambda}(x_{n}(a)),

where
\nu
factors through
{\rm G}_{n}
. The generality of
[
21
]
is essential in our study since the classical Heegner hypothesis is not satisfied, more precisely
D_{K}
divides the conductor of
\pi_{\lambda}
and
B
is ramified at primes dividing
D_{K}
.

An idea of Vatsal
[
86
]
posits to study the
\ell
-indivisibility of toric periods
P_{\varphi_{\lambda}}(\nu)
via equidistribution of images of special points
x_{n}(a)
in a self-product of the Shimura set
X_{U}
as
n
varies. The equidistribution is a consequence of Ratner’s seminal ergodicity of unipotent flows (cf.
[
74
]
). In turn it suffices to show that
\varphi_{\lambda}\ \mathrm{mod}\ {\ell}
is non-Eisenstein on certain components of
X_{U}
.
The prior non-Eisenstein argument
[
86
]
does not apply.

In fact, a new phenomenon happens: there is a partition

X_{U}=X_{U}^{+}\sqcup X_{U}^{-}

where

X_{U}^{+}:=\{[h]\in X_{U}\ \Big|\ {\rm{N}}(h)\in{\mathbb{Q}}^{\times}_{+}\backslash{\mathbb{Q}}_{+}^{\times}{\rm{N}}(\widehat{K}^{\times})/{\rm{N}}(U)\},

and for
p
inert,
\varphi_{\lambda}\ \mathrm{mod}\ {\ell}
is non-Eisenstein on
exactly one
of the subsets
X_{U}^{\pm}
depending on
\epsilon(\lambda)
.
Our non-Eisenstein argument is indirect:
\varphi_{\lambda}\ \mathrm{mod}\ {\ell}
is non-zero by definition, and consequently non-Eisenstein on
X_{U}
. (See Lemma
5.6
which is specific to the CM setting.) So it is non-Eisenstein on at least one of the subsets
X_{U}^{\tilde{\varepsilon}}\in\{X_{U}^{+},X_{U}^{-}\}
. As the above non-vanishing strategy applies on
X_{U}^{\tilde{\varepsilon}}
, it follows that

\tilde{\varepsilon}
has the desired parity since
L(1/2,\pi_{\lambda,K}\otimes\nu)=0
for
\nu\in\Xi_{\lambda,p}^{-}
.

1.2.4.
About Theorem
1.11

The
\ell
-integral period relation in Theorem

- a comparison of automorphic and motivic periods - is a basic problem (cf.

For weight two newforms with square-free conductor the comparison is a consequence of Ribet’s level raising (cf.

However, our CM setting
5
5

5

It is also excluded by conjectures of Prasanna
[

and Ichino–Prasanna

which concern the arithmetic of ratios of Petersson norms under Jacquet–Langlands correspondence.
is neither semistable nor does it involve an eigenform on the definite Shimura set.

Our roundabout strategy is based on a tenuous link of the
\ell
-adic valuation of
\Omega_{\lambda}/\Omega_{K}^{2}
with mod

non-vanishing as in Theorem

.
A key observation: if there exists an auxiliary

and

(1.7)

then Theorem

implies
6
6

6

This relies on a key property of

: it
is a universal test vector for primes

i.e. a test vector for self-dual pairs

(\pi_{\lambda},\nu)
for any such

and

.

Theorem
1.11
!

If
\epsilon(\lambda)=+1
, then we check the above criterion using Finis’ mod

[

]
: for any prime

split in

provides the existence of
\nu
as in (

).

Now suppose that

. Proceeding as above, one may seek to choose a prime
p\nmid 2\ell{\rm N}_{K/{\mathbb{Q}}}(\mathrm{cond^{r}}{\lambda})

inert

.
(If

L(1,\lambda\nu)=0

However, Finis’ work

The decisive idea is to bootstrap the problem by choosing an auxiliary

such that
\epsilon(\lambda\nu_{0})=+1
and apply the prior non-vanishing strategy to

We begin by showing a variant of Theorem

, which allows

p|N_{K/{\mathbb{Q}}}(\mathrm{cond^{r}}{\lambda\nu_{0}})
), and obtain

v_{\ell}\left(\frac{L(1/2,\pi_{\lambda\nu_{0},K}\otimes\nu)}{\Omega_{\lambda\nu_{0}}^{\{p\}}}\right)=0

for inert

.
This non-vanishing involves a different period

, arising from an

-primitive

\varphi_{\lambda\nu_{0}}^{\{p\}}
which is new at

and the same as
\varphi_{\lambda\nu_{0}}
at other primes. We emphasise that

itself does not work in the strategy as
K_{p}^{\times}
-invariant vectors in
\pi_{\lambda\nu_{0}}
are not test vectors for self-dual pairs
(\pi_{\lambda\nu_{0}},\nu)
.

Now, to utilise (

), it suffices to determine
v_{\ell}({\Omega_{\lambda\nu_{0}}^{\{p\}}}/{\Omega_{K}^{2}})
. Since
\ell\geq 5
and

is auxiliary in regards to Theorem

, it maybe assumed that
\ell\nmid p(p^{2}-1)
. Then our principle result:

v_{\ell}\left(\frac{\Omega_{\lambda\nu_{0}}^{\{p\}}}{\Omega_{K}^{2}}\right)=v_{\ell}\left(\frac{\Omega_{\lambda\nu_{0}}}{\Omega_{K}^{2}}\right)=2\mu_{\ell}(\lambda).

(1.9)

In light of (

) and (

) it follows that

v_{\ell}\left(\frac{L(1,\lambda\nu)}{\Omega_{K}}\right)=\mu_{\ell}(\lambda)

for all but finitely many

. Therefore (

.

We now outline
the period relation (
1.9
).
Its’ second equality just follows from the aforementioned root number

\epsilon(\lambda\nu_{0})=+1
.
As for the first,
the strategy is based on yet another variant of Theorem
1.10
for a different prime
q
!

Let

be a prime inert in

.
We show that

v_{\ell}\left(\frac{L(1/2,\pi_{\lambda\nu_{0},K}\otimes\chi)}{\Omega_{\lambda\nu_{0}}^{\{p\}}}\right)=0

(1.10)

for all but finitely many

Since

by Theorem

,
the desired period relation (

Besides equidistribution of special points, the non-vanishing (

) rests on a new result on explicit construction of
\ell
-optimal test vectors for supercuspidal representations, which is the content of the next subsection.

1.2.5.
Newforms as
\ell
-optimal test vectors for supercuspidal representations

Given an irreducible admissible representation
\pi
of
{\mathrm{PGL}}_{2}({\mathbb{Q}}_{q})
and a
separable quadratic extension
K/{\mathbb{Q}}_{q}
, a natural question: whether newform is a test vector for

{\mathrm{Hom}}_{K^{\times}}(\pi,{\mathbb{C}})
.
This local problem is linked with global arithmetic in view of Waldspurger and Gross–Zagier formulas
(cf.
[
83
,
49
]
).

Now suppose that
q
is odd and
K/{\mathbb{Q}}_{q}
the unramified quadratic extension.
Let
\lambda
be a character of
K^{\times}
of exponential conductor
m\geq 2
such that
\lambda|_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{q}^{\times}}=\eta_{K},
where
\eta_{K}
is the quadratic character
7
7

7

In this local setting we still use the prior global notation such as
K
and
\lambda
.
of
{\mathbb{Q}}_{q}^{\times}
corresponding to
K
.
Let
\pi=\pi_{\lambda}
be the associated supercuspidal representation of
{\mathrm{PGL}}_{2}({\mathbb{Q}}_{q})
and

f\in\pi^{R^{\times}}

a newform for
R
the standard Eichler order of discriminant
q^{2m}
. Note that
(\pi,1)
is a self-dual pair.

We consider
K^{\times}
-toric period of
f
under a family of optimal embeddings
\iota:O_{K,q^{m}}\hookrightarrow R
, parameterized by a trace zero unit
\theta\in K
and
u\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{q}^{\times}
such that
u^{2}\theta^{2}-1\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{q}^{\times 2}
(see §
7.1.1
). We emphasise that the embedding of the unramified
torus in
{\mathrm{PGL}}_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{q})
depends only on the conductor of the representation.
Let
(\ ,\ )
be a
{\mathrm{PGL}}_{2}({\mathbb{Q}}_{q})
-invariant non-degenerate Hermitian pairing on
\pi
.
Define the toric period

\gamma_{\theta,u}:=\frac{1}{{\mathrm{vol}}(K^{\times}/{\mathbb{Q}}_{q}^{\times})(f,f)}\int_{K^{\times}/{\mathbb{Q}}_{q}^{\times}}(\pi(\iota(t))f,f)d^{\times}t.

Theorem 1.12
.

Let the setting be as above.

(a)

For a given
\theta
, there exists
u\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{q}^{\times}
as above such that the newform
f
is a test vector for the self-dual pair
(\pi,1)
, that is,

\gamma_{\theta,u}\neq 0.

(b)

Let
\ell\neq q
be a prime. Then for a given
\theta
, there exists
u\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{q}^{\times}
as above such that

v_{\ell}((q^{2}-1)\gamma_{\theta,u})=0.

In fact, we obtain an explicit formula for
\gamma_{\theta,u}
in terms of
\lambda
(see Theorem
7.1
).

The Kirillov model is central to the proof. We first interpret the toric period as a linear combination of epsilon factors of twists of
\pi_{\lambda}
. This relies on harmonic analysis in the framework of Kirillov model and action of Atkin–Lehner operators on twists of newforms. Then, being in the supercuspidal case, the epsilon factor of a
{\mathrm{GL}}_{2}({\mathbb{Q}}_{q})
-representation equals that of the associated character of
K^{\times}
. In turn, we relate the linear of combination of epsilon factors with values of
\lambda
via an explicit epsilon factor formula and analysis of Jacobi sums. This builds on the work of Murase and Sugano
[
63
]
on local primitive theta functions.

The test vector problem has been studied in the literature in special cases. For instance, the
m=1
case of Theorem
1.12
(a) is due to Vastal
(cf.
[
87
, Thm. 7.2]
). His notably different approach is based on representation theory of
{\mathrm{GL}}_{2}({\mathbb{F}}_{q})

and ideas from Deligne–Lusztig theory (cf.
[
69
]
). For
m\geq 2
, the test vector problem eluded prior methods. The reader may refer to
[
87
, Ch. 7]
for an overview (see also
[
88
]
).

1.2.6.
About Theorems
1.3
and
1.5

Suppose that
p\nmid 6{\rm N}_{K/{\mathbb{Q}}}(\mathrm{cond^{r}}{\lambda})
is inert in
K
. Then
p
is a prime of non-ordinary reduction for
\pi_{\lambda}
.

Based on the principle of non-ordinary Iwasawa theory
[
70
,
58
]
, we introduce a plus/minus
p
-adic
L
-function

\mathscr{L}_{p}(\pi_{\lambda}):=\mathscr{L}_{p}^{-\epsilon}(\pi_{\lambda})\in\Lambda_{O}

for a finite extension
O
of
O_{\Psi}
with
\Lambda_{O}:=O[\![\Gamma]\!]\simeq O[\![T]\!]
and
\epsilon
the sign of
\epsilon(\lambda)
, such that

\nu(\mathscr{L}_{p}(\pi_{\lambda}))\doteq p^{t+1}\nu(\Phi_{p}^{\epsilon})\cdot\frac{L(1/2,\pi_{\lambda,K}\otimes\nu)}{\Omega_{\lambda}}.

Here
\nu\in\Xi_{\lambda,p}^{+}
is of order
p^{t}
,
\Phi_{p}^{\epsilon}
denotes a half cyclotomic polynomial and
\doteq
an equality up to
p
-units independent of
\nu
.
The construction of
\mathscr{L}_{p}(\pi_{\lambda})
relies on an explicit Waldspurger formula on the definite Shimura set
X_{U}
and the recipe in
[
70
,
56
]
. In the case
\epsilon(\lambda)=-1
it slightly differs from
loc. cit.

(cf. Definition
4.12
).

Recall that the work of Pollack
[
70
]
, the third-named author
[
56
]
and Lei
[
60
]
concerns Iwasawa theory of
{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
-cyclotomic deformation of an elliptic newform
f
at supersingular primes
p
for which
a_{p}(f)=0
. Its relevance to anticyclotomic deformations over an imaginary quadratic field
K
was first noticed by Darmon and Iovita
[
26
]
. While they assume
p
to be split in
K
, the inert case appears in recent study of the first-named author with Buyukboduk and Lei
[
5
,
6
]
(see also
[
11
]
).
The prior anticyclotomic studies exclude the case that
f
has CM by
K
, basically due to complications with explicit Waldspurger formula, which we analyse via the work of Cai–Shu–Tian
[
21
]
.

The
(\ell,p)
non-vanishing strategy outlined in §
1.2.3
also applies to the
p
-adic
L
-function
\mathscr{L}_{p}(\pi_{\lambda})
, leading to

\mu(\mathscr{L}_{p}(\pi_{\lambda}))=0.

(1.11)

Then Theorem
1.3
(b) just follows from the comparison of periods as in Theorem
1.11
.

As for Rubin’s
p
-adic
L
-function,
in light of
p
-adic Artin formalism and (
1.6
) one may expect a factorisation

\mathscr{L}_{p}(\pi_{\lambda})\frac{\Omega_{\lambda}}{\Omega_{K}^{2}}=\mathscr{L}_{p}(\lambda)\mathscr{L}_{p}^{\iota}(\lambda),

(1.12)

of
p
-adic
L
-functions
up to an element in
\Lambda_{O}^{\times}
,
where
\iota
denotes the involution of
\Lambda_{O}
arising from inversion on
\Gamma
. These
p
-adic
L
-functions live in distant worlds:
\mathscr{L}_{p}(\pi_{\lambda})
being automorphic and

\mathscr{L}_{p}(\lambda)
an incarnation of a zeta element (elliptic unit). Moreover, local invariants in their interpolation formulas also differ, primarily due to which the factorisation remains open.

We still prove an identity of
\mu
-invariants

\mu(\mathscr{L}_{p}(\pi_{\lambda}))+v_{p}\left(\frac{\Omega_{\lambda}}{\Omega_{K}^{2}}\right)=2\mu(\mathscr{L}_{p}(\lambda))

(1.13)

mirroring (
1.12
).
It is based on the main result of
[
16
]
,
which determines the
p
-adic valuation of generalised Gauss sum
\delta_{\chi}(v_{\pm})
appearing in the interpolation formula (
1.5
) of Rubin’s
p
-adic
L
-function. The latter employs ramification theory and builds on the proof of Rubin’s conjecture.
Finally, in light of (
1.11
), (
1.13
), and Remark
1.6
, Theorem
1.11
concludes the proof of Theorem
1.5
.

While the latter involves only a given prime
p
, a salient feature of our method is that it relies on
(\ell,p)
non-vanishing for auxiliary primes
\ell\neq p
.

1.2.7.
Further remarks on the work of Hida and Finis

This paper is independent from Hida’s approach
[
39
,
40
,
41
,
42
,
44
,
38
]
.
The latter begins with translation of the non-vanishing problem (
Q
) in terms of
\ell
-indivisibility of toric periods of a
{\mathrm{GL}}_{2}
-Eisenstein series, and studies it via Chai’s theory of Hecke-stable subvarieties of a mod
\ell
Shimura variety (cf.
[
20
]
).
A crucial hypothesis is that
\ell
splits in
K
so that elliptic curve with CM by
O_{K}
have ordinary reduction at
\ell
. The approach is flexible and applies to non self-dual Hecke characters
\lambda
.

Finis’ method
[
27
,
28
]
connects the non-vanishing (
Q
) to
\ell
-indivisibility of a linear functional on the space of
U(1)
-theta functions with complex multiplication, and studies it via a Manin–Mumford conjecture in the context of Mumford’s theory of theta functions. A key hypothesis is that
p
splits in
K
. In contrast, our definite
U(2)
-setting first relates the non-anishing to
\ell
-indivisibility of toric periods of a CM form on a Shimura set, and an
\ell
-integral comparison of periods.
While the former is independent from Finis’ work, the latter relies on his
(\ell,p)
non-vanishing
[
27
]
for a particular prime
p=p_{0}
split in
K
. As a byproduct of our method, the non-vanishing holds for all other primes
p\nmid 2p_{0}N_{K/{\mathbb{Q}}}(\mathrm{cond^{r}}{\lambda})
, including the missing case of inert primes
p
!

1.3.
Organisation

We begin with the framework of definite Shimura sets in section
2
.
Then sections
3
and
4
present explicit Waldspurger formulas and construction of Rankin–Selberg
p
-adic
L
-functions, respectively. These sections treat general self-dual pairs, following which the CM hypothesis ensues.
Section
5
constitutes the technical core of the paper, showing mod
\ell
non-vanishing of Rankin–Selberg
L
-values in the CM case. Then section
6
establishes the desired mod
\ell
non-vanishing of Hecke
L
-values. It rests on an explicit construction of
\ell
-optimal test vectors for supercuspidal representations, which is the content of section
7
. This last section is purely local and may be of independent interest.

1.4.
Vistas

Our
(\ell,p)
-non-vanishing method generalises to self-dual Hecke characters over
\ell
-ordinary CM fields. Such a non-vanishing is essential for completion of Hsieh’s proof of Eisenstein congruence divisibility
[
46
]
towards the CM Iwasawa main conjecture
8
8

8

Due to Hida’s weakening
[
42
]
of the
(\ell,p)
non-vanishing results on Hecke
L
-values
[
39
,
40
]
, Hsieh’s work
[
46
]
on the CM main conjecture is incomplete. In
[
46
]
the non-vanishing
[
44
]
was crucially used for primitivity of an Eisenstein series on
U(2,1)
over the CM field: the non-vanishing holds for
all but finitely many
twists was decisive, which remained an open problem due to Hida’s weakening until the very recent sequel
[
10
]
by a subset of authors.
and will be presented in the very recent sequel
[
10
]
. Our result on
\ell
-optimal test vectors for supercuspidal representations suggests new phenomena regarding correlation coefficients of representations of reductive groups over finite fields
[
34
]
and has led to new investigations (cf.
[
1
]
).

For the
(\ell,p)
-non-vanishing problem over imaginary quadratic fields, we hope to consider the mysterious case of primes
\ell
ramified in the imaginary quadratic field in the near future.
Another natural problem is to generalise main results of this paper to self-dual Hecke characters of general infinity type. The sought after factorisation (
1.12
) of
p
-adic
L
-functions will also be investigated.
Our approach to
\ell
-optimal test vectors for supercuspidal representations appears to generalise to other settings.

The automorphic view on Rubin’s supersingular theory presented in this paper may manifest to symplectic self-dual Galois deformations (cf.
[
13
]
).

1.5.
Notation

1.5.1.
Global fields

Let
F
be a number field. Let
O_{F}
denote its integer ring,
{\mathbb{A}}_{F}
the ad
\grave{\text{e}}
les and
{\mathbb{A}}_{F,f}\subset{\mathbb{A}}_{F}

the finite part. In the text we fix an imaginary quadratic field
K
. Let
\eta_{K}
denote the associated quadratic character over
{\mathbb{Q}}
.

Put
{\mathbb{A}}={\mathbb{A}}_{\mathbb{Q}}
.

Fix an embedding

\iota_{\infty}:\overline{\mathbb{Q}}\hookrightarrow{\mathbb{C}}
and an isomorphism

{\mathbb{C}}\simeq{\mathbb{C}}_{q}

for a prime
q
.
Let

\iota_{q}:\overline{\mathbb{Q}}\hookrightarrow{\mathbb{C}}_{q}
be their composition. Let
v_{q}:{\mathbb{C}}_{q}\to\mathbb{Q}\cup\{\infty\}
be the
q
-adic valuation
so that
v_{q}(q)=1
. We regard
L
as a subfield of
{\mathbb{C}}
(resp.
{\mathbb{C}}_{q}
) via

\iota_{\infty}
(resp.
\iota_{q}
) and
{\mathrm{Hom}}(L,\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}})={\mathrm{Hom}}(L,{\mathbb{C}}_{q})
.

Denote by
\widehat{{\mathbb{Z}}}
the finite completion of
{\mathbb{Z}}
. For an abelian group
G
, put

\widehat{G}=G\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\widehat{{\mathbb{Z}}}
.

Write

M=M^{+}M^{-}

for
M^{+}
and
M^{-}
divisible only by the split and non-split primes in
K
respectively, and further

M^{-}=M_{\rm sf}^{-}M_{\rm{a}}^{-}

for
M_{\rm sf}^{-}
the square-free part of
M^{-}
so that
(M_{\rm sf}^{-},M_{\rm a}^{-})=1
.

For an algebraic group
G
over
{\mathbb{Q}}
and
q
a prime, denote by
G_{q}
the group of its

{\mathbb{Q}}_{q}
-points.

1.5.2.
L
-functions

Local

Let
F={\mathbb{Q}}_{q}
or
{\mathbb{R}}
.

Let
\sigma
be an irreducible admissible representation of
{\mathrm{GL}}_{2}(F)
. Let
L(s,\sigma)
and
\epsilon(s,\sigma,\psi_{F})
be the associated
L
-function and epsilon factor respectively (cf.
[
52
, Thm. 2.18 (iv)]
), where
\psi_{F}
is a non-trivial additive character of
F
.

Let
K
be a quadratic extension of
F
, and
\sigma_{K}
the base change of
\sigma
.
Let
\chi:K^{\times}\to{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}
be a character.
Let
L(s,\sigma_{K}\otimes\chi)
be the Rankin–Selberg
L
-function as in
[
53
, §20]
.

For an irreducible admissible representation of
{\mathrm{GL}}_{2}(F)
with trivial central character, we will simply denote
\epsilon(1/2,\sigma,\psi_{F})
by
\epsilon(\sigma)
, since it does not depend on the choice of

\psi_{F}
.
We adopt similar convention for representations of

{\mathrm{GL}}_{2}(K)
with trivial central character.

Global

We consider Rankin–Selberg
L
-functions over
{\mathbb{Q}}
.

Let
\sigma
be an irreducible cuspidal automorphic representation of
{\mathrm{GL}}_{2}({\mathbb{A}})
.
Let
K
be a separable quadratic extension of
{\mathbb{Q}}
and

\chi:{\mathbb{A}}_{K}^{\times}\rightarrow{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}
an algebraic Hecke character.
For
\sigma_{K}
the base change of
\sigma
to
K
,
we have the associated Rankin–Selberg
L
-function defined by

L(s,\sigma_{K}\otimes\chi)=\prod_{q\leq\infty}L(s,\sigma_{K_{q}}\otimes\chi_{q}).

The automorphic
L
-function
L(s,\sigma_{K}\otimes\chi)

satisfies the functional equation

L(s,\sigma_{K}\otimes\chi)=\epsilon(s,\sigma_{K}\otimes\chi)L(1-s,\widetilde{\sigma}_{K}\otimes\chi^{-1}),

where
\epsilon(s,\sigma_{K}\otimes\chi)=\prod_{q}\epsilon(s,\sigma_{K_{q}}\otimes\chi_{q},\psi_{K_{q}}).
If
(\sigma,\chi)
is self-dual in the sense that
\omega_{\sigma}\chi|_{{\mathbb{A}}^{\times}}=1
, where
\omega_{\sigma}
is the central character, then the functional equation becomes

L(s,\sigma_{K}\otimes\chi)=\epsilon(s,\sigma_{K}\otimes\chi)L(1-s,\sigma_{K}\otimes\chi).

Let
\epsilon(\sigma_{K}\otimes\chi):=\epsilon(1/2,\sigma_{K}\otimes\chi)
denote the associated root number.

For
\Sigma
a finite set of finite places of
{\mathbb{Q}}
, let
L^{(\Sigma)}(s,\sigma_{K}\otimes\chi)
denote the
incomplete
L
-function with Euler factors at
\Sigma\cup\{\infty\}
removed. In this article we simply denote
L^{(\infty)}(s,\pi_{K}\otimes\chi)
by

L(s,\pi_{K}\otimes\chi)
.
We use the same convention for Hecke
L
-functions.

2.
Definite Shimura sets

The section introduces definite quaternion algebras, associated Shimura sets and
modular forms.

2.1.
Set-up

2.1.1.
Imaginary quadratic field

Let
K
be an imaginary quadratic field and
-D_{K}<0
the discriminant. Put
\delta=\sqrt{-D_{K}}
.

Write
z\mapsto\overline{z}
for the complex conjugation on
K
. Define
\theta\in K
by

\theta=\frac{D^{\prime}+\delta}{2},\,D^{\prime}=\begin{cases}D_{K}&\text{ if }2\nmid D_{K},\\
D_{K}/2&\text{ if }2\mid D_{K}.\end{cases}

Then
O_{K}={\mathbb{Z}}+{\mathbb{Z}}\cdot\theta
and
\theta\overline{\theta}
is a local uniformizer of primes that are ramified in
K
.

Let
c\in{\mathrm{Gal}}(K/{\mathbb{Q}})
be the non-trivial element. For a Hecke character
\lambda
over
K
, put
\lambda^{c}=\lambda\circ c
.

2.1.2.
Definite quaternion algebra

Let
B
be a definite quaternion algebra over
\mathbb{Q}
and
S_{B}
the set of finite places at which it ramifies.
Let
D_{B}=\prod_{q\in S_{B}}q
be the discriminant of
B
.

Write
\rm T
and
\rm N
for the reduced trace and norm of
B
respectively. Let
G=B^{\times}
be an algebraic group over
\mathbb{Q}
. Note that
Z=\mathbb{Q}^{\times}
is the center of
G
.

Fix a prime
p\notin S_{B}
. Let
S
be a finite set of places containing
S_{B}
and
\ell\notin S
be a prime, which may equal
p
.
Let
\mathfrak{p}
be the prime of
K
above
p
induced by the embedding

\iota_{p}:K\hookrightarrow{\mathbb{C}}_{p}
.

Suppose that

\bullet

K
can be embedded into
B
,

\bullet

-1\in{\mathbb{Q}}_{q}^{\times}
is a norm of
K_{q}^{\times}
for any
q\notin S_{B}
.

We often fix an embedding
\iota:K\hookrightarrow B
, and then
choose a basis
\{1,J\}
of
B

as a
K
-algebra such that

\bullet

J^{2}=\beta\in\mathbb{Q}^{\times}
with
\beta<0
and
Jt=\overline{t}J
for all
t\in K
,

\bullet

\beta\in(\mathbb{Z}_{q}^{\times})^{2}
for all
q\in\{p,\ell\}\cup S\backslash S_{B}
.

The existence of
J
may be seen as follows: recall that
B_{q}
splits if and only if
\beta\in{\rm N}(K_{q}^{\times})
. Take
k\in K^{\times}
such that
-N(k)N(J)\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{q}^{\times 2}
for all
q\in\{p,\ell\}\cup S\backslash S_{B}
. Then replacing
J
by
Jk
the second property holds.

Let
\sqrt{\beta}\in\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}
be a square root of
\beta
.

Fix an isomorphism

i=\prod i_{q}:\widehat{B}^{(S_{B})}\simeq\widehat{M_{2}}({\mathbb{A}}_{f}^{(S_{B})})

(2.1)

as follows. For
q\in\{p,\ell\}\cup S\backslash S_{B}
,
define
i_{q}:B_{q}\simeq M_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{q})
by

i_{q}(\theta)=\begin{pmatrix}{\rm T(\theta)}&{-\rm{\rm{N}}(\theta)}\\
1&0\\
\end{pmatrix};\quad i_{q}(J)=\sqrt{\beta}\cdot\begin{pmatrix}-1&\rm T(\theta)\\
0&1\\
\end{pmatrix}\quad(\sqrt{\beta}\in\mathbb{Z}_{q}^{\times}).

(2.2)

For a finite place
q\notin\{p,\ell\}\cup S
, choose
i_{q}:B_{q}\simeq M_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{q})
such that

i_{q}(O_{K}\otimes{\mathbb{Z}}_{q})\subset M_{2}(\mathbb{Z}_{q}).

(2.3)

We further choose
i_{q}
so that
i_{q}(J)\in i_{q}(K_{q}^{\times}){\mathrm{GL}}_{2}({\mathbb{Z}}_{q})
for all
q\notin S
.

From now, we often identify
B_{q}
with
M_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{q})
via
i_{q}
for finite
q\notin S_{B}
, and in turn
G_{q}
with
{\mathrm{GL}}_{2}({\mathbb{Q}}_{q})
.

2.2.
Modular forms

2.2.1.
Classical modular forms

Let
A\subset{\mathbb{C}}
be a
{\mathbb{Z}}
-algebra and
U\subset\widehat{B}^{\times}
an open compact subgroup.

Let
M_{2}(U,A)
be the space of modular forms of weight
2
, trivial central character defined over
A
, which consists of functions
f:\widehat{B}^{\times}\to A
such that

f(z\gamma gu)=f(g)\text{ for }\gamma\in G(\mathbb{Q}),\,u\in U,\ z\in\widehat{{\mathbb{Q}}}^{\times}.

Via right translation,
M_{2}(A):=\varinjlim_{U}M_{2}(U,A)
is an admissible
G({\mathbb{A}}_{f})
-representation.
The space
M_{2}({\mathbb{C}})
can be identified with automorphic forms on
G({\mathbb{A}})
on which
\widehat{{\mathbb{Q}}}^{\times}G_{\infty}
acts trivially.

2.2.2.
\ell
-adic modular forms

Let
\ell\nmid S
be a prime
as in §
2.1
.

Let
A\subset\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}
be a
{\mathbb{Z}}_{(\ell)}
-algebra. We regard it as a subalgebra of
\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell}
via the fixed embedding
\iota_{\ell}:\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}\rightarrow\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell}
.
For
f\in M_{2}(U,A)
, we refer to
\widehat{f}:=\iota_{\ell}\circ f\in M_{2}(U,\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell})
as the
\ell
-adic avatar of
f\in M_{2}(U,A)
, often simply denoted by
f
.

2.3.
Special points

This subsection describes an analogue of CM points in the definite setting, which arises from the

{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
-anticyclotomic extension of
K
.

Let
S^{+}\subset S
(resp.
S^{-}\subset S
) be a subset consisting of primes that are split (resp. non-split) in
K
. Note that
S^{+}\cap S_{B}=\emptyset
since
K\hookrightarrow B
.
For
q\in S^{+}
, choose a prime
w
of
K
above
q
.

2.3.1.
Toric embedding

We identify
G({\mathbb{A}}_{f}^{(S_{B})})
with
{\mathrm{GL}}_{2}({\mathbb{A}}_{f}^{(S_{B})})
as in (
2.1
).
For
{g,h\in\widehat{B}^{\times}}
, put

\iota_{h}(g)=h^{-1}gh.

For a finite place
q\nmid p
, define
\varsigma_{q}\in G(\mathbb{Q}_{q})
by

\displaystyle\varsigma_{q}=

\displaystyle 1\text{ if $q\nmid pS^{+}$,}

(2.4)

\displaystyle\varsigma_{q}=

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}\theta&\overline{\theta}\\
1&1\\
\end{pmatrix}\in{\mathrm{GL}}_{2}(K_{w})={\mathrm{GL}}_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{q})\text{ if $q\in S^{+}$.}

If
q\in S^{+}\setminus\{p\}
and
t=(t_{1},t_{2})\in K_{q}:=K\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{Q}_{q}=K_{w}\oplus K_{\overline{w}}
, note that

\iota_{\varsigma_{q}}(t)=\begin{pmatrix}t_{1}&0\\
0&t_{2}\\
\end{pmatrix}.

(2.5)

For a non-negative integer
n
, define
\varsigma_{p}^{(n)}\in G(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
as follows.
If
p
splits in
K
as
(p)=\mathfrak{p}\overline{\mathfrak{p}}
, then

\displaystyle\varsigma_{p}^{(n)}=

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}\theta&-1\\
1&0\\
\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}p^{n}&0\\
0&1\\
\end{pmatrix}\in{\mathrm{GL}}_{2}(K_{\mathfrak{p}})={\mathrm{GL}}_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}).

(2.6)

If
p
is non-split in
K
, then

\displaystyle\varsigma_{p}^{(n)}=

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}0&1\\
-1&0\\
\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}p^{n}&0\\
0&1\\
\end{pmatrix}.

(2.7)

The above local embeddings lead to a family of embeddings
\iota_{\varsigma^{(n)}}:\widehat{K}\rightarrow\widehat{B}
.

2.3.2.
Quaternionic order

We introduce an order in the definite quaternion algebra, with respect to which special points will be introduced in the next subsection.

Let
R\subset B
be an order such that

\bullet

For
q\notin S\cup\{p\}
,
R_{q}=M_{2}({\mathbb{Z}}_{q})
;

\bullet

For
q\in S_{B}
,
R_{q}
contains
\iota_{\varsigma_{q}}O_{K_{q}}
;

\bullet

For
q\in S\backslash(\{p\}\cup S_{B})
,
R_{q}\cap\iota_{\varsigma_{q}}O_{K_{q}}
is a fixed order in
\iota_{\varsigma_{q}}O_{K_{q}}
;

\bullet

R_{p}
is the standard Eichler order

M_{0}(p^{s})_{p}=\left\{\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{pmatrix}\in M_{2}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p})\ |\ p^{s}|c\right\}

of discriminant
p^{s}
for an integer
s\geq 0
.

In view of the last property and the choice of
\varsigma_{p}^{(n)}
in (
2.6
) and (
2.7
), we have

R_{p}\cap\iota_{\varsigma_{p}^{(n)}}K_{p}=\iota_{\varsigma_{p}^{(n)}}O_{K_{p},p^{n}}

for
n\geq s
, where
O_{K_{p},p^{n}}
is the order of
O_{K_{p}}
of conductor
p^{n}
.

Suppose that

\widehat{R}\cap\iota_{\varsigma^{(n)}}\widehat{K}=\iota_{\varsigma^{(n)}}\widehat{O}_{K,p^{n}c}

for
n\geq s
, the latter being order of conductor
p^{n}c
.

2.3.3.
Special points

Define
x_{n,c}:{\mathbb{A}}_{K}^{\times}\to G({\mathbb{A}})
by

x_{n,c}(a):=a\cdot\varsigma^{(n)}\quad(\varsigma^{(n)}:=\varsigma_{p}^{(n)}\prod_{q\neq p}\varsigma_{q}).

(2.8)

This gives a family of special points

\{x_{n,c}(a)\}_{a\in{\mathbb{A}}_{K}^{\times}}
.

For
c=1
, we denote
x_{n,c}(a)
just by
x_{n}(a)
.

3.
Explicit Waldspurger formula

This section presents explicit Waldspurger formulas in a general context.
It is based on the work of Cai–Shu–Tian
[
21
]
to which we refer for an introduction. The main results are anticyclotomic twist family versions of the formula which involve a fixed test vector (cf. Theorems
3.12
and
3.15
).

3.1.
Backdrop

3.1.1.
Setting

Let
K
be an imaginary quadratic field.

Let
\sigma
be a unitary irreducible cuspidal automorphic representation of
{\mathrm{GL}}_{2}({\mathbb{A}})
with trivial central character and conductor
N
such that

(H1)

The archimedean component
\sigma_{\infty}
is the discrete series of weight
2
;

(H2)

\epsilon(\sigma_{K})=+1
.

Here
\epsilon(\sigma_{K})
denotes the root number of the base change
\sigma_{K}
.

The following example will be of particular interest for the paper.

Example 1
.

Let
\lambda
be a self-dual Hecke character over
K
of infinity type
(1,0)
in the sense of (
1.1
).
Then the automorphic representation generated by the associated theta series
\phi_{\lambda}
satisfies the hypotheses (H1) and (H2).

Let
B
be the quaternion algebra over
{\mathbb{Q}}
such that the Tunnell–Saito condition

\epsilon(\sigma_{K,q})=\epsilon(B_{q})

(3.1)

holds for any place
q
, where
\epsilon(\sigma_{K,q})
denotes the local base change root number and
\epsilon(B_{q})
is the Hasse invariant of
B_{q}
. It is a definite quaternion algebra.

Lemma 3.1
.

Suppose that

(H3)

\epsilon(\sigma_{q})=-1
for
q|(D_{K},N^{-}_{\rm sf})
.

Then we have

N_{\rm sf}^{-}\mid D_{B}\mid N^{-}.

Proof.

Let
q
be a prime divisor of
N_{\rm sf}^{-}
.
It follows from
[
80
, Prop. 3.1.2]
that the condition (H3) is equivalent to
\sigma_{q}=\chi_{0}\otimes{\mathrm{St}}
for
\chi_{0}
quadratic so that
\chi_{0}\circ{\rm{N}}_{K_{q}/{\mathbb{Q}}_{q}}
is trivial.
Thus the result is a consequence of
[
21
, Lem. 3.1]
.

∎

Lemma 3.2
.

Suppose that
\pi
has CM by
K
. Then

D_{B}=\prod_{\eta_{K_{q}}(-1)=-1}q.

Proof.

Suppose that
\sigma
is generated by
\phi_{\lambda}
as in Example
1
. For each
q
, let
\psi_{q}
be a non-trivial character of
{\mathbb{Q}}_{q}
and
\psi_{K_{q}}=\psi_{q}\circ{\mathrm{tr}}_{K_{q}/{\mathbb{Q}}_{q}}
. Then

\begin{aligned} \epsilon(\sigma_{K_{q}},\psi_{K_{q}})=&\epsilon(\sigma_{q},\psi_{q})\epsilon(\sigma_{q}\otimes\eta_{K_{q}},\psi_{q})\eta_{K_{q}}(-1)\\
=&\epsilon(\lambda_{q}^{*},\psi_{K_{q}})^{2}(\lambda_{K_{q}}(\psi_{q})^{2}\eta_{K_{q}}(-1))\\
=&\epsilon(\lambda_{q}^{*},\psi_{K_{q}})^{2}\\
=&\eta_{K_{q}}(-1).\end{aligned},

Here
\lambda_{q}^{*}=\lambda_{q}\cdot|\cdot|_{K_{q}}^{1/2}
denotes unitarisation of
\lambda_{q}
, the second equality follows from
[
52
, Thm. 4.7]
, and
the last from

\displaystyle\overline{\epsilon(\lambda_{q}^{*},\psi_{K_{q}})}=

\displaystyle\epsilon((\lambda_{q}^{*})^{-1},\psi_{K_{q}})\cdot(\lambda_{q}^{*})^{-1}(-1)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\epsilon(\lambda_{q}^{*,c},\psi_{K_{q}})\cdot\eta_{K_{q}}(-1)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\epsilon(\lambda_{q}^{*},\psi_{K_{q}})\cdot\eta_{K_{q}}(-1).

In view of (
3.1
) the proof concludes.
∎

Let
G=B^{\times}
be the algebraic group over
{\mathbb{Q}}
.
Let
\pi=\otimes\pi_{q}
denote the Jacquet–Langlands transfer of
\sigma
to
G({\mathbb{A}})
,
which exists by Tunnell–Saito theorem
[
85
,
78
]
and the condition (
3.1
). It is a unitary irreducible cuspidal automorphic representation with trivial central character such that

\bullet

\pi_{\infty}
is the trivial representation of
G_{\infty}
,

\bullet

For
q\mid N_{\rm sf}^{-}
,
\pi_{q}
is an unramified one dimensional representation of
G_{q}
.

\bullet

If
q\nmid N
, then
\pi_{q}=\sigma_{q}
is an unramified principal series
\pi(\mu_{q},\mu_{q}^{-1})
of

G_{q}={\mathrm{GL}}_{2}({\mathbb{Q}}_{q})
.

Now let
S
be the set of prime divisors of
N
.

Let
p\nmid D_{B}
be a prime.
Let
K_{\infty}
be the anticyclotomic
{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
-extension of
K
and
\Gamma={\mathrm{Gal}}(K_{\infty}/K)
.
Let
\Xi_{p}
be the set of
characters
\chi:\Gamma\to{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}\text{ of finite order.}

Lemma 3.3
.

For any
\chi\in\Xi_{p}
with
\mathrm{cond^{r}}{\chi_{p}}\geq p^{v_{p}(N)}
,
we have

\epsilon(\pi_{K}\otimes\chi)=+1.

Proof.

By the Tunnell–Saito theorem
[
85
,
78
]
, we have

\dim_{{\mathbb{C}}}{\mathrm{Hom}}_{{\mathbb{A}}_{K}^{\times}}(\pi,{\mathbb{C}})\leq 1,

(3.2)

and the equality is equivalent to the condition (
3.1
).

Let
q
be a prime.
If
q=p
, then
B_{p}
is split, and so

{\mathrm{Hom}}_{K_{p}^{\times}}(\pi_{p},\chi_{p}^{-1})\neq 0

by
[
21
, Lem. 3.1]
since
\mathrm{cond^{r}}{\chi_{p}}\geq p^{v_{p}(N)}
.
Now consider the case
q\neq p
.
If
q
is split in
K
, then
B_{q}
is split and so

{\mathrm{Hom}}_{K_{q}^{\times}}(\pi_{q},\chi_{q}^{-1})\neq 0
by Tunnell–Saito.
Lastly, if
q
is non-split in
K
, then
\chi_{q}
is trivial and hence

{\mathrm{Hom}}_{K_{q}^{\times}}(\pi_{q},\chi_{q}^{-1})={\mathrm{Hom}}_{K_{q}^{\times}}(\pi_{q},{\mathbb{C}})\neq 0

by (
3.2
).

It follows that
\dim_{{\mathbb{C}}}{\mathrm{Hom}}_{{\mathbb{A}}_{K}^{\times}}(\pi,\chi^{-1})=1
, concluding the proof.

∎

3.2.
Test vectors

For a self-dual pair
(\pi,\chi)
,
the Waldspurger formula
[
89
]
links the Rankin–Selberg
L
-value
L(\frac{1}{2},\pi_{K}\otimes\chi)
with
K^{\times}
-toric period of a test vector on
G
.

Recall that, following Gross and Prasad
[
35
]
, a form in
\pi
is called a test vector if its image under a basis of
{\mathrm{Hom}}_{K_{{\mathbb{A}}}^{\times}}(\pi,\chi^{-1})
is non-zero with respect to a suitable embedding
{\mathbb{A}}_{K}\hookrightarrow B_{{\mathbb{A}}}
.
Let
p\nmid D_{B}
be a prime. This subsection describes a choice of the test vector which is uniform
for any
\chi\in\Xi_{p}
with conductor at least
p^{v_{p}(N)}
.

In the rest of this section we suppose that the hypotheses (H1)-(H3) hold.

3.2.1.
Test vector

Fix a prime
p\nmid D_{B}
.
For
M_{0}(q^{n})_{q}=\left\{\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{pmatrix}\in M_{2}({\mathbb{Z}}_{q})\ |\ q^{n}|c\right\}

the Eichler of discriminant
q^{n}
, let
U_{0}(q^{n})_{q}=M_{0}(q^{n})_{q}\cap{\mathrm{GL}}_{2}({\mathbb{Z}}_{q})
.

Definition 3.4
.

For a place
q
, define a non-zero vector
\varphi_{q}\in\pi_{q}
as follows.

(a)

For
q\mid N_{\rm sf}^{-}
,
\varphi_{q}
is a basis of the one dimensional representation
\pi_{q}
of
G_{q}
.

(b)

For
q\mid N_{\rm a}^{-}
,
\varphi_{q}
is invariant under the action of
K_{q}^{\times}
if
q\neq p
and
\varphi_{p}
is fixed by
U_{0}(p^{v_{p}(N)})_{p}
.

(c)

For
q\nmid N^{-}
,
\varphi_{q}
is fixed by
U_{0}(q^{v_{q}(N)})_{q}
.

(d)

For
q=\infty
,
\varphi_{q}
is a non-zero element of the trivial representation
\pi_{\infty}
.

Remark 3.5
.

The above choice of
\varphi_{q}
is as in
[
21
, §3]
.

Definition 3.6
.

Let
R\subset B
be an order of discriminant
N
satisfying the following.

(a)

For
q|N_{\rm sf}^{-}
,
R_{q}\subset B_{q}
is maximal.

(b)

For
q|N_{\rm a}^{-}
,
R_{q}\subset B_{q}
so that
R_{q}\cap\iota_{\varsigma_{q}}K_{q}=\iota_{\varsigma_{q}}O_{K_{q}}
if
q\neq p
, and
R_{p}
is the Eichler order
M_{0}(p^{v_{p}(N)})_{p}
if
q=p
.

(c)

For
q|N^{+}
,
R_{q}\subset B_{q}
is the Eichler order
M_{0}(q^{v_{q}(N)})_{q}
.

(d)

For
q\nmid N
,
R_{q}=M_{2}({\mathbb{Z}}_{q})
.

Lemma 3.7
.

(i)

For any
q
, an order
R_{q}\subset B_{q}
with discriminant
q^{v_{q}(N)}

as in Definition
3.6

exists and is unique up to
K_{q}^{\times}
-conjugation. Moreover, if
q|N^{-}
and
q\nmid p
, then it is unique.

(ii)

If
q\nmid p
, then
R_{q}\cap\iota_{\varsigma_{q}}K_{q}=\iota_{\varsigma_{q}}O_{K_{q}}
and if
q=p
,

R_{p}\cap\iota_{\varsigma_{p}^{(n)}}O_{K_{p}}=\iota_{\varsigma_{p}^{(n)}}O_{K_{p},p^{n}}

for
n\geq v_{p}(N)
.

Proof.

For the existence and uniqueness of
R_{q}
, see
[
21
, Lem. 3.3]
and
[
21
, Lem. 3.4]
respectively. The second part just follows from the definition of
\varsigma_{q}
and the choice of
R_{q}
.
∎

Note that
R
satisfies the properties in §
2.3.2
for
S
consisting of prime factors of
N
.

Lemma 3.8
.

(i)

For any
q
, there exists
\varphi_{q}\in\pi_{q}
as in Definition
3.4
.
Moreover, it is unique up to scalars.

(ii)

For any
q
, we have
\varphi_{q}\in(\pi_{q})^{R_{q}^{\times}}
.

Proof.

(i)

It suffices to consider the cases (b) and (c).
For (c) or (b) with
q=p
, the assertion is a simple consequence of the newform theory. As for the remaining case, in view of (
3.1
) it follows from the Tunnell–Saito theorem
[
85
,
78
]
.

(ii)

This is a special case of
[
21
, Prop. 3.8]
.

∎

Remark 3.9
.

Note that
\varphi_{q}
is a newform besides the case (b).

Definition 3.10

(Test vector)
.

Define
\varphi\in\pi
by

\varphi=\otimes_{q}\varphi_{q}

for
\varphi_{q}
as in Definition
3.4
.

Note that for any prime
p\nmid N^{-}
the test vector
\varphi
does not depend on
p
.
On the other hand, if
p|N_{\rm a}^{-}
, the test vector is new at
p
, and we use the notation
\varphi^{\{p\}}
to emphasise the dependence.

The following preliminary will be used in our later arguments.

Lemma 3.11
.

Suppose that
v_{q}(N)>v_{q}(D_{K})
for any prime
q|D_{K}
.
Then
{\rm{N}}(R_{r}^{\times})={\rm{N}}(O_{K_{r}}^{\times})
for any prime
r
.

Proof.

Let
r\nmid pD_{K}
be a prime. Then we have

O_{K_{r}}\subset R_{r}
.
Since
{\rm{N}}:O_{K_{r}}^{\times}\rightarrow{\mathbb{Z}}_{r}^{\times}
is surjective for
r\nmid D_{K}
, it follows that

{\rm{N}}(R_{r}^{\times})={\rm{N}}(O_{K_{r}}^{\times})
.

For
r=p
, note that
B_{p}
split and
R_{p}=M_{0}(p^{v_{p}(N)})_{p}
is an Eichler order, and so
{\rm{N}}(R_{p}^{\times})\rightarrow{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}^{\times}
is surjective.

Now consider the remaining case, namely
r
is ramified in
K
.
Note that
R_{r}
is of the form
O_{K_{r}}+\varpi^{n-1}O_{B_{r}}
, where
\varpi\in K_{r}
is a uniformiser,
n=v_{r}(N)
and
O_{B_{r}}
the maximal order of
B_{r}
.
We have

O_{B_{r}}=O_{K_{r}}+\varpi^{v_{r}(D_{B})-v_{r}(D_{K})}O_{K_{r}}(1+J),

where
{\rm{N}}(J)\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{r}^{\times}
and for
r=2
, further choose

J^{2}\equiv 1\pmod{D_{K_{r}}/r}

to not lie in the norm of
K_{r}^{\times}
.
In this case,
v_{r}(N)>v_{r}(D_{K_{r}})
, thus
R_{r}=O_{K_{r}}+\varpi^{n-v_{r}(D_{K_{r}})}O_{K_{r}}J
.

For
a\in O_{K_{r}}
and
b\in\varpi O_{K_{r}}
, we have

\displaystyle{\rm{N}}(a+bJ)=

\displaystyle(a+bJ)(\overline{a}+\overline{J}\overline{b})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\rm{\displaystyle N}}(a)+{\rm{N}}(b){\rm{N}}(J)

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle{\rm{\displaystyle N}}(a)\pmod{D_{K_{r}}}.

It follows that
{\rm{N}}(R_{r}^{\times})={\rm{N}}(O_{K_{r}}^{\times})
.
∎

3.2.2.
p
-stabilization

Let
R\subset B
be an order as in §
2.3.2
that is maximal outside
N
.

We first suppose that
p\nmid N
.
Note that
G
is split at
p
and
\pi_{p}
is an unramified principal series
\pi(\mu_{p},\mu_{p}^{-1})
.
Put

\alpha_{p}:=\mu_{p}(p)|p|_{p}^{-1/2},\ \beta_{p}:=\mu_{p}^{-1}(p)|p|_{p}^{-1/2},\ a_{p}=\alpha_{p}+\beta_{p}.

(3.3)

The
p
-stabilization
f^{\dagger}:=f_{\alpha_{p}}^{\dagger}
of
f\in M_{2}(R,{\mathbb{C}})[\pi]
with respect to
\alpha_{p}
is defined by

f^{\dagger}=f-\frac{1}{\alpha_{p}}\cdot\pi(\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
0&p\\
\end{pmatrix})f.

(3.4)

Since the
U_{p}
-operator is given by

U_{p}h(g)=\sum_{x\in\mathbb{Z}/p\mathbb{Z}}h(g\begin{pmatrix}p&x\\
0&1\\
\end{pmatrix}),

note that
f^{\dagger}
is an
U_{p}
-eigenform with eigenvalue
\alpha_{p}
.

If
p|N
and
p\nmid D_{B}
, then we simply put
f^{\dagger}=f
.

In either case we have

\displaystyle f^{\dagger}(x_{n,c}(\gamma au))=

\displaystyle f^{\dagger}(x_{n,c}(a_{f}))

(3.5)

\displaystyle(\gamma\in K^{\times},

\displaystyle a=(a_{\infty},a_{f})\in{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}\times\widehat{K},\,u\in{\widehat{O}_{K,p^{n}c}^{\times}}).

3.2.3.
Normalised test vectors

The following normalisation of test vectors will appear throughout the paper.

Let
\ell\nmid N
be a prime
as in §
2.1
.
Let
\mathbb{Q}(\pi)
be the Hecke field of
\pi
, and

O_{\pi,\ell}\subset{\mathbb{C}}_{\ell}
the completion
of the ring of integers with respect to the prime
{\mathfrak{l}}|\ell
determined via the embedding
\iota_{\ell}
.

Let
\varphi\in M_{2}(\widehat{R}^{\times},{\mathbb{Q}}(\pi))
be an
\ell
-optimally normalised test vector, i.e.
\varphi
is a test vector as in Definition
3.10
such that

\varphi\in M_{2}(\widehat{R}^{\times},O_{\pi,\ell})

and

\varphi\equiv\hskip-10.0pt/\ 0\pmod{{\mathfrak{l}}}.

The above normalisation is crucial in
\ell
-integrality of certain Rankin–Selberg
L
-values as well as construction of
\ell
-adic
L
-functions.

3.3.
Explicit Waldspurger formulas

The aim of this subsection is to explicitly link Rankin–Selberg
L
-values with toric periods of
the test vector introduced in Section

3.2
or its variants.

3.3.1.
Setting

We begin with generalities regarding Waldspurger formula, and then specialise to the prior setting.

Let
B
be a quaternion algebra over
{\mathbb{Q}}
. Let
\pi
be an irreducible cuspidal automorphic representation of
B_{{\mathbb{A}}}^{\times}
and
\sigma
its base change to
{\mathrm{GL}}_{2}({\mathbb{A}})
.
Let
K
be an quadratic field with an embedding
K\hookrightarrow B
and
\chi
a Hecke character over
K
such that

\chi|_{{\mathbb{A}}^{\times}}\omega_{\pi}=1

for
\omega_{\pi}
the central character of
\pi
.
Suppose that

{\mathrm{Hom}}_{K_{{\mathbb{A}}}^{\times}}(\pi,\chi^{-1})\neq 0.

Then the Waldspurger formula
[
89
,
91
]
connects the toric periods

P_{\chi}(f)=\int_{K^{\times}{\mathbb{A}}^{\times}\backslash{\mathbb{A}}_{K}^{\times}}\chi(t)f(t)dt,\quad f\in\pi

with the Rankin–Selberg
L
-value
L(1/2,\pi_{K}\otimes\chi)
.

Now let the setting, and in particular
B
and
\pi
, be as in §
3.1.1
. Let
\chi\in\Xi_{p}
be a finite order anticyclotomic Hecke character.
Let
R\subset B
be an order as in Definition
3.6
, and
pick a test vector
\varphi
as in Definition
3.10
.

Let
X_{\widehat{R}^{\times}}
be the Shimura set
B^{\times}\backslash\widehat{B}^{\times}/\widehat{R}^{\times}
, whose elements may be chosen as a set of representatives in
\widehat{B}^{\times}=G({\mathbb{A}}_{f})
. For
\varphi\in\pi^{\widehat{R}^{\times}}
, define the inner product of
\varphi
by

\langle\varphi,\varphi\rangle:=\sum_{[g_{i}]\in X_{\widehat{R}^{\times}}}\frac{1}{w_{i}}\cdot\varphi(g_{i})^{2},\quad w_{i}:=[B^{\times}\cap g_{i}\widehat{R}^{\times}g_{i}^{-1}:{\mathbb{Z}}^{\times}].

(3.6)

For
\phi
the newform of level
\Gamma_{0}(N)
associated to
\pi
, the Petersson norm is defined by

(\phi,\phi)=\int_{\Gamma_{0}(N)\backslash{\mathcal{H}}}|\phi(z)|^{2}\frac{dxdy}{y^{2}},

with
z=x+iy
.

For
\chi\in\Xi_{p}
of conductor
p^{s}
so that
s\geq v_{p}(N)
, we have
\widehat{R}\cap\iota_{\varsigma^{(s)}}\widehat{O}_{K}=\iota_{\varsigma^{(s)}}\widehat{O}_{K,p^{s}}
. For
n=\max\{1,s\}
, the toric period
P_{\chi}(\pi(\varsigma^{(n)})\varphi^{\dagger})
with respect to embedding
\iota
is essentially given by

P(\varsigma^{(n)},\varphi^{\dagger},\chi):=\sum_{[a]\in{\rm G}_{n}}\chi(a)\varphi^{\dagger}(x_{n}(a)),

where
{\rm G}_{n}=K^{\times}\backslash\widehat{K}^{\times}/\widehat{O}_{K,p^{n}}^{\times}
(cf.
[
21
, Lem. 2.3]
).

The following
p
-adic multiplier will also appear in Waldspurger formulas:

e_{p}(\pi,\chi)=\begin{cases}1&\text{if $\chi_{p}$ is ramified};\\
(1-\alpha_{p}^{-1}\chi(\mathfrak{p}))(1-\alpha_{p}^{-1}\chi(\overline{\mathfrak{p}}))&\text{if $\chi_{p}$ is unramified, $p=\mathfrak{p}\overline{\mathfrak{p}}$ is split};\\
1-\alpha_{p}^{-2}&\text{if $\chi_{p}$ is unramified, $p=\mathfrak{p}$ is inert.}\\
\end{cases}

3.3.2.
Explicit Waldspurger formula I

The main result of this subsection is the following Waldspurger formula.

Theorem 3.12
.

Let
(\pi,\chi)
be a self-dual pair as in §
3.3.1
with

\pi
of conductor
N
and

\chi\in\Xi_{p}
. Let
\varphi
be an associated test vector as in Definition
3.10
, and
\varphi^{\dagger}
its
\alpha_{p}
-stabilization if
p\nmid N
, and else put
\varphi^{\dagger}=\varphi
. Suppose that the
p
-local character associated to
\chi
is of conductor
p^{s}
with
s\geq v_{p}(N)
, and put
n=\max\{1,s\}
.
Then we have

\displaystyle p^{-s}\cdot P(\varsigma^{(n)},\varphi^{\dagger},\chi)^{2}=

\displaystyle\frac{\langle{\varphi,\varphi}\rangle}{8\pi^{2}(\phi,\phi)}\sqrt{|D_{K}|}\cdot L^{(p^{s}N_{\rm{r}})}(\frac{1}{2},\pi_{K}\otimes\chi)

\displaystyle\cdot\frac{\epsilon(\pi)}{\epsilon(\pi_{p})}{2^{\#\Sigma_{D}}}\chi_{S^{+}\backslash\{p\}}({\mathfrak{N}}^{+})\begin{cases}1,\quad&\text{$v_{p}(N)\geq 1$ or $s\geq 1$},\\
e_{p}(\pi,\chi)^{2}\alpha_{p}^{2},\quad&v_{p}(N)=0,s=0.\\
\end{cases}

Here
N_{\rm{r}}
is the factor of
N_{\rm{a}}^{-}
precisely divisible by the ramified primes and

\Sigma_{D}
the set of prime divisors of
(D_{K},N)
coprime to
p
,
S^{+}=\{q\ |\ q|N^{+}\}
,
N^{+}={\mathfrak{N}}^{+}\overline{{\mathfrak{N}}^{+}}
with
w|{\mathfrak{N}}^{+}
for
q=w\overline{w}
, and
\chi_{T}=\prod_{q\in T}\chi_{q}
.

The above result is a consequence of a general explicit Waldspurger formula
[
21
, Thm. 1.8]
as we now describe.
In
loc. cit.
absolute value square of toric period appears, and the following analysis relates it to square of the toric period.

Lemma 3.13
.

For
\chi\in\Xi_{p}
of conductor
p^{s}
with
s\geq v_{p}(N)
and
n=\max\{1,s\}
, we have

P(J\varsigma^{(n)}\tau,\varphi^{\dagger},\chi)=P(\varsigma^{(n)},\varphi^{\dagger},\chi)\frac{\epsilon(\pi)}{\epsilon(\pi_{p})}.

Proof.

Let
S
be a set of primes as in §
2.1.2
given by prime factors of
N
.

If
q\notin S
, note that
J\in K_{q}^{\times}{\mathrm{GL}}_{2}({\mathbb{Z}}_{q})
and
\varsigma_{q}=1
, and so the Hecke action of
J
at
q
does not change the toric period.
For
q\in S^{+}\backslash\{p\}
,

\varsigma_{q}^{-1}J\varsigma_{q}\tau\varphi_{q}=w_{\pi_{q}}\varphi_{q}=\epsilon(\pi_{q})\varphi_{q},

where
w_{\pi_{q}}=\begin{pmatrix}&1\\
q^{v_{q}(N)}\end{pmatrix}\in{\mathrm{GL}}_{2}({\mathbb{Q}}_{q})
is the Atkin–Lehner operator (cf.
[
80
, Thm. 3.2.2]
).
If
q|N^{-}
and
q\nmid p
, we have
\chi_{q}=1
and
{\mathrm{Hom}}_{K_{q}^{\times}}(\pi_{q},{\mathbb{C}})\neq 0
. Let
P_{q}
be a basis. Then
J
acts on
P_{q}
by
\epsilon(\pi_{q})\epsilon(B_{q})

[
71
, Thm. 4]
. Thus

P_{q}(J\varsigma_{q}\varphi_{q})=\epsilon(\pi_{q})\epsilon(B_{q})P_{q}(\varsigma_{q}\varphi_{q}).

Now consider the case
q=p
.
Then
\varsigma_{p}^{(n),-1}J\varsigma_{p}^{(n)}
stabilises
\varphi^{\dagger}
.

Therefore

P(J\varsigma^{(n)}\tau,\varphi^{\dagger},\chi)=\prod_{q\in S,q\neq p}\epsilon(\pi_{q})\epsilon(B_{q})P(\varsigma^{(n)},\varphi^{\dagger},\chi),

and the result then follows from the fact that
\prod_{q|S,q\neq p}\epsilon(\pi_{q})\epsilon(B_{q})=\frac{\epsilon(\pi)}{\epsilon(\pi_{p})}
.
∎

Proof of Theorem
3.12
.

The following is based on
[
21
, Thm. 1.8]
.

Taking
f
in
ibid.
to be
\varsigma^{(s)}\varphi
, we have

\left|\frac{P(\varsigma^{(s)},{\varphi},\chi)}{p^{s}[O_{K,p^{s}}^{\times}:{\mathbb{Z}}^{\times}]}\right|^{2}={2^{\#\Sigma_{D}}}{}\frac{\langle{\varphi,\varphi}\rangle_{H,\widehat{R}^{\times}}}{8\pi^{2}(\phi,\phi)_{U_{0}(N)}}\sqrt{|D_{K}|}\cdot L^{(p^{s}N_{\rm{a}})}(\frac{1}{2},\pi_{K}\otimes\chi),

(3.7)

where
\langle{\ ,\ }\rangle_{H,\widehat{R}^{\times}}
is Hermitian invariant pairing of level
\widehat{R}^{\times}
.
Indeed, this follows from
[
21
, Thm. 1.8]
since

\langle{\varphi_{1},\varphi_{2}}\rangle_{H,\widehat{R}^{\times}}=\langle{\varphi_{1},\overline{\varphi}_{2}}\rangle,

P_{\chi}^{0}(\varsigma^{(s)}\varphi)=P(\varsigma^{(s)},\varphi,\chi),

C_{\infty}=4\pi^{3}
,
2\pi\langle{\phi^{0},\phi^{0}}\rangle_{U_{0}(N)}=(\phi,\phi)_{U_{0}(N)}
, and
\nu_{p^{s}}=[O_{K,p^{s}}^{\times}:{\mathbb{Z}}^{\times}]
in our setting, the notation being as in
[
21
]
.

Put

\tau=\begin{pmatrix}N^{+}&\\
&1\end{pmatrix}\in\prod_{q|N^{+},q\nmid p}{\mathrm{GL}}_{2}({\mathbb{Q}}_{q})\subset{\mathrm{GL}}_{2}({\mathbb{A}}_{f}).

Then in view of the
S
-version of Waldspurger formula
[
21
, Thm. 1.9]
we have

\frac{P(\varsigma^{(s)},\varphi,\chi)P(\varsigma^{(s)}\tau,\overline{\varphi},\chi^{-1})}{p^{s}[O_{K,p^{s}}^{\times}:{\mathbb{Z}}^{\times}]^{2}}=\frac{\langle{\varphi,\varphi}\rangle_{H,\widehat{R}^{\times}}}{8\pi^{2}(\phi,\phi)_{U_{0}(N)}}\cdot\sqrt{|D_{K}|}\chi_{S^{+}\backslash\{p\}}({\mathfrak{N}}^{+}){2^{\#\Sigma_{D}}}{}\cdot L^{(p^{s}N_{\rm{a}})}(\frac{1}{2},\pi_{K}\otimes\chi).

We now analyse the left hand side.
Since
\pi=\overline{\pi}
, by multiplicity one of
\varphi
(cf. Lemma
3.8
), note that

\varphi=C\overline{\varphi}
for a non-zero constant
C\in{\mathbb{C}}
.

Case I
. Suppose that
v_{p}(N)\geq 1
.

Then by definition,
\varphi^{\dagger}=\varphi
.
So we have

\displaystyle\frac{P(\varsigma^{(s)},\varphi,\chi)P(\varsigma^{(s)}\tau,\overline{\varphi},\chi^{-1})}{\langle{\varphi,\varphi}\rangle_{H,\widehat{R}^{\times}}}=

\displaystyle\frac{P(\varsigma^{(s)},\varphi,\chi)P(\varsigma^{(s)}\tau,{\varphi},\chi^{-1})}{\langle{\varphi,\varphi}\rangle_{R}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{P(\varsigma^{(s)},\varphi,\chi)P(J\varsigma^{(s)}\tau,{\varphi},\chi)}{\langle{\varphi,{\varphi}}\rangle_{R}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\epsilon(\pi)}{\epsilon(\pi_{p})}\frac{P(\varsigma^{(s)},\varphi,\chi)P(\varsigma^{(s)},{\varphi},\chi)}{\langle{\varphi,{\varphi}}\rangle_{R}}\quad(\text{Lemma \ref{sc}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\epsilon(\pi)}{\epsilon(\pi_{p})}\frac{P(\varsigma^{(s)},\varphi,\chi)^{2}}{\langle{\varphi,{\varphi}}\rangle_{R}}.

Here the first equality follows from the multiplicity one of
\varphi
(cf. Lemma
3.8
), and the second from automorphy of
\varphi
and the fact
\overline{t}=J^{-1}tJ
.

Therefore, noting that
[O_{K,p^{n}}^{\times}:{\mathbb{Z}}^{\times}]=1
for
n\geq 1
,
the result is a consequence of (
3.7
).

Case II
. Suppose that
v_{p}(N)=0
.

Then we similarly have

\frac{P(\varsigma^{(n)},\varphi^{\dagger},\chi)P(\varsigma^{(n)}\tau,\overline{\varphi_{\beta_{p}}^{\dagger}},\chi^{-1})}{\langle{\varphi,\varphi}\rangle_{H,\widehat{R}^{\times}}}=\frac{\epsilon(\pi)}{\epsilon(\pi_{p})}\frac{P(\varsigma^{(n)},\varphi^{\dagger},\chi)^{2}}{\langle{\varphi,{\varphi}}\rangle_{R}},

(3.8)

where the only difference in this case is that
\overline{\varphi_{\beta_{p}}^{\dagger}}=C\varphi^{\dagger}
(recall that
\varphi^{\dagger}:=\varphi^{\dagger}_{\alpha_{p}}
). In the following we consider these toric periods.

Henceforth, without loss of generality, we suppose that
L(1/2,\pi_{K}\otimes\chi)\neq 0
.
By the Waldspurger formula and multiplicity one of
{\mathrm{Hom}}_{{\mathbb{A}}_{K}^{\times}}(\pi,\chi^{-1})
, we then have

\displaystyle\frac{P(\varsigma^{(n)},\varphi^{\dagger},\chi)P(\varsigma^{(n)}\tau,\overline{\varphi_{\beta_{p}}^{\dagger}},\chi^{-1})}{[{\rm G}_{n}:{\rm G}_{s}]^{2}}=P(\varsigma^{(s)},\varphi,\chi)P(\varsigma^{(s)}\tau,\overline{\varphi},\chi^{-1})

(3.9)

\displaystyle\cdot\int_{K_{p}^{\times}/{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}^{\times}}\frac{(\iota_{\varsigma^{(n)}}(t)\varphi_{p}^{\dagger},\varphi^{\dagger}_{\beta_{p},p})\chi_{p}(t)}{(\varphi_{p},\varphi_{p})}d^{\times}t\cdot\left(\int_{K_{p}^{\times}/{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}^{\times}}\frac{(\iota_{\varsigma^{(s)}}(t)\varphi_{p},\varphi_{p})\chi_{p}(t)}{(\varphi_{p},\varphi_{p})}d^{\times}t\right)^{-1},

where the local invariant pairings are Hermitian. Here

P(\varsigma^{(s)},\varphi,\chi)=\sum_{[a]\in{\rm G}_{s}}\chi(a)\varphi(x_{s}(a))

with
{\rm G}_{s}=K^{\times}\backslash\widehat{K}^{\times}/\widehat{O}_{K,p^{s}}^{\times}
.

By
[
23
, Prop. 3.12]
,

\int_{K_{p}^{\times}/{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}^{\times}}\frac{(\iota_{\varsigma^{(s)}}(t)\varphi_{p},\varphi_{p})}{(\varphi_{p},\varphi_{p})}\chi_{p}(t)d^{\times}t=\begin{cases}|D_{K}|_{p}^{1/2}c_{p},\quad s=0,\\
|D_{K}|_{p}^{1/2}c_{p}L(1,\eta_{K_{p}})^{2}p^{-s},\quad s>0,\\
\end{cases}

where

c_{p}=\frac{L(2,1_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}})L(1/2,\pi_{K_{p}}\otimes\chi_{p})}{L(1,\eta_{K_{p}})L(1,\pi_{p},{\mathrm{ad}})}.

Recall that for normalised spherical Whittaker functional
W_{\pi_{p}}
so that
W_{\pi_{p}}(1)=1
, we have

(W_{\pi_{p}},W_{\pi_{p}})=\frac{L(1,\pi,{\mathrm{ad}})L(1,1_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}})}{L(2,1_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}})}

for
(\ ,\ )
the standard Hermitian pairing on the Whittaker model (cf.
[
21
, Prop. 3.11]
). In combination with
[
23
, Prop. 3.10]
, which is an explicit toric period formula for stabilized newforms with respect to the Hermitian pairing, we have

\int_{K_{p}^{\times}/{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}^{\times}}\frac{(\iota_{\varsigma^{(n)}}(t)\varphi_{\alpha_{p},p}^{\dagger},\varphi^{\dagger}_{\beta_{p},p})\chi(t)d^{\times}t}{(\varphi_{p},\varphi_{p})}=|D_{K}|_{p}^{1/2}c_{p}\cdot L(1,\eta_{K_{p}})^{2}\cdot\begin{cases}e_{p}(\pi,\chi)^{2}\alpha_{p}^{2}p^{-2}&\text{ if $s=0$},\\
p^{-s}&\text{ if $s>0$}.\end{cases}

Note that

[{\rm G}_{1}:{\rm G}_{0}]L(1,\eta_{K_{p}})p^{-1}=\frac{1}{[O_{K}^{\times}:O_{K,p}^{\times}]}.

Therefore, in view of the previous paragraph and (
3.7
), (
3.8
), (
3.9
), the proof concludes.

∎

3.3.3.
Explicit Waldspurger formula II

In this subsection we consider a choice of test vector for self-dual pairs
(\pi,\chi)

which differs from §
3.2.1
. Specifically, newform is a test vector at certain primes
q
so that
\pi_{q}
is supercuspidal and
\chi_{q}=1
, as shown in section
7
. This choice will be a key to subsequent applications.

Setting

We consider self-dual pairs
(\pi,\chi)
for
\chi\in\Xi_{p}
as in §
3.3.1
.
Let
q\neq p
be a prime such that

\bullet

q
is an odd prime inert in
K
,

\bullet

B_{q}
split and
\pi_{q}=\pi_{\lambda}
is the CM lifting of a character
\lambda
of
K_{q}^{\times}
with conductor
{q^{m}}
for
m\geq 2
such that
\lambda|_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{q}^{\times}}=\eta_{K_{q}}
.

Definition 3.14
.

A test vector
\widetilde{\varphi}=\otimes_{v}\widetilde{\varphi}_{v}
for
(\pi,\chi)
is chosen to be the following:

\bullet

If a prime
r\nmid q
, then
\widetilde{\varphi}_{r}=\varphi_{r}
is as in §
3.2.1
.

\bullet

If
r=q
,
let
R_{q}
be the Eichler order
M_{0}(q^{2m})_{q}
of discriminant
q^{2m}
under the identification
i_{q}
as in §
2.1.2
. Let
\widetilde{\varphi}_{q}\in\pi_{q}^{R_{q}^{\times}}
be a newform.

That
\widetilde{\varphi}_{q}
is a test vector for the pair
(\pi_{q},1)
is the main result of section
7
, which is a new contribution to explicit construction of test vectors.

For a prime
r\neq q
, let
\varsigma_{r}^{(n)}
be as in §
2.3
.
If
r=q
, we choose
\varsigma_{q}
so that

R_{q}\cap\iota_{\varsigma_{q}}K_{q}=\iota_{\varsigma_{q}}O_{K_{q},q^{m}}.

Let
\theta\in K
be a unit so that
\overline{\theta}=-\theta
, where
\overline{\cdot}
denotes the action of non-trivial element in
{\mathrm{Gal}}(K/{\mathbb{Q}}_{q})
.
Let
u\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{q}^{\times}
be such that

u^{2}\theta^{2}-1\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{q}^{\times 2}.
Choose
\varsigma_{q}
such that

\iota_{\varsigma_{q}}(\theta)=\begin{pmatrix}q^{-m}&\\
&1\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}1&-u\\
&1\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}&1\\
\theta^{2}&\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}1&u\\
&1\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}q^{m}&\\
&1\end{pmatrix}.

For
s\geq v_{p}(N)
, we have

\widehat{R}\cap\iota_{\varsigma^{(s)}}\widehat{O}_{K}=\iota_{\varsigma^{(s)}}\widehat{O}_{K,p^{s}q^{m}}.

Define CM points
x_{n,q^{m}}(a)
as in §
2.3
.

For
n=\max\{1,s\}
, consider

P(\varsigma^{(n)},\widetilde{\varphi}^{\dagger},\chi):=\sum_{[a]\in{\rm G}_{n,q}}\widetilde{\varphi}^{\dagger}(x_{n,q^{m}}(a))\chi(a),

where
{\rm G}_{n,q}=K^{\times}\backslash\widehat{K}^{\times}/\widehat{O}_{K,p^{n}q^{m}}^{\times}
.

Result

Theorem 3.15
.

Let
(\pi,\chi)
be as in §
3.3.1
with
\chi\in\Xi_{p}
. Suppose that the
p
-local character associated to
\chi
is of conductor
p^{s}
with
s\geq v_{p}(N)
, and put
n=\max\{1,s\}
. Then we have

\begin{aligned} p^{-s}\cdot P(\varsigma^{(n)},\widetilde{\varphi}^{\dagger},\chi)^{2}=&\frac{\langle{\varphi,\varphi}\rangle}{8\pi^{2}(\phi,\phi)}\sqrt{|D_{K}|}\cdot L^{(p^{s}{N_{\rm{r}}})}(\frac{1}{2},\pi_{K}\otimes\chi)\\
&\cdot\frac{\epsilon(\pi)}{\epsilon(\pi_{p})}{2^{\#\Sigma_{D}}}\chi_{S^{+}\backslash\{p\}}({\mathfrak{N}}^{+})\cdot{[{\rm G}_{n,q}:{\rm G}_{n}]^{2}}\gamma_{q}\begin{cases}1,\quad&\text{$v_{p}(N)\geq 1$ or $s\geq 1$}\\
e_{p}(\pi,\chi)^{2}\alpha_{p}^{2},\quad&v_{p}(N)=0,s=0,\\
\end{cases},\end{aligned},

where
N_{\rm{r}}
is the factor of
N_{\rm{a}}^{-}
precisely divisible by the ramified primes,

\Sigma_{D}
the set of prime divisors of
(D_{K},N)
coprime to
p
,
S^{+}=\{q\ |\ q|N^{+}\}
,
N^{+}={\mathfrak{N}}^{+}\overline{{\mathfrak{N}}^{+}}
with
w|{\mathfrak{N}}^{+}
for
q=w\overline{w}
,
\chi_{T}=\prod_{t\in T}\chi_{t}
, and

\gamma_{q}:=\gamma_{\theta,u}
is as in Theorem
7.1
. Moreover, the following holds.

(a)

For given
\lambda
and
\theta
, there exists
u
such that
\gamma_{\theta,u}\neq 0
.

(b)

Let
\ell\nmid q
be a prime. Then for a
given
\theta
, there exists
u
such that

v_{\ell}((q^{2}-1)\gamma_{\theta,u})=0.

Proof.

The assertion is a consequence of Theorem
3.12
,
S
