-version of explicit Waldspurger formula
[
21
, Thm. 1.9]
, local toric period formula for newform at
q
as in Theorem
7.1
, and Corollary
7.2
.
∎

4.
p
-adic
L
-functions

We introduce

p
-adic
L
-functions interpolating Rankin–Selberg
L
-values. The main results are their constructions for general self-dual pairs (cf. Theorems
4.4
and
4.9
). In the supersingular CM inert, we also compare the associated Iwasawa invariants with that of Rubin’s
p
-adic
L
-function (cf. Proposition
4.18
).

4.1.
Theta elements

4.1.1.
Definition

Let the setting be as in §
3.1.1
.

Let
n\geq 0
be an integer. Recall that

{\rm G}_{n}=K^{\times}\backslash\widehat{K}^{\times}/\widehat{O}_{K,p^{n}}^{\times}

is the associated Picard group of
O_{K,p^{n}}
. We identify
{\rm G}_{n}
with the Galois group of the ring class field of conductor
p^{n}
over
K
via geometrically normalised reciprocity law. Denote by

[\cdot]_{n}:\widehat{K}^{\times}\to{\rm G}_{n},\,a\mapsto[a]_{n}

the natural projection map.

Let
\varphi\in\pi
be the
\ell
-optimally normalised test vector as in §
3.2.3
. For
p\nmid N
, recall that
\varphi^{\dagger}
is the stabilization of
\varphi
with respect to a root
\alpha_{p}
of the Hecke polynomial at
p
as in (
3.3
). We occasionally adopt the convention that
\alpha_{p}=1
if
p|N
.

Definition 4.1
.

The
n
-th theta element

\Theta_{n}(\pi)\in O_{\pi,\ell}[\alpha_{p}^{-1}][{\rm G}_{n}]

is defined as

\displaystyle\Theta_{n}(\pi):=\begin{cases}\displaystyle\alpha_{p}^{-n}\cdot\sum_{a\in{\rm G}_{n}}\varphi^{\dagger}(x_{n}(a))\cdot[a]_{n},&\quad p\nmid N,\\
\displaystyle\sum_{a\in{\rm G}_{n}}\varphi(x_{n}(a))\cdot[a]_{n},&\quad p|N.\end{cases}

We have the following compatibility.

Lemma 4.2
.

Suppose that
p\nmid N
, let
\pi_{n+1,n}:{\rm G}_{n+1}\to{\rm G}_{n}
be the natural quotient map. For
n\geq 1
, we have

\pi_{n+1,n}(\Theta_{n+1}(\pi))=\Theta_{n}(\pi).

Proof.

The assertion follows from

\alpha_{p}\varphi^{\dagger}(x_{n}(a))=U_{p}\varphi^{\dagger}(x_{n}(a))=\sum_{[u]_{n+1}\in\ker{\rm G}_{n+1}\rightarrow{\rm G}_{n}}\varphi^{\dagger}(x_{n+1}(ua)).

∎

4.1.2.
Interpolation

Let
\phi\in S_{2}(\Gamma_{0}(N))
be the normalised elliptic newform corresponding to
\sigma
.

Define a period
\Omega_{\pi}
by

\Omega_{\pi}:=\frac{8\pi^{2}(\phi,\phi)}{\langle{\varphi,\varphi}\rangle},

(4.1)

where
\varphi\in\pi
denotes the
\ell
-optimally normalised test vector as before.

Let
\chi\in\Xi_{p}
be with conductor
p^{s}
.
For
n\geq\max\{s,1\}
, note that

\displaystyle\chi(\Theta_{n}(\pi))=

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\displaystyle\alpha_{p}^{-n}\cdot\sum_{a\in{\rm G}_{n}}\varphi^{\dagger}(x_{n}(a))\cdot\chi(a),&\quad p\nmid N,\\
\displaystyle\sum_{a\in{\rm G}_{n}}\varphi(x_{n}(a))\cdot\chi(a),&\quad p|N.\end{cases}

Proposition 4.3
.

Let
(\pi,\chi)
be as in §
3.1.1
.
Suppose that
\chi\in\Xi_{p}
is of conductor
p^{s}
with
s\geq v_{p}(N)
.
Then for
n\geq\max\{s,1\}
, we have

\displaystyle\chi(\Theta_{n}(\pi)^{2})=

\displaystyle\sqrt{|D_{K}|}\cdot\frac{L^{(p^{s}N_{\rm{r}})}(\frac{1}{2},\pi_{K}\otimes\chi)}{\Omega_{\pi}}

\displaystyle\cdot p^{s}\chi_{S^{+}\backslash\{p\}}({\mathfrak{N}}^{+})\frac{\epsilon(\pi)}{\epsilon(\pi_{p})}{2^{\#\Sigma_{D}}}\begin{cases}1,\quad&v_{p}(N)\geq 1\\
\alpha_{p}^{-2s},\quad&s\geq 1\\
e_{p}(\pi,\chi)^{2},\quad&v_{p}(N)=0,s=0.\\
\end{cases}

Proof.

This is a simple consequence of Theorem
3.12
.
∎

4.2.
p
-adic
L
-functions: ordinary case

Theta elements readily lead to an integral
p
-adic
L
-function in the ordinary case.

In this subsection we suppose that
\ell=p\nmid 2N
. Fix embeddings
\iota_{\infty}:\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}\hookrightarrow{\mathbb{C}}
and
\iota_{p}:\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}\hookrightarrow\overline{{\mathbb{C}}}_{p}
.

Let
{\rm G}_{\infty}:=\varprojlim_{n}{\rm G}_{n}
. Let
\Gamma\simeq\mathbb{Z}_{p}
be the maximal
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-free quotient group of
{\rm G}_{\infty}
and
\Delta
the torsion subgroup of
{\rm G}_{\infty}
.
Fix a non-canonical isomorphism

{\rm G}_{\infty}\simeq\Delta\times\Gamma.

If
n\geq 1
, then

{\rm G}_{n}\simeq\Delta\times\Gamma_{n},\,\Gamma\twoheadrightarrow\Gamma_{n}:={\rm G}_{n}/\Delta.

Let
1_{\Delta}:\Delta\to\overline{\mathbb{Q}}^{\times}
be the trivial branch character. Put

O=O_{\pi,p}[\alpha_{p}],\ \Lambda=O[\![\Gamma]\!]

for
O_{\pi,p}
the completion of integer ring of the Hecke field at the prime above
p
determined via the embedding
\iota_{p}
.

Put

\Theta_{n}(\pi,1)=1_{\Delta}(\Theta_{n}(\pi))\in O[\alpha_{p}^{-1}][\Gamma_{n}]\,

and

\Theta_{\infty}(\pi)=\{\Theta_{n}(\pi)\}_{n}\in O[\alpha_{p}^{-1}][\![{{\rm G}_{\infty}}]\!];\,\quad\Theta_{\infty}(\pi,1)=\{\Theta_{n}(\pi,1)\}_{n}=1_{\Delta}(\Theta_{\infty}(\pi))\in O[\alpha_{p}^{-1}][\![\Gamma]\!].

The latter are well-defined by Lemma
4.2
. In some applications we extend
O
to contain
O_{K_{\mathfrak{p}}}
for
{\mathfrak{p}}
the prime of
K
above
p
determined via the embedding

\iota_{p}
.

If the Hecke eigenvalue
\alpha_{p}
as in (
3.3
) satisfies

v_{p}(\alpha_{p})=0,

(ord)

where
\alpha_{p}
is viewed as an element in
{\mathbb{C}}_{p}
via
\iota_{p}
, then

\Theta_{\infty}(\pi,1)\in\Lambda.

If the condition (
ord
) holds, define the
p
-adic
L
-function

\mathscr{L}_{p}(\pi)=\Theta_{\infty}(\pi,1)^{2}\in\Lambda.

To describe an interpolation property of the theta elements,
put

C(\pi,K)=\frac{\epsilon(\pi)}{\epsilon(\pi_{p})}2^{\#\Sigma_{D}}\sqrt{|D_{K}|}.

(4.2)

Theorem 4.4
.

Let
\chi\in\Xi_{p}
be of conductor
p^{s}
.
We have

\displaystyle\chi(\Theta_{\infty}(\pi,1)^{2})=\frac{L^{(p^{s}N_{\rm{r}})}(\frac{1}{2},\pi_{K}\otimes\chi)}{\Omega_{\pi}}\cdot e_{p}(\pi,\chi)^{2}p^{s}\alpha_{p}^{-2s}\cdot\chi_{S^{+}}({\mathfrak{N}}^{+})C(\pi,K).

In particular, under the condition (
ord
), the same interpolation formula holds for the
p
-adic
L
-function
\mathscr{L}_{p}(\pi)\in\Lambda
.

The result just follows from Theorem
3.12
.

4.3.
p
-adic
L
-functions: supersingular case

The section describes a construction of integral plus/minus
p
-adic
L
-functions in the supersingular case.
It builds on an idea of Pollack
[
70
]
.

4.3.1.
Setting

Recall that
p
is an odd prime split or inert in
K
.

Suppose that
a_{p}
as in (
3.3
) satisfies

a_{p}=0.

(ss)

One then has
\alpha_{p}=-\beta_{p}
.

For
\tiny{\bullet}\in\{\alpha_{p},\beta_{p}\}
,
recall that
\varphi^{\dagger}_{\tiny{\bullet}}
denotes the
p
-stabilization of
p
-optimally normalised test vector
\varphi
with respect to
\bullet
as in §
4.1.1
.
Let
\Theta_{\tiny{\bullet}}(\pi)
be the theta element

\Theta_{\tiny{\bullet}}(\pi)=\{\Theta_{n}(\varphi^{\dagger}_{\tiny{\bullet}},1)\}_{n}

associated to the pair
(\pi,1)
.

Lemma 4.5
.

For
\tiny{\bullet}\in\{\alpha_{p},\beta_{p}\}
, the theta element

\Theta_{\tiny{\bullet}}(\pi)
is a
(1/2)
-admissible distribution on
\Gamma
.

Proof.

Recall that
\varphi
is
p
-integral as in §
3.2.3

and

\tiny{\bullet}
is a square-root of
-p
.
Hence the assertion just follows from the definition of
\Theta_{\tiny{\bullet}}(\pi)
.
∎

Fix an isomorphism

\Lambda=O[\![\Gamma]\!]\simeq O[\![T]\!],\ \gamma\mapsto 1+T

for
\gamma
a topological generator of
\Gamma
. For a
p
-th power root of unity
\zeta\in\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}^{\times}
, let

\psi_{\zeta}:\Gamma\rightarrow\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}^{\times},\ \gamma\mapsto\zeta

be a character, and
\psi_{\zeta}:\Lambda\rightarrow O[\zeta]
also denote the associated homomorphism.
Let
\Xi^{+}_{p}\subset\Xi_{p}
and
\Xi^{-}_{p}\subset\Xi_{p}
be subsets of characters corresponding to
\zeta
of order
p^{t}
with t even and odd respectively.

Definition 4.6
.

Let

\log_{p}^{+}(1+T)=\frac{1}{p}\prod_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{\Phi_{p^{2n}}(1+T)}{p},\ \log_{p}^{-}(1+T)=\frac{1}{p}\prod_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{\Phi_{p^{2n-1}}(1+T)}{p}

be the half
p
-adic logarithms of Pollack
[
70
]
, where
\Phi_{p^{m}}(X)
denotes the
p^{m}
-th cyclotomic polynomial.

4.3.2.
Plus/minus
p
-adic
L
-functions

Proposition 4.7
.

Let
\pi
be as in §
3.1.1
. Suppose that the condition (
ss
) holds. Then
there exist

\Theta^{\pm}(\pi)\in\Lambda

such that

\Theta_{\pm\alpha_{p}}(\pi)=\log_{p}^{+}(1+T)\cdot\Theta^{-}(\pi)\pm\alpha_{p}\log_{p}^{-}(1+T)\cdot\Theta^{+}(\pi).

Proof.

In the following we proceed as in the proof of
[
70
, Thm. 5.6]
and
[
56
, §8]

(see also
[
26
, §2]
and
[
5
, §3]
).

Consider theta elements
\{\widetilde{\Theta}_{n}(\pi)\}_{n\geq 0}

given by

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Theta}_{n}(\pi)=\sum_{a\in{\rm G}_{n}}\varphi(x_{n}(a))\cdot[a]_{n}\in O[{\rm G}_{n}].

If
n\geq 2
, we have

\pi^{n}_{n-1}(\widetilde{\Theta}_{n}(\pi))=-\xi_{n-1}\widetilde{\Theta}_{n-2}(\pi)

for
\pi^{n}_{n-1}:O[{\rm G}_{n}]\rightarrow O[{\rm G}_{n-1}]
the projection map and

\xi_{n-1}:=\sum_{\sigma\in{\rm G}_{n-1}/{\rm G}_{n-2}}\sigma
.

Consequently,
\widetilde{\Theta}_{n}(\pi)
is divisible by the half cyclotomic polynomial
\omega_{n}^{\epsilon}
as defined in
[
56
, p. 10]
for
\epsilon
the parity of

(-1)^{n-1}
. (These are denoted by
{\rm G}_{n}^{-\epsilon}
in
[
70
, p. 544]
.)
For a fixed parity
\epsilon
of
n
, factoring out these extra zeros yields a
p
-integral norm compatible sequence

\{\widetilde{\Theta}_{n}^{\epsilon}(\pi)\in O[\Delta][\![T]\!]/(\omega_{n}^{\epsilon})\}.

Let
\Theta_{n}(\pi)\in O[\![T]\!]/(\omega_{n}^{\epsilon})
denote the image of

\{\widetilde{\Theta}_{n}^{\epsilon}(\pi)\}
under the projection
{\rm G}_{n}\twoheadrightarrow\Gamma_{n}
.

Define

\Theta^{\epsilon}(\pi)=\lim\Theta^{\epsilon}_{n}(\pi)\in O[\![T]\!]\simeq\Lambda.

In view of the construction the proof concludes.
∎

Remark 4.8
.

While the sign labelling of
\Theta_{\pm}(\pi)
is opposite to
[
70
]
, it is compatible with
[
56
]
.

Define

{\mathcal{L}}_{p}^{\pm}(\pi)=\Theta^{\pm}(\pi)^{2}.

An interpolation property:

Theorem 4.9
.

Let
\pi
be as in §
3.1.1
. Suppose that the condition (
ss
) holds.

(a)

For
\chi=\psi_{\zeta}\in\Xi_{p}^{+}
of order
p^{t}>1
and conductor
p^{s}
, we have

\displaystyle\chi({\mathcal{L}}_{p}^{+}(\pi))=

\displaystyle\frac{L^{(p^{s}N_{\rm{r}})}(\frac{1}{2},\pi_{K}\otimes\chi)}{-\Omega_{\pi}}\cdot p^{t+1}\prod_{\text{odd $m=1$}}^{t-1}\Phi_{p^{m}}(\zeta)^{-2}\cdot\chi_{S^{+}}({\mathfrak{N}}^{+})C(\pi,K)

for
C(\pi,K)
as in (
4.2
).

Moreover, if
p
is inert in
K
, then

1(\Theta^{+}(\pi))=0.

(b)

For
\chi=\psi_{\zeta}\in\Xi_{p}^{-}
of order
p^{t}
and conductor
p^{s}
, we have

\displaystyle\chi({\mathcal{L}}_{p}^{-}(\pi))=

\displaystyle\frac{L^{(p^{s}N_{\rm{r}})}(\frac{1}{2},\pi_{K}\otimes\chi)}{\Omega_{\pi}}\cdot p^{t+1}\prod_{\text{even $m=2$}}^{t-1}\Phi_{p^{m}}(\zeta)^{-2}\cdot\chi_{S^{+}}({\mathfrak{N}}^{+})C(\pi,K).

Proof.

(a)

For
\chi=\psi_{\zeta}\in\Xi_{p}^{+}
of order
p^{t}>1
,
note that

\displaystyle\psi_{\zeta}(\Theta_{\alpha_{p}}(\pi))

\displaystyle=\alpha_{p}\cdot\phi_{\zeta}(\log_{p}^{-}(1+T))\phi_{\zeta}(\Theta^{+}(\pi))

\displaystyle=\alpha_{p}\cdot\frac{1}{p}\prod_{\text{odd $m=1$}}^{t-1}\frac{\Phi_{p^{m}}(\zeta)}{p}\cdot\phi_{\zeta}(\Theta^{+}(\pi))

by
[
70
, Lem. 4.7]
.
Hence,

\psi_{\zeta}(\Theta_{\alpha_{p}}(\pi)^{2})=\frac{-1}{p^{t+1}}\cdot\prod_{\text{odd $m=1$}}^{t-1}\Phi_{p^{m}}(\zeta)^{2}\cdot\phi_{\zeta}(\Theta^{+}(\pi)^{2}).

Now the assertion just follows from Theorem
4.4
.

As for
\psi_{1}
, if
p
is inert in
K
, we have

\displaystyle 1(\Theta^{+}(\pi))=

\displaystyle-\sum_{a\in{\rm G}_{1}}\varphi(x_{1}(a))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\sum_{a\in{\rm G}_{0}}T_{p}\varphi(x_{0}(a))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0.

Here the first equality follows from definitions of
\Theta(\pi)
,
\varphi_{\pm\alpha_{p}}^{\dagger}
(cf. §
3.2.2
) and
\Theta^{+}(\pi)
(cf. Proposition
4.7
),
the second from: if
p
is inert in
K
, then we have an identity

\sum_{u\in O_{K_{p}}^{\times}/O_{K_{p},p}^{\times}}au\varsigma^{(1)}\equiv a\varsigma^{(0)}T_{p}\pmod{{\mathrm{GL}}_{2}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p})}

of Hecke operators
for
a\in\widehat{K}^{\times}
(see §
2.3
for the definition of
x_{n}(a)
), and the third just follows from (
ss
).

(b)

One may proceed as in part (a).

∎

Remark 4.10
.

(i)

The evaluation
1({\mathcal{L}}^{-}_{p}(\pi))
basically equals
L(\frac{1}{2},\pi_{K})
.
Indeed, we have

1(\Theta^{-}(\pi))=[G_{1}:G_{0}]\sum_{a\in{\rm G}_{0}}\varphi(x_{0}(a))

whose square equals algebraic part of the central
L
-value
L(\frac{1}{2},\pi_{K})
up to explicit factors by (
3.7
).

(ii)

The vanishing of
1(\Theta^{+}(\pi))
in Theorem
4.9
(a) is intertwined with direct sum decomposition of local Iwasawa cohomology groups in Rubin’s conjecture (cf.
[
14
,
15
]
). This phenomenon does not occur in the cyclotomic setting
[
56
]
.

Corollary 4.11
.

Let
\pi
be as in §
3.1.1
. Suppose that the condition (
ss
) holds.

(a)

For
\psi_{\zeta}\in\Xi_{p}^{+}
of order
p^{t}\gg 1
, we have

v_{p}\left(\frac{L^{(N_{\rm{r}})}(\frac{1}{2},\pi_{K}\otimes\chi)}{\Omega_{\pi}}\right)=\mu^{+}+\frac{2(p^{t-1}-p^{t-2}+\cdots+p-1)+\lambda^{+}}{p^{t-1}(p-1)}-(t+1).

for
\mu^{+}
and
\lambda^{+}
the Iwasawa invariants of
{\mathcal{L}}_{p}^{+}(\pi)
.

(b)

For
\psi_{\zeta}\in\Xi_{p}^{-}
of order
p^{t}\gg 1
, we have

v_{p}\left(\frac{L^{(N_{\rm{r}})}(\frac{1}{2},\pi_{K}\otimes\chi)}{\Omega_{\pi}}\right)=\mu^{-}+\frac{2(p^{t-1}-p^{t-2}+\cdots+p^{2}-p)+\lambda^{-}}{p^{t-1}(p-1)}-(t+1).

for
\mu^{-}
and
\lambda^{-}
the Iwasawa invariants of
{\mathcal{L}}_{p}^{-}(\pi)
.

Primitive
p
-adic
L
-functions

In view of Theorem
4.9
we are led to the following.

Definition 4.12
.

For
\circ\in\{+,-\}
, define

\mathscr{L}_{p}^{\circ}(\pi)=\begin{cases}(\frac{\Theta^{+}(\pi)}{T})^{2}&\text{if $p$ is inert and $\circ=+$};\\
{\mathcal{L}}_{p}^{\circ}(\pi)&\text{else}.\\
\end{cases}

We expect
\mathscr{L}_{p}^{\circ}(\pi)
to appear in signed Iwasawa main conjectures (cf.
[
26
,
14
,
5
,
6
]
).

Remark 4.13
.

For
p
inert in
K
, an interesting problem: to formulate a conjecture predicting the value of
\mathscr{L}_{p}^{+}(\pi)
at the trivial character (cf.
[
62
]
). In the CM case, it encodes
p
-adic logarithm of rational points on the associated CM abelian variety (cf.
[
15
]
).

4.4.
CM case

This section considers
p
-adic
L
-functions associated to a Hecke character over an imaginary quadratic field, and links among them.

4.4.1.
Rubin’s
p
-adic
L
-function

The following is a resume of results of
[
76
,
14
,
16
]
.

Let
K
be an imaginary quadratic field with
p
inert and

H
the Hilbert class field of
K
.
Assume that

p\nmid 6h_{K}.

(4.3)

Let
\Phi
denote the localisation of
K
at the prime ideal above
p
.

Let
K_{\infty}
be the anticyclotomic
{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
-extension of
K

and
K_{n}
the
n
-th layer. In view of (
4.3
) we often regard the set
\Xi_{p}
of anticyclotomic
p
-power order characters of
\Phi
as that of
K
.

Let
\lambda
be a self-dual Hecke character of
K
of infinity type
(1,0)
. Let
E
be a
{\mathbb{Q}}
-curve in the sense of Gross
[
33
]

such that the Hecke character
\lambda\circ{\rm{N}}_{H/K}
is associated to
E
, and
E
has good reduction at each prime of
H
above
p
.
Let
{\mathfrak{p}}
be the prime of
H
above
p
compatible with the embedding
\iota_{p}
.
Fix a Weierstrass model of
E
over
H\cap O_{H_{\mathfrak{p}}}
which is smooth at
{\mathfrak{p}}
.
By considering a Galois conjugate of
E
over
H
,
we may assume
the existence of a complex period
\Omega_{K}\in{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}

such that

L=O_{K}\Omega_{K},

where

L
is the period lattice associated to the model.

Rubin’s
p
-adic
L
-function also involves the following local setting.

Let
O_{\Phi}
be the integer ring of
\Phi
.
Let
\mathscr{F}
be a Lubin-Tate formal group over
O_{\Phi}
for
the uniformizing parameter
\pi:=-p
.
For
n\geq 0
,
write
\Phi_{n}=\Phi({\mathscr{F}}[\pi^{n+1}])
, the extension of
\Phi
in
{\mathbb{C}}_{p}
generated by the
\pi^{n+1}
-torsion points of
\mathscr{F}
.
Put
\Phi_{\infty}=\cup_{n\geq 0}\Phi_{n}

and
T=T_{\pi}{\mathscr{F}}
.

Let
\Theta_{\infty}\subset\Phi_{\infty}
be the
{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}^{2}
-extension of
\Phi
,

\Psi_{\infty}
the anticyclotomic
{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
-extension
and
\Psi_{n}
the
n
-th layer.
Put

\Gamma={\mathrm{Gal}}(\Psi_{\infty}/\Phi)\cong{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
,
\Lambda_{O_{\Phi}}=O_{\Phi}[\![\Gamma]\!]

and fix a topological generator
\gamma
of
\Gamma
.
Let
U_{n}
be the group of principal units in
\Phi_{n}
, that is,
the group of elements in
O_{\Phi_{n}}^{\times}
congruent to one modulo the maximal ideal.

Put

T^{\otimes-1}={\mathrm{Hom}}_{O_{\Phi}}(T,O_{\Phi}),\quad V_{\infty}^{*}=\left(\varprojlim_{n}U_{n}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}T^{\otimes-1}\right)^{\Delta}\otimes_{O_{\Phi}[\![{\mathrm{Gal}}(\Phi_{\infty}/\Phi)]\!]}\Lambda_{O_{\Phi}},

where
\Delta:={\mathrm{Gal}}(\Phi_{\infty}/\Theta_{\infty})

and the superscript
\Delta
refers to
\Delta
-invariants.
For a finite character
\chi
of
{\mathrm{Gal}}(\Psi_{\infty}/\Phi)
, let

\delta_{\chi}
be the associated Coates–Wiles logarithmic derivative on
V_{\infty}^{*}
.

Let
\Xi_{p}
be the set of finite characters of
\Gamma
and put

\begin{split}\Xi_{p}^{+}&={\{}\chi\in\Xi_{p}\text{ $|$}\text{ order $\chi$}\text{ is an even power of }p{\}},\\
\Xi_{p}^{-}&={\{}\chi\in\Xi_{p}\text{ $|$}\text{ order $\chi$}\text{ is an odd power of }p{\}}.\end{split}

Define

V^{*,\pm}_{\infty}:={\{}v\in V_{\infty}^{*}\text{ $|$}\text{ }\delta_{\chi}(v)=0\quad\text{for every }\chi\in\Xi_{p}^{\mp}{\}}.

(4.4)

Rubin showed that
V^{*,\pm}_{\infty}
is a free
\Lambda_{O_{\Phi}}
-module of rank one (cf.
[
76
, Prop. 8.1]
).

An insight of Rubin is the following existence of
a
p
-adic
L
-function (cf.
[
76
, §10]
,
[
14
, §6]
).

Theorem 4.14
.

Let
\varepsilon\in\{+,-\}
be the sign of the functional equation of the Hecke
L
-function

L(\lambda,s)
.
Let
v_{\varepsilon}
be a generator of the
\Lambda
-module
V_{\infty}^{*,\varepsilon}
.
Then there exists

\mathscr{L}_{p}(\lambda,\Omega,v_{\varepsilon})=:\mathscr{L}_{p}(\lambda)\in\Lambda

such that

\chi(\mathscr{L}_{p}(\lambda))=\frac{1}{\delta_{\chi}(v_{\varepsilon})}\cdot\frac{L(1,\overline{\lambda\chi})}{\Omega_{K}}

for
\chi\in\Xi_{p}^{-\varepsilon}\setminus\{1\}
.

Here the non-vanishing of
\delta_{\chi}(v_{\varepsilon})
is a consequence of Rubin’s conjecture (cf.
[
76
, Lem. 10.1]
).

The main result of
[
16
]
is the following.

Theorem 4.15
.

Let
\chi
be a finite character of
\mathrm{Gal}(\Psi_{n}/\Phi)
of order
p^{t}>1
, and put
\varepsilon=(-1)^{t-1}
.
Let
v_{\varepsilon}
be a generator of
V_{\infty}^{*,\varepsilon}
.
Then we have

v_{p}(\delta_{\chi}(v_{\varepsilon}))=-\frac{t+1}{2}+\frac{1}{p^{t-1}(p-1)}\left(\frac{1-\varepsilon}{2}+\sum_{(-1)^{k}=\varepsilon}(p^{k}-p^{k-1})\right)

where
1\leq k\leq t-1
such that
(-1)^{k}=\varepsilon
.

4.4.2.
A link with Rankin–Selberg
p
-adic
L
-function

Let the setting be as before.
In particular,
\pi_{\lambda}
denotes the irreducible cuspidal automorphic representation associated to
\lambda
.

We have a factorisation

L(1/2,\pi_{\lambda,K}\otimes\chi)=L(1,\lambda\chi)L(1,\lambda\chi^{-1})

of
L
-values.
In light of
p
-adic Artin formalism,
one may expect a factorisation

\mathscr{L}_{p}(\pi_{\lambda})=\mathscr{L}_{p}(\lambda)\mathscr{L}_{p}(\lambda)^{\iota}

(4.5)

up to an element in
\Lambda^{\times}
.
Here

\mathscr{L}_{p}(\pi_{\lambda}):=\mathscr{L}_{p}^{-\varepsilon}(\pi_{\lambda})

(4.6)

for
\varepsilon
the sign of
\epsilon(\lambda)
, and

\iota
denotes the involution of
\Lambda
arising from
\gamma\mapsto\gamma^{-1}
.
A difficulty in realising the factorisation is that interpolation formula for the Rubin
p
-adic
L
-function involves the local invariant
\delta_{\chi}(v_{\varepsilon})
and the CM period

\Omega_{K}
, whereas that for
\mathscr{L}_{p}(\pi_{\lambda})
involves a half cyclotomic polynomial and the automorphic period

\Omega_{\lambda}:=\Omega_{\pi_{\lambda}}
.

We prove a comparison of Iwasawa invariants predicted by (
4.5
). We begin with the following preliminary.

Lemma 4.16
.

For
\chi
of order
p^{t}\gg 1
so that
(-1)^{t-1}=\varepsilon
,
we have

\displaystyle v_{p}(\delta_{\chi}(v_{\varepsilon}))

\displaystyle=-\frac{t+1}{2}+\frac{1}{p^{t-1}(p-1)}\left(\frac{1-\varepsilon}{2}+\sum_{(-1)^{k}=\varepsilon}(p^{k}-p^{k-1})\right)+\frac{\lambda(\mathscr{L}_{p}(\pi_{\lambda}))-2\lambda(\mathscr{L}_{p}(\lambda))}{2p^{t-1}(p-1)}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\left(\mu(\mathscr{L}_{p}(\pi_{\lambda}))+v_{p}(\frac{\Omega_{\lambda}}{\Omega_{K}^{2}})-2\mu(\mathscr{L}_{p}(\lambda))\right).

Here
1\leq k\leq t-1
such that
(-1)^{k}=\varepsilon
,

\mu(\cdot)
and
\lambda(\cdot)
are associated Iwasawa invariants, and
\mathscr{L}_{p}(\pi_{\lambda}):=\mathscr{L}_{p}^{-\varepsilon}(\pi_{\lambda})
.

Proof.

The following is based on comparison
p
-adic valuation of
L
-values
interpolated
9
9

9

Note that the interpolated
L
-values
are generically non-zero (cf. §
5
).
by the
p
-adic
L
-functions
\mathscr{L}_{p}(\pi_{\lambda})
and
\mathscr{L}_{p}(\lambda)
.
We consider the case
\epsilon(\lambda)=-1
, and leave the other case to the interested reader.

In view of Corollary
4.11
and Definition
4.12
,
for
\psi_{\zeta}\in\Xi_{p}^{+}
of order
\chi=p^{t}\gg 1
, we have

v_{p}\left(\frac{L(\frac{1}{2},\pi_{\lambda,K}\otimes\chi)}{\Omega_{\lambda}}\right)=\mu(\mathscr{L}_{p}(\pi_{\lambda}))+\frac{2(p^{t-1}-p^{t-2}+\cdots+p-1)+\lambda(\mathscr{L}_{p}(\pi_{\lambda}))+2}{p^{t-1}(p-1)}-(t+1).

On the other hand, by Theorem
4.14
,

v_{p}\left(\frac{L(1,\lambda\chi)L(1,\lambda\chi^{-1})}{\Omega_{K}^{2}}\right)=2\mu(\mathscr{L}_{p}(\lambda))+\frac{2\lambda(\mathscr{L}_{p}(\lambda))}{p^{t-1}(p-1)}+2v_{p}(\delta_{\chi}(v_{-})).

By comparing the above two, the proof concludes.
∎

Remark 4.17
.

The left hand side of Corollary
4.16
is a local invariant, and Rubin asked for determination of its
p
-adic valuation in
[
76
]
.

Towards the factorisation (
4.5
) our main result is the following.

Proposition 4.18
.

Let
\lambda
be a self-dual Hecke character over an imaginary quadratic field
K
of infinity type
(1,0)
and
\pi_{\lambda}
the associated cuspidal automorphic representation.
For a prime
p\nmid 6h_{K}\mathrm{cond^{r}}{\lambda}
inert in
K
, let
\mathscr{L}_{p}(\lambda)
and
\mathscr{L}_{p}(\pi_{\lambda})
be the associated Rubin and Rankin–Selberg
p
-adic
L
-functions.
Then we have

\mu(\mathscr{L}_{p}(\pi_{\lambda}))+v_{p}\left(\frac{\Omega_{\lambda}}{\Omega_{K}^{2}}\right)=2\mu(\mathscr{L}_{p}(\lambda)),\,\lambda(\mathscr{L}_{p}(\pi_{\lambda}))=2\lambda(\mathscr{L}_{p}(\lambda)).

Proof.

In view of Corollary
4.16
and Theorem
4.15

it follows that

\frac{\lambda(\mathscr{L}_{p}(\pi_{\lambda}))-2\lambda(\mathscr{L}_{p}(\lambda))}{p^{t-1}(p-1)}=2\mu(\mathscr{L}_{p}(\lambda))-\mu(\mathscr{L}_{p}(\pi_{\lambda}))-v_{p}(\frac{\Omega_{\lambda}}{\Omega_{K}^{2}}).

Since the numerator is a constant, it vanishes.
∎

5.
Non-vanishing of Rankin–Selberg
L
-values modulo
\ell
: CM case

The section presents mod
\ell
non-vanishing of Rankin–Selberg
L
-values in the CM case.
The main results are
(\ell,p)
non-vanishing Theorems
5.9
,
5.10
,
5.11
and
5.14
for
\ell\neq p
, and Theorems
5.15
and
5.17
which concern
\mu
-invariants.

5.1.
Key tools

5.1.1.
Equidistribution of special points

We describe a special case of the main result of
[
24
]
, which is based on Ratner’s ergodicity of unipotent flows.

Let the setting be as in §
2.1
, where we fix a definite quaternion algebra
B
over
{\mathbb{Q}}
,
an odd prime
p
with
B_{p}
split and an embedding
\iota:K\rightarrow B
of an imaginary quadratic field.

We have the ring class group
{\rm G}_{\infty}:=\varprojlim_{n}{\rm G}_{n}
of conductor
p^{\infty}
.
Let
\Delta^{\mathrm{alg}}
be the subgroup of
{\rm G}_{\infty}
generated by the image of

K^{\times}_{\rm ram}:=\prod_{q|D_{K}}K^{\times}_{q}.

Note that
\Delta^{\mathrm{alg}}
is a
(2,\cdots,2)
-subgroup of
\Delta
. Let
D_{0}
be a set of representatives of
\Delta^{\mathrm{alg}}
in
K^{\times}_{{\mathrm{ram}}}
, and
D_{1}
that of

\Delta/\Delta^{\mathrm{alg}}
in
\widehat{K}^{\times}
. Then
D:=D_{1}D_{0}
is a set of representatives of
\Delta
in
\widehat{K}^{\times}
.

Write
\overline{K}^{\times}
for the closure of
K^{\times}
in
\widehat{K}^{\times}
and
\overline{B}^{\times}
that of
B^{\times}
in
\widehat{B}^{\times}
.
Put

{\rm{CM}}:=\overline{K}^{\times}\backslash\widehat{B}^{\times},\,\quad X:=\overline{B}^{\times}\backslash\widehat{B}^{\times},\,\quad Z:=\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{+}^{\times}\backslash\widehat{{\mathbb{Q}}}^{\times}.

The group
\widehat{B}^{\times}
acts on these spaces via right multiplication on the first two spaces and via multiplication by the norm on the third space. Similarly, there is a left action of the group
\widehat{K}^{\times}
on these spaces.
Let
{\rm{Red}}:{\rm{CM}}\to X
be the natural quotient map and
c:X\to Z
the one induced by the reduced norm
{\rm{N}}:B^{\times}\to\mathbb{Q}^{\times}
. For
g\in\widehat{B}^{\times}
, let
[g]
denote the image of
g
in
{\rm{CM}}
. Let
U
be an open compact subgroup of
\widehat{B}^{\times}
. Put

X(D_{1},U)=\prod_{\tau\in D_{1}}X/U\text{ and }Z(D_{1},U)=\prod_{\tau\in D_{1}}Z/{\rm{N}}(U).

Define

\displaystyle{\rm{Red}}_{D_{1}}:\

\displaystyle{\rm{CM}}\longrightarrow X(D_{1},U),\quad x\mapsto({\rm{Red}}(\tau\cdot x)U)_{\tau\in D_{1}}

and

\displaystyle c_{D_{1}}:\

\displaystyle X(D_{1},U)\longrightarrow Z(D_{1},U),\quad(x_{\tau})_{\tau\in D_{1}}\mapsto({\rm{N}}(x_{\tau}))_{\tau\in D_{1}}.

The following key result is a special case of
[
24
, Cor. 2.10]
.

Theorem 5.1
.

Let
{\mathcal{H}}
be a
B^{\times}_{p}
-orbit in
{\rm{CM}}
and
\overline{{\mathcal{H}}}
the image of
{\mathcal{H}}
in
{\rm{CM}}/U
. Then for all but finitely many
x\in\overline{{\mathcal{H}}}
, we have

{\rm{Red}}_{D_{1}}(\widehat{O}_{K}^{\times}\cdot x)=c_{D_{1}}^{-1}(\widehat{O}_{K}^{\times}\cdot\overline{x}),

where
\overline{x}=c_{D_{1}}\circ{\rm{Red}}_{D_{1}}(x)
.

5.1.2.
Non-Eisenstein functions: generalities

Let
A
be a commutative
\mathbb{Z}
-algebra.

Let
U
be an open compact subgroup of
\widehat{B}^{\times}
. Recall that
M_{2}(U,A)
is the set of functions
h:B^{\times}\widehat{{\mathbb{Q}}}^{\times}\backslash\widehat{B}^{\times}\to A
such that
h
is right invariant by
U
. Let

M_{2}(A):=\varinjlim_{U\subset\widehat{B}^{\times}}M_{2}(U,A)

be the space of smooth
A
-valued functions on
B^{\times}\widehat{{\mathbb{Q}}}^{\times}\backslash\widehat{B}^{\times}
. Let
\rho:\widehat{B}^{\times}\to{\mathrm{Aut}}(M_{2}(A))
denote the right translation of
\widehat{B}^{\times}
.

Definition 5.2
.

Let
B^{1}=\{g\in B^{\times}\mid{\rm{N}}(g)=1\}
be the algebraic group over
\mathbb{Q}
. Let

M_{2}(A)_{{\mathrm{Eis}}}:=\{h\in M_{2}(A)\mid\rho(g_{1})h=h\text{ for all $g_{1}\in B^{1}({\mathbb{A}}_{f})$}\}

and

S_{2}(A):=M_{2}(A)/M_{2}(A)_{{\mathrm{Eis}}}.

Denote by
S_{2}(U,A)
the image of
M_{2}(U,A)
in
S_{2}(A)
.

A function
h\in M_{2}(A)
is called Eisenstein if
h\in M_{2}(A)_{{\mathrm{Eis}}}
. Equivalently,
h
is Eisenstein if and only if

h(g)=h_{1}({\rm{N}}(g))
for a smooth function
h_{1}:\mathbb{Q}_{+}^{\times}\backslash\widehat{\mathbb{Q}}^{\times}\to A
.

The following properties of non-Eisenstein functions will be used in our non-vanishing arguments.

Lemma 5.3
.

Let
q
be a finite place such that
B_{q}
is split and
U_{q}=U_{0}(q^{k})_{q}
for some
k
. For
\beta_{1},\cdots,\beta_{s}\in A
, define
{\mathcal{R}}\in{\mathrm{End}}(M_{2}(A))
by

{\mathcal{R}}=1+\sum_{i=1}^{s}\beta_{i}\cdot\rho(\begin{pmatrix}q^{-i}&\\
&1\\
\end{pmatrix}).

Then
{\mathcal{R}}:{\mathcal{S}}_{2}(U,A)\to{\mathcal{S}}_{2}(A)
is injective (cf.
[
23
, Lem. 5.5]
).

In the following lemma, let
U=\widehat{R}^{\times}
for an order
R
of
B
and
q
a prime such that
B_{q}
is a split quaternion algebra. Let
K_{q}\subset B_{q}
be a quadratic subalgebra.
Let
A
be the ring of integers of a finite extension of
{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}
and
\varpi
a uniformizer of
A
.

Lemma 5.4
.

Let
\pi
be a cuspidal automorphic representation and
pick a non-zero
f\in M_{2}(U,A)[\pi]
.
For a prime
q
, suppose that
K_{q}
is a field and
B_{q}
splits.
Moreover, for
f_{q}
the newform, suppose that

\gamma:=\int_{K_{q}^{\times}/{\mathbb{Q}}_{q}^{\times}V_{q}}\frac{(\pi(t)f_{q},f_{q})_{q}}{(f_{q},f_{q})_{q}}d^{\times}t

is an
\ell
-adic unit, where
(\ ,\ )_{q}
is a non-degenerate
{\mathrm{GL}}_{2}({\mathbb{Q}}_{q})
-invariant bilinear pairing on
\pi_{q}
and
V_{q}=K_{q}^{\times}\cap U_{q}
.

Assume that
\ell\nmid q(q^{2}-1)
.
Then if
f\pmod{\varpi}
is non-zero, so is

F:=\sum_{t\in K_{q}^{\times}/{\mathbb{Q}}_{q}^{\times}V_{q}}\pi(t)f\pmod{\varpi}.

Proof.

Let
R_{q}^{\prime}\subset R_{q}
be a suborder such that
R_{q}^{\times}
stabilizes
F
. Put

F^{\prime}=\sum_{g\in R_{q}^{\times}/R_{q}^{\prime\times}}\pi(g)F\in\pi^{\widehat{R}^{\times}}.

If
F^{\prime}
is non-zero modulo
\varpi
, then
so is
F
.
In the following, we consider
F^{\prime}
.

Note that
F^{\prime}\in{\mathbb{C}}f
and so
F^{\prime}=\kappa f
for a constant
\kappa
.
Let
\langle{\ ,\ }\rangle
be a
B_{{\mathbb{A}}}^{\times}
-invariant bilinear pairing on
\pi
. We have

\displaystyle\kappa=

\displaystyle\frac{\langle{F^{\prime},f}\rangle}{\langle{f,f}\rangle}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\#R_{q}^{\times}/R_{q}^{\prime\times}\cdot\frac{\langle{F,f}\rangle}{\langle{f,f}\rangle}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\#R_{q}^{\times}/R_{q}^{\prime\times}\cdot\gamma.

Here the last equality follows from the uniqueness of
B_{{\mathbb{A}}}^{\times}
-invariant bilinear pairing on
\pi
up to scalars (cf.
[
52
, Lem. 2.6]
). Note that
\#{\mathrm{GL}}_{2}({\mathbb{Z}}_{q})/(1+q^{n}M_{2}({\mathbb{Z}}_{q}))
is an
\ell
-adic unit since
\ell\nmid q(q^{2}-1)
and in turn so is
\#R_{q}^{\times}/R_{q}^{\prime\times}
.
Since
\gamma
is an
\ell
-adic unit and
f\equiv\hskip-10.0pt/\ 0\pmod{\varpi}
, the proof concludes.
∎

5.1.3.
Non-Eisenstein functions: CM case

This endoscopic case exhibits peculiar features, which the subsection describes.

We begin with the setting.
Let
K\subset B
be an imaginary quadratic field. Let
U
be an open compact subgroup of
\widehat{B}^{\times}
and
X_{U}:=B^{\times}\backslash\widehat{B}^{\times}/U
the associated Shimura set.

If
U
is of the form
\widehat{R}^{\times}
for an order
R
of
B
with
{\rm{N}}(U)\subset{\mathbb{Q}}_{+}^{\times}{\rm{N}}(\widehat{K}^{\times})
, then we may write

X_{U}=X_{U}^{+}\sqcup X_{U}^{-},

where

X_{U}^{+}:=\{[h]\in X_{U}\ \Big|\ {\rm{N}}(h)\in{\mathbb{Q}}^{\times}_{+}\backslash{\mathbb{Q}}_{+}^{\times}{\rm{N}}(\widehat{K}^{\times})/{\rm{N}}(U)\}.

Let
A
be the ring of integers of some finite extension of
{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}
and
\varpi
a uniformizer of
A
.
For
f\in M_{2}(U,A)
, let
f^{\epsilon}
denote its restriction to
X^{\epsilon}_{U}

for
\epsilon\in\{\pm\}
.

Henceforth, we suppose that
U
is of the form
\widehat{R}^{\times}
such that

{\rm{N}}(U)\subset{\mathbb{Q}}_{+}^{\times}{\rm{N}}(\widehat{K}^{\times}).

(5.1)

Then restricting to
X^{\epsilon}_{U}
, we define spaces of non-Eisenstein forms
S_{2}(U,A)^{\epsilon}
and
S_{2}(A)^{\epsilon}
.

Definition 5.5
.

We say
f\in M_{2}(U,A)
has CM by
K
if

T_{q}f=a_{q}f

for all but finitely many primes
q
and
a_{q}
the Hecke eigenvalue of the theta series associated to a self-dual Hecke character
\lambda
over
K
of infinity type
(1,0)
.

Lemma 5.6
.

Suppose that

\bullet

\ell\nmid 2D_{K}
,

\bullet

f\in M_{2}(U,A)
has CM by
K
,

\bullet

f^{\epsilon}\pmod{\varpi}
is non-zero.

Then
f^{\epsilon}\pmod{\varpi}
is non-Eisenstein.

Proof.

Pick a prime
q
so that

\circ

\ell\nmid q+1
,

\circ

q
is inert in
K
,

\circ

f
is
T_{q}
-eigen.

In view of the first two hypotheses, such a
q
exists.
Note that the
T_{q}
-eigenvalue is
0

since
f
has CM by
K
.

Now assume that

f^{\epsilon}\pmod{\varpi}
is Eisenstein.

By the hypothesis,
f^{\epsilon}(z)\equiv\hskip-10.0pt/\ 0\pmod{\varpi}
for some
z\in X_{U}^{\epsilon}
. Then we consider
T_{q}f\left(z\begin{pmatrix}1&\\
&q^{-1}\end{pmatrix}\right)
. Note that

0\equiv T_{q}f(z\begin{pmatrix}1&\\
&q^{-1}\end{pmatrix})\equiv(q+1)f^{\epsilon}(z)\equiv\hskip-10.0pt/\ 0\pmod{\varpi},

where the congruence
T_{q}f(z\begin{pmatrix}1&\\
&q^{-1}\end{pmatrix})\equiv(q+1)f^{\epsilon}(z)\pmod{\varpi}
just follows from
T_{q}=\sum_{i=1}^{q+1}u_{q}
for
u_{q}\in{\mathrm{GL}}_{2}({\mathbb{Q}})
with
{\rm{N}}(u_{q})=q
, and
f^{\epsilon}\pmod{\varpi}
being Eisenstein.
A contradiction.
∎

Lemma 5.7
.

Suppose that
{\rm{N}}(U)\subset{\mathbb{Q}}_{+}^{\times}{\rm{N}}(\widehat{K}^{\times})
.
Let
q
be a prime unramified in
K
such that
U_{q}=U_{0}(q^{k})_{q}
for some
k
. For a commutative

{\mathbb{Z}}
-algebra
A
, let
\beta_{1},\cdots,\beta_{s}\in A
and
{\mathcal{R}}\in{\mathrm{End}}(M_{2}(A))^{\epsilon}
be the endomorphism defined
10
10

10

Since
q\nmid D_{K}
, it is well defined.
by

{\mathcal{R}}=1+\sum_{i=1}^{s}\beta_{i}\cdot\rho(\begin{pmatrix}q^{-2i}&\\
&1\\
\end{pmatrix}).

Then
{\mathcal{R}}:{\mathcal{S}}_{2}(U,A)^{\epsilon}\to{\mathcal{S}}_{2}(A)^{\epsilon}
is injective. Moreover, if
q
splits in
K
, then the same holds when
2i
in the definition of
{\mathcal{R}}
is replaced with
i
.

Proof.

Let
f^{\epsilon}\in{\mathcal{S}}_{2}(U,A)^{\epsilon}
be so that
{\mathcal{R}}f^{\epsilon}\in M_{2}(A)^{\epsilon}_{\text{Eis}}
. In the following we show
f^{\epsilon}\in M_{2}(U,A)_{\text{Eis}}^{\epsilon}
. (See also the proof of
[
23
, Lem. 5.5]
.)

Note that
N({\mathbb{Z}}_{q})\subset{\mathrm{Stab}}(f^{\epsilon})
for
N({\mathbb{Z}}_{q})
the subgroup of upper triangular matrices. Put

u=\begin{pmatrix}q^{-1}&\\
&1\end{pmatrix}

and

P(u)=\sum_{i=1}^{s}\beta_{i}\cdot\rho(u^{2i-2}).

Then
P\in{\mathrm{End}}(M_{2}(A)^{\epsilon})
.
By the assumption, we have
(1-\rho(u^{2})P(u))f^{\epsilon}\in M_{2}(A)^{\epsilon}_{\text{Eis}}
, and so

(1-\rho(u^{2n})P(u)^{n})f^{\epsilon}\in M_{2}(A)^{\epsilon}_{\text{Eis}}

for any
n\geq 1
.

Note that
(1-\rho(u^{2n})P(u)^{n})f^{\epsilon}
and
\rho(u^{2n})P(u)^{n}f^{\epsilon}
are fixed by
\begin{pmatrix}1&x\\
&1\end{pmatrix}
for
x\in{\mathbb{Q}}_{q}
with
q^{2n}x\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{q}
since
u^{-2n}\begin{pmatrix}1&x\\
&1\end{pmatrix}u^{2n}=\begin{pmatrix}1&q^{2n}x\\
&1\end{pmatrix}
. Thus
f^{\epsilon}
is fixed by
\begin{pmatrix}1&x\\
&1\end{pmatrix}
for all
x\in{\mathbb{Q}}_{q}
. By smoothness, the same holds for
\begin{pmatrix}1&\\
y&1\end{pmatrix}
for some
y\in{\mathbb{Q}}_{q}^{\times}
. Therefore,
f^{\epsilon}
is fixed by
w_{0}=\begin{pmatrix}&y^{-1}\\
-y&\end{pmatrix}
,
and in turn by

\begin{pmatrix}1&\\
x&1\end{pmatrix}=w_{0}\begin{pmatrix}1&-y^{2}x\\
&1\end{pmatrix}w_{0}^{-1}.

Hence
{\mathrm{SL}}_{2}({\mathbb{Q}}_{q})
fixes
f^{\epsilon}
and
f^{\epsilon}(tg)=f^{\epsilon}(g)
for all
t\in B^{1}({\mathbb{Q}}_{q})
. By strong approximation,
B^{1}
is dense in
B^{1}({\mathbb{A}}_{f}^{(q)})
, thus
f^{\epsilon}(tg)=f^{\epsilon}(g)
for all
t\in B^{1}({\mathbb{A}}_{f}^{(q)})
. It follows that
f^{\epsilon}(tg)=f^{\epsilon}(g)
for all
t\in B^{1}({\mathbb{A}}_{f})
and hence
f^{\epsilon}(gt)=f^{\epsilon}(g)
for all
t\in B^{1}({\mathbb{A}})
, concluding the proof.
∎

5.1.4.
An independence of CM values

The following consequence of equidistribution of special points will be a key to our non-vanishing results.

Let
x_{n}(a)
be a family of special points for
a\in\widehat{K}^{\times}
as in §
2.3.3
.

Proposition 5.8
.

Let
A
be the ring of integers of a finite extension of
{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}
and
\varpi
a uniformizer.
Let
(\beta_{\tau})_{\tau\in D_{1}}
be a sequence in
A
such that
\beta_{\tau_{1}}\in A^{\times}
for some
\tau_{1}
. Let
f\in M_{2}(U,A)
be a CM form of level
U
as in (
5.1
). Assume that

f
is non-zero modulo
\varpi
.

(a)

Suppose that
p
is inert in
K
. Then there exists an integer
n_{0}
such that for every
n>n_{0}
of a fixed parity, we have

\sum_{\tau\in D_{1}}\beta_{\tau}\cdot f(x_{n}(a\tau))\equiv\hskip-10.0pt/\ 0{\pmod{\varpi}}\text{ for some }a\in\widehat{K}^{\times}.

(b)

Suppose that
p
splits in
K
, and that
f
is non-zero on
X_{U}^{+}
.
Then there exists an integer
n_{0}
such that for every
n>n_{0}
, we have

\sum_{\tau\in D_{1}}\beta_{\tau}\cdot f(x_{n}(a\tau))\equiv\hskip-10.0pt/\ 0\pmod{\varpi}\text{ for some }a\in\widehat{K}^{\times}.

Proof.

Consider a special point

P_{0}:=[\varsigma^{(0)}]\in{\rm{CM}}

for
\varsigma^{(0)}
as in §
2.3
.
Note that
{\rm{N}}(\varsigma^{(0)})\in{\mathbb{Q}}_{+}^{\times}\backslash{\mathbb{Q}}_{+}^{\times}{\rm{N}}(\widehat{K}^{\times})
. Let
{\mathcal{H}}=P_{0}\cdot B^{\times}_{p}
be the
B^{\times}_{p}
-orbit of
P_{0}
. For integers
n\geq 1
, put

P_{n}:=P_{0}\begin{pmatrix}p^{n}&\\
&1\\
\end{pmatrix}\in{\mathcal{H}}.

(5.2)

Note that the images of
(P_{n})_{n=1,2,\cdots}
are distinct in
X_{U}
.
Hence, by Theorem
5.1
, there exists
n_{0}
such that

{\rm{Red}}_{D_{1}}(\widehat{O}_{K}^{\times}P_{n})=c_{D_{1}}^{-1}(\widehat{O}_{K}^{\times}\overline{P_{n}})\text{ for every }n>n_{0}.

(5.3)

Since
f\pmod{\varpi}
is non-Eisenstein by Lemma
5.6
, there exist
y,z\in X_{U}
such that

f(y)\equiv\hskip-10.0pt/\ f(z)\pmod{\varpi}

and
c(y)=c(z)
.

(a)

Let
n\geq 1
be an integer. Note that

c({\rm Red}(P_{n}))=c({\rm Red}(P_{n+2}))\pmod{{\rm{N}}(\widehat{K}^{\times}){\rm{N}}(U)}
, and

c(y)=c(z)\neq c({\rm Red}(P_{n}))\pmod{{\rm{N}}(\widehat{K}^{\times}){\rm{N}}(U)}\implies c(y)=c(z)=c({\rm Red}(P_{n+1}))\pmod{{\rm{N}}(\widehat{K}^{\times}){\rm{N}}(U)}

since
p
in inert in
K
. It follows that

c(y)=c(z)=c(P_{n})\pmod{{\rm{N}}(\widehat{K}^{\times}){\rm{N}}(U)}
for
n
of a fixed parity.

In the following we consider
n>n_{0}
of that parity.

Replacing
D_{1}
by
a^{\prime}D_{1}
for
a^{\prime}\in\widehat{K}^{\times}
, we may assume that

c(y)=c(z)=c(P_{n})\pmod{{\rm{N}}(U)}.

Pick
(w_{\tau})_{\tau\in D_{1}}\in c_{D_{1}}^{-1}(\overline{P}_{n})
.
In view of (
5.3
) there exist
a_{1},a_{2}\in\widehat{O}_{K}^{\times}
such that

{\rm Red}_{D_{1}}(a_{1}P_{n})=(y,w_{\tau_{2}},w_{\tau_{3}},\cdots),\,{\rm Red}_{D_{1}}(a_{2}P_{n})=(z,w_{\tau_{2}},w_{\tau_{3}},\cdots).

Hence

\sum_{\tau\in D_{1}}\beta_{\tau}\cdot f(x_{n}(a_{1}\tau))-\sum_{\tau\in D_{1}}\beta_{\tau}\cdot f(x_{n}(a_{2}\tau)){\equiv}\beta_{\tau_{1}}(f(y)-f(z))\equiv\hskip-10.0pt/\ 0\pmod{\varpi},

and the assertion follows.

(b)

Since
p
splits in
K
, we have

c({\rm Red}(P_{n}))=c({\rm Red}(P_{n+1}))\pmod{{\rm{N}}(\widehat{K}^{\times}){\rm{N}}(U)}
.

In view of the assumption we may suppose that

c(y)=c(z)=c({\rm{Red}}(P_{n}))\pmod{{\rm{N}}(U)}

and
f(y)\equiv\hskip-10.0pt/\ f(z)\pmod{\varpi}
. Then the assertion follows just as in the proof of part (a).

∎

5.2.
Setting

We introduce set-up for the rest of the section.

Let
\lambda
be a self-dual Hecke character over
K
of infinity type
(1,0)
and
\phi\in S_{2}(\Gamma_{0}(N))
the associated theta series with
N=D_{K}{\rm N}_{K/{\mathbb{Q}}}(\mathrm{cond^{r}}\lambda)
. Let
B
the quaternion algebra over
{\mathbb{Q}}
so that

D_{B}=\prod_{\eta_{K_{q}}(-1)=-1}q

(cf. Lemma
3.2
).
Let
\pi_{\lambda}
be the cuspidal automorphic representation of
B_{{\mathbb{A}}}^{\times}
associated to
\phi_{\lambda}
with conductor
N
.

Let
\ell\nmid 2N
and
p\nmid 2D_{B}
be primes.
Let
\varphi,\varphi^{\{p\}}\in\pi_{\lambda}
be the
\ell
-optimally normalised test vectors in the cases when

p\nmid N^{-}
,
p|N^{-}
respectively as in §
3.2.3
.
We have the associated periods

\begin{cases}\Omega_{\lambda}=\frac{8\pi^{2}(\phi,\phi)}{\langle{\varphi,\varphi}\rangle},&\quad p\nmid N^{-},\\
\Omega_{\lambda}^{\{p\}}=\frac{8\pi^{2}(\phi,\phi)}{\langle{\varphi^{\{p\}},\varphi^{\{p\}}}\rangle},&\quad p|N^{-}.\\
\end{cases}

The first period does not depends on the choice of
p
.

In the following subsections we study
\ell
-indivisibility of the
L
-value
L^{\text{alg}}(1/2,\pi_{\lambda,K}\otimes\chi)

via studying
\ell
-divisibility of toric periods.

5.3.
(\ell,p)
non-vanishing

This subsection concerns the case
\ell\neq p
.

5.3.1.
Inert case

Let
p
be an odd prime inert in
K
.

As before, let
K_{\infty}
be the anticyclotomic
{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
-extension of
K
,
\Gamma={\mathrm{Gal}}(K_{\infty}/K)
, and

\Xi_{p}
the set of finite order characters of
\Gamma
. For
\nu\in\Xi_{p}
, the pairs
(\pi_{\lambda},\nu)
are self-dual with root number
+1
.

Put

\Xi_{\lambda,p}^{\pm}=\{\nu\in\Xi_{p}|\,\epsilon(\lambda\nu)=\pm 1\},

where
\epsilon(\lambda\nu)
denotes the global root number.
One may consider non-vanishing of central
L
-values
L^{\text{alg}}(1/2,\pi_{\lambda,K}\otimes\chi)
modulo
\ell
for
\nu\in\Xi_{\lambda,p}^{+}
, where
\ell
is a fixed prime.

Case I.
p\nmid 2{\rm{N}}_{K/{\mathbb{Q}}}(\mathrm{cond^{r}}{\lambda})

For
\nu\in\Xi_{p}
, we have

\epsilon(\lambda\nu)=(-1)^{t}\epsilon(\lambda),

(5.4)

where the associated local character
\nu_{p}
is of conductor
p^{t}>1
(cf.
[
63
, Prop. 3.7]
).

Our main result is the following.

Theorem 5.9
.

Let
\lambda
be a self-dual Hecke character over
K
of infinity type
(1,0)
and
\pi_{\lambda}
the associated cuspidal automorphic representation. Let
p\nmid 2{\rm{N}}_{K/{\mathbb{Q}}}(\mathrm{cond^{r}}{\lambda})
be a prime inert in
K
.
Let
\ell\nmid 2p{\rm{N}}_{K/{\mathbb{Q}}}(\mathrm{cond^{r}}{\lambda})
be a prime.
Then for all but finitely many
\nu\in\Xi_{\lambda,p}^{+}
, we have

v_{\ell}\left(\frac{L(1/2,\pi_{\lambda,K}\otimes\nu)}{\Omega_{\lambda}}\right)=0.

Proof.

Choose a finite extension
O
of
\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}
in
{\mathbb{C}}_{\ell}
so that
O
contains
O_{\pi_{\lambda},\ell}
and
\alpha_{p}

and let
\varpi
be a uniformizer of
O
.

Let
F_{\ell}
be the
\ell
-adic avatar of the
p
-stabilization
\varphi^{\dagger}
of
\varphi
with respect to
\alpha_{p}
. For
R
the order in the definition of
\varphi
, let
U=\widehat{R}^{\times}
, we have
\varphi\in M_{2}(U,O)
. Put

U^{\prime}=\widehat{R}^{\times,(p)}{U_{0}(p)_{p}},

where
R
is the order in the definition of
\varphi
.
Then
F_{\ell}\pmod{\varpi}\in M_{2}(U^{\prime},{\bf k}_{\ell})
for
{\bf k}_{\ell}:=O/\varpi{O}
.

For each integer
n\geq 0
, put

\Theta_{n}:=\sum_{[a]_{n}\in{\rm G}_{n}}F_{\ell}(x_{n}(a))\cdot[a]_{n}\in O[{\rm G}_{n}].

In view of explicit Waldspurger formula (cf. Theorem
3.12
), it suffices to show that

v_{\ell}(\nu(\Theta_{n}))\neq 0

for all but finitely many
\nu\in\Xi_{\lambda,p}^{+}
.

Recall that
{\rm G}_{n}=(D_{1}\times D_{0})\cdot\Gamma_{n}
. Note that elements in
D_{0}
are represented by product of uniformizers of
K_{q}
for
q|D_{K}
, it follows from the definition of
\varphi
that

\sum_{d\in D_{0}}\pi(\iota(d))F_{\ell}=|D_{0}|\cdot F_{\ell}.

(5.5)

Let
p^{s}
be the order of the Sylow
p
-subgroup of
{\bf k}_{\ell}^{\times}
. Let
\nu:\Gamma_{n}\to\mu_{p^{\infty}}
be a character of conductor
p^{n}
with
n>2s
. Put

C_{n}=\{\gamma\in\Gamma_{n}\mid\nu(\gamma)\in{\bf k}_{\ell}^{\times}\}.

Note that
C_{n}={\mathrm{Ker}}({\rm G}_{n}\to{\rm G}_{n-s})
. Let
{\bf k}_{\ell}(\nu)
be the field generated by the values of
\nu
over
{\bf k}_{\ell}
. Since
{\bf k}_{\ell}
contains
\mu_{p}
,
d_{\nu}:=[{\bf k}_{\ell}(\nu):{\bf k}_{\ell}]
is a
p
-power, and for a
p
-power root of unity
\zeta\in{\bf k}_{\ell}(\nu)
, we have

{\rm Tr}_{{\bf k}_{\ell}(\nu)/{\bf k}_{\ell}}(\zeta)=\begin{cases}0&\cdots\zeta\not\in{\bf k}_{\ell},\\
d_{\nu}&\cdots\zeta\in{\bf k}_{\ell}.\end{cases}

It follows from the above that for each
a\in\widehat{K}^{\times}
,

\displaystyle{\rm Tr}_{{\bf k}_{\ell}(\nu)/{\bf k}_{\ell}}(\nu(a^{-1})\cdot\nu(\Theta_{n})\pmod{\varpi}){\equiv}

\displaystyle|D_{0}|d_{\nu}\cdot\sum_{\tau\in D_{1}}\sum_{y\in\mathbb{Z}/p^{s}\mathbb{Z}}F_{\ell}(x_{n}(a\tau)\begin{pmatrix}1&\frac{y}{p^{s}}\\
0&1\\
\end{pmatrix}\zeta_{\nu}^{y}{\pmod{\varpi}}

(5.6)

for a primitive
p^{s}
-th root of unity
\zeta_{\nu}
.

Define
\widetilde{F}_{\ell}\in M_{2}({\bf k}_{\ell}(\nu))
by

\widetilde{F}_{\ell}(g):=\sum_{y\in\mathbb{Z}/p^{s}\mathbb{Z}}\zeta_{\nu}^{y}\rho(\begin{pmatrix}1&\frac{y}{p^{s}}\\
0&1\\
\end{pmatrix})F_{\ell}(g)\pmod{\varpi}.

(5.7)

Then
\widetilde{F}_{\ell}\in M_{2}(\widetilde{U}^{\prime},{\bf k}_{\ell}(\nu))
for

\widetilde{U}^{\prime}=\{g\in U^{\prime}\ |\ g_{p}\equiv\begin{pmatrix}1&*\\
0&1\end{pmatrix}\pmod{p^{2s}}\}
.

By (
5.6
), we have

{\rm Tr}_{{\bf k}_{\ell}(\nu)/{\bf k}_{\ell}}(\nu(a^{-1})\cdot\nu(\Theta_{n})\pmod{\varpi})=|D_{0}|d_{\nu}\cdot\sum_{\tau\in D_{1}}\widetilde{F}_{\ell}(x_{n}(a\tau)).

(5.8)

We next show that
\widetilde{F}_{\ell}
is non-Eisenstein.

Note that (
5.1
) holds by Lemma
3.11
.
Under our assumptions,
F_{\ell}\pmod{\varpi}
is non-Eisenstein by Lemmas
5.3
and
5.6
. A simple calculation shows that

\displaystyle\sum_{a\in(\mathbb{Z}/p^{s}\mathbb{Z})^{\times}}\rho\begin{pmatrix}a&\\
&1\\
\end{pmatrix}\widetilde{F}_{\ell}{\equiv}p^{s}\cdot(1-p^{-1}\alpha_{p}\cdot\rho\left(\begin{pmatrix}p^{-1}&\\
&1\\
\end{pmatrix}\right)F_{\ell}{\pmod{\varpi}}.

Therefore
\widetilde{F}_{\ell}
is non-Eisenstein by Lemma
5.3
.

In view of (
5.8
) and Proposition
5.8
, it thus follows that

v_{\ell}(\nu(\Theta_{n}))\neq 0

for all but finitely many
\nu\in\Xi_{\lambda,p}^{{\varepsilon_{0}}}
, where
{\varepsilon_{0}}
denotes the sign of
(-1)^{n}\epsilon(\lambda)
for
n
the parity arising from Proposition
5.8
(cf. (
5.4
)).

If
\varepsilon_{0}\neq+
, then
L(1,\lambda\nu)\neq 0
for all but finitely many

\nu\in\Xi_{\lambda,p}^{-}
. But the latter
L
-value vanish since
\epsilon(\lambda\nu)=-1
for any

\nu\in\Xi_{\lambda,p}^{-}
. Thus we have
\varepsilon_{0}=+
and the parity
n
in Proposition
5.8
satisfies
(-1)^{n}\epsilon(\lambda)=+1
. Moreover,
\widetilde{F}_{\ell}^{{\mathrm{sgn}}(\epsilon(\lambda))}
is non-Eisenstein.
∎

Case II.
p|{\rm{N}}_{K/{\mathbb{Q}}}(\mathrm{cond^{r}}{\lambda})

Let
p^{m}
be the conductor of
\lambda_{p}
with
m>0
.
By
[
63
, Prop. 3.7]
,
for
\nu\in\Xi_{p}
with
\mathrm{cond^{r}}{\nu_{p}}=p^{n}>p^{m}
, we have

\epsilon(\lambda\nu)=(-1)^{n-m}\epsilon(\lambda).

Our main result is the following.

Theorem 5.10
.

Let
\lambda
be a self-dual Hecke character over
K
of infinity type
(1,0)
and
\pi_{\lambda}
the associated cuspidal automorphic representation.
Let
p\mid{\rm{N}}_{K/{\mathbb{Q}}}(\mathrm{cond^{r}}{\lambda})
be an odd prime inert in
K
.
Let
\ell\nmid 2p{\rm{N}}_{K/{\mathbb{Q}}}(\mathrm{cond^{r}}{\lambda})
be a prime.
Then for all but finitely many
\nu\in\Xi_{\lambda,p}^{+}
, we have

v_{\ell}\left(\frac{L(1/2,\pi_{\lambda,K}\otimes\nu)}{\Omega_{\lambda}^{\{p\}}}\right)=0.

Proof.

The proof is essentially the same as in the Case I, except we consider

F_{\ell}
the
\ell
-adic avatar of the test vector
\varphi^{\{p\}}
.

Let
\nu:\Gamma_{n}\to\mu_{p^{\infty}}
be a character of conductor
p^{n}
with
n>\max\{2s,{2m}\}
.

Define
\widetilde{F}_{\ell}
as in the proof of Theorem
5.9
. Then
\widetilde{F}_{\ell}\in M_{2}(\widetilde{U}^{\prime},{\bf k}_{\ell}(\nu))
for

\widetilde{U}^{\prime}=\{g\in U^{\prime}\ |\ g_{p}\equiv\begin{pmatrix}1&*\\
0&1\end{pmatrix}\pmod{p^{\max\{{2m},2s\}}}\}.

It suffices to show that
\widetilde{F}_{\ell}
is non-Eisenstein.

Note that
F_{\ell}\pmod{\varpi}
is non-Eisenstein by Lemma
5.6
and (
5.5
).
We have

\displaystyle\sum_{a\in(\mathbb{Z}/p^{s}\mathbb{Z})^{\times}}\rho\begin{pmatrix}a&\\
&1\\
\end{pmatrix}\widetilde{F}_{\ell}{\equiv}

\displaystyle p^{s}F_{\ell}-{p^{s-1}}\rho\left(\begin{pmatrix}p^{-1}&\\
&1\end{pmatrix}\right)U_{p}F_{\ell}\pmod{\varpi}

\displaystyle{\equiv}

\displaystyle p^{s}F_{\ell}\pmod{\varpi}

since the
U_{p}
-eigenvalue of a newform is
0
whenever
p^{2}
divides the conductor of
\pi_{\lambda}
.
Therefore
\widetilde{F}_{\ell}
is non-Eisenstein.

∎

Variant
.

Let
p
and
\ell
as before.
Fix an odd prime
p_{0}\neq p\ell
inert in
K
such that
p_{0}^{2}|\mathrm{cond^{r}}\lambda
.

Take
\widetilde{\varphi}
to be the
\ell
-optimally normalised test vector as in §
3.3.3

with
p_{0}=q
therein.
Recall that
\widetilde{\varphi}
is new at
*
and
p_{0}
, where

*=\begin{cases}p,&\quad p|N^{-},\\
\emptyset,&\quad p\nmid N^{-}.\end{cases}

For
p\nmid N^{-}
, note that
\widetilde{\varphi}
does not depend on
p
. We denote
\widetilde{\varphi}
by
\begin{cases}\varphi^{\{p_{0}\}},&\quad p\nmid N^{-}\\
\varphi^{\{p,p_{0}\}},&\quad p\mid N^{-}\end{cases}
to emphasise the dependence.
Define periods

\begin{cases}\Omega_{\lambda}^{\{p_{0}\}}=\frac{8\pi^{2}(\phi,\phi)}{\langle{\varphi^{\{p_{0}\}},\varphi^{\{p_{0}\}}}\rangle},&\quad p\nmid N^{-},\\
\Omega_{\lambda}^{\{p,p_{0}\}}=\frac{8\pi^{2}(\phi,\phi)}{\langle{\varphi^{\{p,p_{0}\}},\varphi^{\{p,p_{0}\}}}\rangle},&\quad p\nmid N^{-}.\end{cases}

The following result will be used in section
6
to connect the mod
\ell
non-vanishing of Hecke
L
-values with an
\ell
-integral comparison of periods (cf. Theorem
6.4
).

Theorem 5.11
.

Let
\lambda
be a self-dual Hecke character over
K
of infinity type
(1,0)
and
\pi_{\lambda}

the associated cuspidal automorphic representation.
Let
p
be an odd prime inert in
K
.
Let
\ell\nmid 2p{\rm{N}}_{K/{\mathbb{Q}}}(\mathrm{cond^{r}}{\lambda})
be a prime.
Let
p_{0}\nmid p\ell
be an odd prime inert in
K

such that
p_{0}^{2}|\mathrm{cond^{r}}{\lambda}
and
\ell\nmid p_{0}(p_{0}^{2}-1)
. Then for all but finitely many
\nu\in\Xi_{\lambda,p}^{+}
, we have

\begin{cases}v_{\ell}\left(\frac{L(1/2,\pi_{\lambda,K}\otimes\nu)}{\Omega_{\lambda}^{\{p_{0}\}}}\right)=0,&\quad p\nmid N^{-},\\
v_{\ell}\left(\frac{L(1/2,\pi_{\lambda,K}\otimes\nu)}{\Omega_{\lambda}^{\{p,p_{0}\}}}\right)=0,&\quad p\mid N^{-}.\end{cases}

Proof.

Let
F_{\ell}
be defined as in Cases I and II by replacing
\varphi
therein with
\widetilde{\varphi}
. Put
p_{0}^{m}=\mathrm{cond^{r}}\lambda_{p_{0}}
and

F_{\ell}^{\prime}=\sum_{k\in O_{K_{p_{0}}}^{\times}/O_{K_{p_{0}},p_{0}^{m}}^{\times}}\pi_{\lambda}(\iota_{\varsigma_{p_{0}}}(k))F_{\ell},

where

\iota_{\varsigma_{p_{0}}}
is the local embedding
K_{p_{0}}\hookrightarrow B_{p_{0}}
arising from
\theta
and
u
as in the second bullet point of Theorem
3.15
.

For integers
n\geq 0
, put

{\Theta_{n}}:=\sum_{[a]_{n}\in{\rm G}_{n}}F^{\prime}_{\ell}(x_{n,p_{0}^{m}}(a))\cdot[a]_{n}\in O[{\rm G}_{n}],

where we use the modified
\varsigma^{(n)}
in subsection

3.3.3
to define CM points.
In view of Theorem
3.15

the proof of the
\ell
-indivisibility of
\nu(\Theta_{n})
is essentially the same as that of Theorems
5.9
and
5.14
: it suffices to show that the similarly defined
\tilde{F}_{\ell}^{\prime}
is non-Eisenstein,
which is again a consequence of
Lemma
5.4
and Corollary
7.2
.
∎

5.3.2.
Restriction of test vector to components of Shimura set

We describe some consequences of the proof of Theorem
5.9
which will be used in the split case.

Let
\varphi\in M_{2}(U,\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}})
be the test vector as in §
3.2
associated to
\lambda
.

Proposition 5.12
.

If
\epsilon(\lambda)=\pm 1
, then
\varphi^{\pm}\neq 0
and
\varphi^{\mp}=0
.

Proof.

In the following we choose an auxiliary prime
\ell\nmid 2{\rm{N}}_{K/{\mathbb{Q}}}(\mathrm{cond^{r}}{\lambda})
and normalise
\varphi
to be
\ell
-optimal.

As seen in the proof of Theorem
5.9
, for
p\nmid 2\ell{\rm{N}}_{K/{\mathbb{Q}}}(\mathrm{cond^{r}}{\lambda})
inert in
K
, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{a\in(\mathbb{Z}/p^{s}\mathbb{Z})^{\times}}\rho\begin{pmatrix}a&\\
&1\\
\end{pmatrix}\widetilde{F}_{\ell}\equiv

\displaystyle p^{s}\cdot(1-p^{-1}\alpha_{p}\cdot\rho(\begin{pmatrix}p^{-1}&\\
&1\\
\end{pmatrix})F_{\ell}\pmod{\varpi}.

and

F_{\ell}=\varphi-\frac{1}{\alpha_{p}}{\rho}\begin{pmatrix}p^{-1}&\\
&1\end{pmatrix}\varphi.

Therefore,

p^{-s}\sum_{a\in(\mathbb{Z}/p^{s}\mathbb{Z})^{\times}}\rho\begin{pmatrix}a&\\
&1\\
\end{pmatrix}\widetilde{F}_{\ell}{\equiv}\varphi+\frac{1}{p}\rho\begin{pmatrix}p^{-2}&\\
&1\\
\end{pmatrix}\varphi\pmod{\varpi}.

(5.9)

Put
\epsilon
for the sign of
\epsilon(\lambda)
. As seen in the proof of Theorem
5.9
,
\widetilde{F}_{\ell}^{-\epsilon}
is Eisenstein but
\widetilde{F}_{\ell}^{\epsilon}
is non-Eisenstein.
Moreover,
\sum_{a\in(\mathbb{Z}/p^{s}\mathbb{Z})^{\times}}\rho\begin{pmatrix}a&\\
&1\\
\end{pmatrix}\widetilde{F}_{\ell}
is non-Eisenstein.
Therefore the proof shows that

\sum_{a\in(\mathbb{Z}/p^{s}\mathbb{Z})^{\times}}\rho\begin{pmatrix}a&\\
&1\\
\end{pmatrix}\widetilde{F}_{\ell}^{-\epsilon}
is Eisenstein, and in turn
\sum_{a\in(\mathbb{Z}/p^{s}\mathbb{Z})^{\times}}\rho\begin{pmatrix}a&\\
&1\\
\end{pmatrix}\widetilde{F}_{\ell}^{\epsilon}
non-Eisenstein.

In view of (
5.9
) and the preceding paragraph, it follows that
\varphi^{\epsilon}\pmod{\varpi}
is non-Eisenstein and

\varphi^{-\epsilon}+\frac{1}{p}\rho\begin{pmatrix}p^{-2}&\\
&1\\
\end{pmatrix}\varphi^{-\epsilon}\equiv 0\pmod{\varpi}

by Lemma
5.6
.
Therefore
\varphi^{-\epsilon}\pmod{\varpi}
is Eisenstein by Lemma
5.7
and so

\varphi^{-\epsilon}\equiv 0\pmod{\varpi}

by Lemma
5.6
again.
If
\varphi^{-\epsilon}\neq 0
, then for
\ell
sufficiently large
\varphi^{-\epsilon}\equiv\hskip-10.0pt/\ 0\pmod{\varpi}
, so contradiction
11
11

11

Alternatively, one may apply the same argument modulo powers of
\ell
.
.
∎

We now describe an application to the split case.
Let
p\nmid 2{\rm N}_{K/{\mathbb{Q}}}(\mathrm{cond^{r}}{\lambda})
be a prime split in
K
.
As in the inert case (
5.7
), define
\widetilde{F}_{\ell}
.

Corollary 5.13
.

Let
\ell\nmid 2{\rm{N}}_{K/{\mathbb{Q}}}(\mathrm{cond^{r}}{\lambda})
be a prime. If
\epsilon(\lambda)=\pm 1
, then
\widetilde{F}_{\ell}^{\pm}
is non-Eisenstein and
\widetilde{F}_{\ell}^{\mp}=0
.

Proof.

In light of Proposition
5.12
,
\varphi^{\pm}\pmod{\varpi}
is non-Eisenstein and
\varphi^{\mp}=0
. To see the same claim for
\widetilde{F}_{\ell}^{\pm}
, just note that they are related as in the proof of Theorem
5.9
, and then Lemma
5.7
applies.

∎

5.3.3.
Split case

Let
\lambda
be a self-dual Hecke character over
K
of infinity type
(1,0)
and
\pi_{\lambda}
the associated cuspidal automorphic representation.

Let
p\nmid 2{\rm{N}}_{K/{\mathbb{Q}}}(\mathrm{cond^{r}}{\lambda})
be a prime split in
K
. For
\nu\in\Xi
of order
p^{n}>1
, we have

\epsilon(\lambda\nu)=\epsilon(\lambda).

So the pair
(\pi_{\lambda},\nu)
is self-dual with root number
+1
. One may consider non-vanishing of central
L
-values
L^{\rm alg}(1/2,\pi_{\lambda,K}\otimes\nu)
modulo
\ell
whenever
\epsilon(\lambda)=+1
.

Our main result is the following.

Theorem 5.14
.

Let
\lambda
be a self-dual Hecke character over
K
of infinity type
(1,0)
and
\pi_{\lambda}
the associated cuspidal automorphic representation. Let
p
be an odd prime split in
K
.
Let
\ell
be a prime. Suppose that

(i)

\ell\nmid 2p{\rm{N}}_{K/{\mathbb{Q}}}(\mathrm{cond^{r}}{\lambda})
,

(ii)

\epsilon(\lambda)=+1
.

Then for all but finitely many
\nu\in\Xi_{p}
, we have

v_{\ell}\left(\frac{L(1/2,\pi_{\lambda,K}\otimes\nu)}{\Omega_{\lambda}}\right)=0.

Proof.

First consider
p\nmid 2{\rm{N}}_{K/{\mathbb{Q}}}(\mathrm{cond^{r}}{\lambda})
.
The argument is similar to the proof of Theorem
5.9
, whose notation will appear below.

Let
F_{\ell}:=\varphi^{\dagger}
be the
\ell
-adic avatar of the
p
-stabilization.
For each integer
n\geq 0
, put

\Theta_{n}:=\sum_{[a]_{n}\in{\rm G}_{n}}F_{\ell}(x_{n}(a))\cdot[a]_{n}\in O[{\rm G}_{n}].

It suffices to show that
v_{\ell}(\nu(\Theta_{n}))=0
for all but finitely many
\nu\in\Xi_{p}
.

Recall that

{\rm Tr}_{{\bf k}_{\ell}(\nu)/{\bf k}_{\ell}}(\nu(a^{-1})\cdot\nu(\Theta_{n})\pmod{\mathfrak{\varpi}}){\equiv}|D_{0}|d_{\nu}\cdot\sum_{\tau\in D_{1}}\widetilde{F}_{\ell}(x_{n}(a\tau)).

(5.10)

Under the assumption
\epsilon(\lambda)=+1
,
\widetilde{F}^{+}_{\ell}
is non-Eisenstein by Corollary
5.13
.
Therefore, in light of (
5.10
) and Proposition
5.8
, we conclude that

\nu(\Theta_{n})\neq 0

for all but finitely many
\nu\in\Xi_{p}
.

Now consider the case
p|{\rm{N}}_{K/{\mathbb{Q}}}(\mathrm{cond^{r}}{\lambda})
. Then
F_{\ell}
is the
\ell
adic avatar of
\varphi
.
We have

\displaystyle\sum_{a\in(\mathbb{Z}/p^{s}\mathbb{Z})^{\times}}\rho\begin{pmatrix}a&\\
&1\\
\end{pmatrix}\widetilde{F}_{\ell}{\equiv}

\displaystyle p^{s}F_{\ell}-{p^{s-1}}\rho\left(\begin{pmatrix}p^{-1}&\\
&1\end{pmatrix}\right)U_{p}F_{\ell}\pmod{\varpi}

and
F_{\ell}
is
U_{p}
-eigen with eigenvalue
\pm 1
if
p\parallel{\rm{N}}_{K/{\mathbb{Q}}}(\mathrm{cond^{r}}{\lambda})
and
0
else.
By Lemma
5.7
and Proposition
5.12
,
\widetilde{F}_{\ell}^{+}
is non-Eisenstein.

∎

5.4.
The vanishing of
\mu
-invariants

We consider the
\mu
-invariant of Rankin–Selberg
p
-adic
L
-functions in the CM case.

5.4.1.
Split case

Theorem 5.15
.

Let
\lambda
be a self-dual Hecke character over
K
of infinity type
(1,0)
and
\pi_{\lambda}
the associated cuspidal automorphic representation. Suppose that
\epsilon(\lambda)=+1
.

Let
p\nmid 2{\rm{N}}_{K/{\mathbb{Q}}}(\mathrm{cond^{r}}{\lambda})
be a prime split in
K
and
\mathscr{L}_{p}(\pi_{\lambda})
the associated
p
-adic
L
-function.
Then

\mu(\mathscr{L}_{p}(\pi_{\lambda}))=0.

Proof.

Let the notation be as in §
4.2
.
The following is a variant of the strategy used for
(\ell,p)
-non-vanishing in §
5.3
.

Let
F_{p}
be the
p
-adic avatar of the
p
-stabilization of the
p
-primitive test vector
\varphi
with respect to
\alpha_{p}
.
Note that

\Theta_{n}(\pi_{\lambda},1)\pmod{\varpi}{\equiv}|D_{0}|\alpha_{p}^{-n}\sum_{[u]_{n}\in\Gamma_{n}}\left(\sum_{\tau\in D_{1}}F_{p}(x_{n}(u\tau))\right)\cdot[u]_{n}{\pmod{\varpi}}.

For the vanishing of the
\mu
-invariant of the theta element
\Theta_{\infty}(\pi_{\lambda})
, it suffices to show that for
n\gg 0
, there exists
a\in\widehat{K}^{\times}
such that

\sum_{\tau\in D_{1}}F_{p}(x_{n}(a\tau))\equiv\hskip-10.0pt/\ 0\pmod{\varpi}.

In turn, it suffices to verify the hypotheses
of Proposition
5.8
(b)
for
F_{p}^{+}
, which may be seen as follows.
By Proposition
5.12
,
\varphi^{+}\pmod{\varpi}
is non-Eisenstein, and consequently so is
F_{\ell}^{+}\pmod{\varpi}
by Lemma
5.7
.

∎

Remark 5.16
.

If
\lambda
has root number
-1
, then
\mathscr{L}_{p}(\pi_{\lambda})=0
.

5.4.2.
Inert case

Theorem 5.17
.

Let
\lambda
be a self-dual Hecke character over
K
of infinity type
(1,0)
and
\pi_{\lambda}
the associated cuspidal automorphic representation.

Let
p\nmid 2{\rm{N}}_{K/{\mathbb{Q}}}(\mathrm{cond^{r}}{\lambda})
be a prime inert in
K
.
Then

\mu(\mathscr{L}_{p}(\pi_{\lambda}))=0.

Proof.

Let the notation be as in the proof of Proposition
4.7
.

Recall that

\widetilde{\Theta}_{n}(\pi_{\lambda})=\omega_{n}^{\epsilon}\widetilde{\Theta}_{n}^{\epsilon}(\pi_{\lambda}),\,\Theta_{\infty}^{\epsilon}(\pi_{\lambda})=\lim\widetilde{\Theta}_{n}^{\epsilon}(\pi_{\lambda}).

for
\epsilon
the parity of
n
.
Note that
\mu(\omega_{n}^{\pm})=0
.

In view of Definition
4.12
and (
4.6
), it suffices to show the vanishing of the
\mu
-invariant of
\Theta_{\infty}^{-\varepsilon}(\pi_{\lambda})
. By the above discussion, this is equivalent to

\mu(\widetilde{\Theta}_{n}(\pi_{\lambda}))=0

for
n\gg 0
of the same parity as
-\varepsilon
. The latter
p
-indivisibility follows by the same argument as in the proof of Theorem
5.9
.
∎

6.
Non-vanishing of Hecke
L
-values modulo
\ell

This section establishes our main results on the non-vanishing of Hecke
L
-values building on
the Rankin–Selberg results in section
5
. The bridge among the two arises from comparison of quaternionic and CM periods, which constitutes the core of the section.

The main results are Corollary
6.8
on the
(\ell,p)
non-vanishing of Hecke
L
-values and Theorem
6.10
concerning
\mu
-invariant. Along the way we prove the comparison of periods (cf. Theorems
6.4
and
6.7
).

6.1.
Backdrop

6.1.1.
Setting

Let
\lambda
be a self-dual Hecke character over an imaginary quadratic field
K
of infinity type
(1,0)
. Let
\phi_{\lambda}\in S_{2}(\Gamma_{0}(N))
be the associated weight
2
theta series associated for

N=D_{K}{\rm{N}}_{K/{\mathbb{Q}}}(\mathrm{cond^{r}}{\lambda}).

Note that
D_{K}|{\rm{N}}_{K/{\mathbb{Q}}}(\mathrm{cond^{r}}{\lambda})
by the self-duality.

Let
B
be the definite quaternion algebra over
{\mathbb{Q}}
such that

\epsilon(B_{q})=\eta_{K_{q}}(-1)

for any
q
(cf. Lemma
3.2
).
Let
\pi_{\lambda}
be the cuspidal irreducible automorphic representation of
B^{\times}_{{\mathbb{A}}}
associated to
\phi_{\lambda}
.

Let
\ell\nmid 2N
be a prime.
Let
\varphi_{\lambda}\in\pi_{\lambda}^{\widehat{R}^{\times}}
be the toric vector as in Definition
3.4
, which is
\ell
-primitive and
K_{q}^{\times}
-invariant for all
q|{\rm{N}}_{K/{\mathbb{Q}}}(\mathrm{cond^{r}}{\lambda})
non-split in
K
.

Note that for any finite order Hecke character
\chi
over
K
, we have a factorisation

L(1/2,\pi_{\lambda,K}\otimes\chi)=L(1,\lambda\chi)L(1,\lambda\chi^{-1}).

(6.1)

In the context of Ranin–Selberg
L
-values the period

\Omega_{\lambda}=\frac{8\pi^{2}(\phi_{\lambda},\phi_{\lambda})}{\langle\varphi_{\lambda},\varphi_{\lambda}\rangle}

arises naturally.
On the other hand, we have a CM period
\Omega_{K}
associated to Hecke
L
-values over
K
, which is well defined up to
\ell
-adic units (cf. §
1
).
In light of the factorisation (
6.1
) of
L
-values a basic problem is to compare the periods

\Omega_{\lambda}
and
\Omega_{K}
.

For a prime
p
, recall
\Gamma={\mathrm{Gal}}(K_{\infty}/K)
is the Galois group of the anticyclotomic
{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
-extension of
K
,

\Xi_{p}
the set of finite order characters of
\Gamma
and

\Xi_{\lambda,p}^{+}=\{\nu\in\Xi_{p}|\,\epsilon(\lambda\nu)=+1\}.

6.1.2.

The subsection describe a lower bound for
\ell
-adic valuation of Hecke
L
-values and of periods in terms of certain local invariants.

For
q|{\rm{N}}_{K/{\mathbb{Q}}}(\mathrm{cond^{r}}{\lambda})
such that
q
is non-split in
K
, put
\mu_{\ell}(\lambda_{q})=\inf_{x\in O_{K_{q}}^{\times}}v_{\ell}(\lambda_{q}(x)-1)
.

Lemma 6.1
.

Let
\lambda
be a self-dual Hecke character over an imaginary quadratic field
K
of infinity type
(1,0)
. Let
\ell\nmid 2{\rm{N}}_{K/{\mathbb{Q}}}(\mathrm{cond^{r}}{\lambda})
be a prime. Then

v_{\ell}\left(\frac{L(1,\lambda)}{\Omega_{K}}\right)\geq\sum_{q|{\rm{N}}_{K/{\mathbb{Q}}}(\mathrm{cond^{r}}{\lambda})\text{ inert}}\mu_{\ell}(\lambda_{q}).

Proof.

This is due to Finis
[
27
, Propositions 3.6 and 3.7]
.
(For
\ell
prime to
2q
, note that
\mu_{\ell}(\lambda_{q})=0
if
K_{q}
is ramified.)
∎

Remark 6.2
.

Let
q
be an inert prime.

\bullet

If conductor of
\lambda_{q}
is
q
and
(\ell,p+1)=1
, then
v_{\ell}(\lambda_{q})=0
.

\bullet

If conductor of
\lambda_{q}
is at least
q^{2}
, then
\mu_{\ell}(\lambda_{q})=0
.

\bullet

Consider anticyclotomic
p
-power order twist
\lambda\nu
with
p
inert and
\nu\in\Xi_{p}
. If
q\neq p
is inert and divides
{\rm{N}}_{K/{\mathbb{Q}}}(\mathrm{cond^{r}}{\lambda})
, then
\nu_{q}
is trivial, and so
\mu_{\ell}(\lambda_{q}\nu_{q})=\mu_{\ell}(\lambda_{q})
. If
p=q
, then
\mu_{\ell}(\lambda_{p}\nu_{p})=0
for
\nu_{p}
so that
\mathrm{cond^{r}}{\nu_{p}}>\max\{p,\mathrm{cond^{r}}{\lambda_{p}}\}
.

Lemma 6.3
.

Let
\lambda
be a self-dual Hecke character over an imaginary quadratic field
K
of infinity type
(1,0)
. Let
\ell\nmid 2{\rm{N}}_{K/{\mathbb{Q}}}(\mathrm{cond^{r}}{\lambda})
be a prime.
Then

v_{\ell}\left(\frac{\Omega_{\lambda}}{\Omega_{K}^{2}}\right)\geq 2\sum_{\text{$q|{\rm{N}}_{K/{\mathbb{Q}}}(\mathrm{cond^{r}}{\lambda})$ inert}}\mu_{\ell}(\lambda_{q}).

Proof.

Let
p\nmid 2\ell{\rm{N}}_{K/{\mathbb{Q}}}(\mathrm{cond^{r}}{\lambda})
be a prime inert
12
12

12

If
\epsilon(\lambda)=+1
, one may use split
p
in the proof.
in
K
.

By Theorem
5.9
, for all but finitely many

\nu\in\Xi_{\lambda,p}^{+}
, we have

v_{\ell}\left(\frac{L(1,\lambda\nu)L(1,\lambda\nu^{-1})}{\Omega_{\lambda}}\right)=0

Then for such a
\nu
,

v_{\ell}\left(\frac{L(1,\lambda\nu)L(1,\lambda\nu^{-1})}{\Omega_{K}^{2}}\right)=v_{\ell}\left(\frac{\Omega_{\lambda}}{\Omega_{K}^{2}}\right).

Therefore Lemma
6.1
concludes the proof.
∎

6.2.
Comparison of periods and
(\ell,p)
non-vanishing

This subsection establishes the comparison and
(\ell,p)
non-vanishing of Hecke
L
-values almost simultaneously.

We begin with an outline of the strategy.
In light of the
(\ell,p)
non-vanishing result for Rankin–Selberg
L
-values established in §
5
and the
\ell
-divisibility lower bound for the Hecke
L
-values in §
6.1.2
, the sought after non-vanishing of Hecke
L
-values and the comparison of periods are equivalent. Since the non-vanishing of Hecke
L
-value in the
p
split case is known due to Finis
[
27
]
and the comparison of periods essentially does not depend on the prime
p
, the non-vanishing in the
p
inert case follows if
\epsilon(\lambda)=+1
.
To approach the case
\epsilon(\lambda)=-1
and
p
inert, we find another link between the non-vanishing and comparison of periods via an anticyclotomic twist, leading to a connection between the root number
+1
and
-1
cases! It turns out that the variant non-vanishing - Theorem
5.11
- is the key to such a connection (see the proof of Theorem
6.5
).

6.2.1.
The case
\epsilon(\lambda)=+1

Theorem 6.4
.

Let
\lambda
be a self-dual Hecke character over an imaginary quadratic field
K
of infinity type
(1,0)
with
\epsilon(\lambda)=+1
. Let
\ell\nmid 2{\rm{N}}_{K/{\mathbb{Q}}}(\mathrm{cond^{r}}{\lambda})
be a prime.

(i)

We have

v_{\ell}\left(\frac{\Omega_{\lambda}}{\Omega_{K}^{2}}\right)=2\sum_{\text{$q|{\rm{N}}_{K/{\mathbb{Q}}}(\mathrm{cond^{r}}{\lambda})$ inert}}\mu_{\ell}(\lambda_{q}).

(ii)

Let
p\nmid 2\ell{\rm{N}}_{K/{\mathbb{Q}}}(\mathrm{cond^{r}}{\lambda})
be a prime.
Then for all but finitely many
\nu\in\Xi_{\lambda,p}^{+}
, we have

v_{\ell}\left(\frac{L(1,\lambda\nu)}{\Omega_{K}}\right)=\sum_{\text{$q|{\rm{N}}_{K/{\mathbb{Q}}}(\mathrm{cond^{r}}{\lambda})$ inert}}\mu_{\ell}(\lambda_{q}).

Proof.

We first show that without any assumption on
\epsilon(\lambda)
, the first assertion and the second assertion for a fixed prime
p\nmid 2\ell{\rm{N}}_{K/{\mathbb{Q}}}(\mathrm{cond^{r}}{\lambda})
are equivalent.

By Theorems
5.9
and
5.14
, for all but finitely many

\nu\in\Xi_{\lambda,p}^{+}
, we have

v_{\ell}\left(\frac{L(1,\lambda\nu)L(1,\lambda\nu^{-1})}{\Omega_{K}^{2}}\right)-2\sum_{\text{$q|{\rm{N}}_{K/{\mathbb{Q}}}(\mathrm{cond^{r}}{\lambda})$ inert}}\mu_{\ell}(\lambda_{q})=v_{\ell}\left(\frac{\Omega_{\lambda}}{\Omega_{K}^{2}}\right)-2\sum_{\text{$q|{\rm{N}}_{K/{\mathbb{Q}}}(\mathrm{cond^{r}}{\lambda})$ inert}}\mu_{\ell}(\lambda_{q}).

(6.2)

Note that
\nu_{q}
is trivial character for
q|{\rm{N}}_{K/{\mathbb{Q}}}(\mathrm{cond^{r}}{\lambda})
inert. So in view of Lemma
6.1
and the third part of Remark
6.2
, we have

v_{\ell}\left(\frac{L(1,\lambda\nu)}{\Omega_{K}}\right),\quad v_{\ell}\left(\frac{L(1,\lambda\nu^{-1})}{\Omega_{K}}\right)\quad\geq\sum_{\text{$q|{\rm{N}}_{K/{\mathbb{Q}}}(\mathrm{cond^{r}}{\lambda})$ inert}}\mu_{\ell}(\lambda_{q})

for all
\nu\in\Xi_{\lambda,p}^{+}
such that
\mathrm{cond^{r}}{\nu_{p}}\geq p^{2}
. Therefore the two assertions are equivalent.

Under the condition
\epsilon(\lambda)=+1
, the second assertion for a prime
p\nmid 2\ell{\rm{N}}_{K/{\mathbb{Q}}}(\mathrm{cond^{r}}{\lambda})
split in
K
is a result of Finis
[
27
, Thm. 1.1]
. The proof concludes.

∎

6.2.2.
An intermediate case

To connect the root number
+1
and
-1
cases, we consider anticyclotomic twist at an auxiliary inert prime as described below.

For
\widetilde{\lambda}
a self-dual Hecke character over
K
with infinitely type
(1,0)
and
r|{\rm{N}}_{K/{\mathbb{Q}}}(\mathrm{cond^{r}}{\widetilde{\lambda}})
an odd inert prime, let
\varphi_{\widetilde{\lambda}}^{\{r\}}
be the
\ell
-primitive test vector defined in §
3.2.1
which is of
U_{0}((\mathrm{cond^{r}}{\widetilde{\lambda})^{2}})_{r}
level at
r
. Recall that
{\mathbb{C}}\varphi_{\widetilde{\lambda}}
and
{\mathbb{C}}{\varphi}_{\widetilde{\lambda}}^{\{r\}}
differ at most at
r
, where the former is
K_{r}^{\times}
-invariant and the latter a newform at
r
. Put

{\Omega}^{\{r\}}_{\widetilde{\lambda}}=\frac{8\pi^{2}(\phi_{\widetilde{\lambda}},\phi_{\widetilde{\lambda}})}{\langle{\varphi}^{\{r\}}_{\widetilde{\lambda}},{\varphi}^{\{r\}}_{\widetilde{\lambda}}\rangle}.

We present the following variant of Theorem
6.4
.

Theorem 6.5
.

Let
\lambda
be a self-dual Hecke character over
K
with infinitely type
(1,0)
. Let
\ell\nmid 2{\rm{N}}_{K/{\mathbb{Q}}}(\mathrm{cond^{r}}{\lambda})
be a prime. Let
r
be an odd inert prime so that
\ell\nmid r(r^{2}-1)
.

(i)

For any anticyclotomic
\chi\in\Xi_{r}
, we have

\begin{cases}\displaystyle v_{\ell}\left(\frac{{\Omega}^{\{r\}}_{\lambda\chi}}{\Omega_{K}^{2}}\right)=v_{\ell}\left(\frac{{\Omega}_{\lambda\chi}}{\Omega_{K}^{2}}\right)=2\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\text{$q|{\rm{N}}_{K/{\mathbb{Q}}}(\mathrm{cond^{r}}{\lambda})$ inert}\\
q\nmid r\end{subarray}}\mu_{\ell}(\lambda_{q}),\quad&\begin{subarray}{c}\text{if $\mathrm{cond^{r}}{\lambda_{r}\chi_{r}}\geq{r^{2}}$}\end{subarray},\\
\displaystyle v_{\ell}\left(\frac{{\Omega}_{\lambda\chi}}{\Omega_{K}^{2}}\right)=2\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\text{$q|{\rm{N}}_{K/{\mathbb{Q}}}(\mathrm{cond^{r}}{\lambda})$ inert}\\
q\nmid r\end{subarray}}\mu_{\ell}(\lambda_{q}),\quad&\text{if $\lambda_{r}\chi_{r}$ is unramified}.\\
\end{cases}

(ii)

For all but finitely many anticyclotomic
\nu\in\Xi_{\lambda,r}^{+}
, we have

v_{\ell}\left(\frac{L(1,\lambda\nu)}{\Omega_{K}}\right)=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\text{$q|{\rm{N}}_{K/{\mathbb{Q}}}(\mathrm{cond^{r}}{\lambda})$ inert}\\
q\nmid r\end{subarray}}\mu_{\ell}(\lambda_{q}).

Proof.

We first show that the assertion (i) for a given
\chi
is equivalent to the assertion (ii). The case
r\nmid{\rm{N}}_{K/{\mathbb{Q}}}(\mathrm{cond^{r}}{\lambda\chi})
is treated in the proof of Theorem
6.4
(without the assumption
\ell\nmid(r^{2}-1)
).

In the following we show the equivalence for
\lambda\chi
ramified at
r
if

\ell\nmid r(r^{2}-1)
.

By Theorem
5.10
for the
{\mathbb{Z}}_{r}
-anticyclotomic twist family of
\lambda
for test vector

{\varphi}^{\{r\}}_{\lambda\chi}
, for all but finitely many
\nu\in\Xi_{\lambda\chi,r}^{+}
,
we have

v_{\ell}\left(\frac{{\Omega}^{\{r\}}_{\lambda\chi}}{\Omega_{K}^{2}}\right)=v_{\ell}\left(\frac{L(1,\lambda\chi\nu)L(1,\lambda\chi\nu^{-1})}{\Omega_{K}^{2}}\right).

(6.3)

Let
p^{\prime}\nmid 2\ell r{\rm{N}}_{K/{\mathbb{Q}}}(\mathrm{cond^{r}}{\lambda})
be an odd prime inert in
K
. Applying the non-vanishing results for
{\mathbb{Z}}_{p^{\prime}}
-anticyclotomic twist of
\lambda
for the test vector
\varphi_{\lambda\chi}
as in Theorem
5.9
and
{\varphi}_{\lambda\chi}^{\{r\}}
as in Theorem
5.11
, under the pertinent hypotheses on
\ell
and
r
, we have

v_{\ell}\left(\frac{L(1,\lambda\chi\nu)L(1,\lambda\chi\nu^{-1})}{{\Omega}_{\lambda\chi}}\right)=0=v_{\ell}\left(\frac{L(1,\lambda\chi\nu)L(1,\lambda\chi\nu^{-1})}{{\Omega}^{\{r\}}_{\lambda\chi}}\right)

for all but finitely many
\nu\in\Xi_{\lambda\chi,p^{\prime}}^{+}
.
It follows that

v_{\ell}\left(\frac{\Omega_{\lambda\chi}}{\Omega_{K}^{2}}\right)=v_{\ell}\left(\frac{{\Omega}^{\{r\}}_{\lambda\chi}}{\Omega_{K}^{2}}\right).

(6.4)

In light of (
6.3
) and (
6.4
),
the analysis in the proof Theorem
6.4
leads to the desired equivalence.

Since
r
is inert, we may choose
\chi
with
\mathrm{cond^{r}}{\chi_{r}\lambda_{r}}\geq r^{2}
so that
\epsilon(\lambda\chi)=+1
. Applying Theorem

6.4
, the part (i) holds for such a
\chi
, concluding the proof.
∎

Remark 6.6
.

The result allows
r
to divide the conductor of
\lambda
. Moreover, the first part allows
\lambda
to vary in its
{\mathbb{Z}}_{r}
-anticyclotomic twist family and the second holds without the root number condition (cf. Theorem
6.4
).

6.2.3.
The case
\epsilon(\lambda)=-1

Theorem 6.7
.

Let
\lambda
be a self-dual Hecke character over an imaginary quadratic field
K

of infinity type
(1,0)

with
\epsilon(\lambda)=-1
. Let
\ell\nmid 6{\rm{N}}_{K/{\mathbb{Q}}}(\mathrm{cond^{r}}{\lambda})
be a prime.

(i)

We have

v_{\ell}\left(\frac{\Omega_{\lambda}}{\Omega_{K}^{2}}\right)=2\sum_{\text{$q|{\rm{N}}_{K/{\mathbb{Q}}}(\mathrm{cond^{r}}{\lambda})$ inert}}\mu_{\ell}(\lambda_{q}).

(ii)

For
p\nmid 2\ell{\rm{N}}_{K/{\mathbb{Q}}}(\mathrm{cond^{r}}{\lambda})
be a prime.
Then for all but finitely many
\nu\in\Xi_{\lambda,p}^{+}
, we have

v_{\ell}\left(\frac{L(1,\lambda\nu)}{\Omega_{K}}\right)=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\text{$q|{\rm{N}}_{K/{\mathbb{Q}}}(\mathrm{cond^{r}}{\lambda})$ inert}\end{subarray}}\mu_{\ell}(\lambda_{q}).

Proof.

The equivalence between the first and the second parts has been shown in the proof of Theorem
6.4
.
Now it is enough to establish the second part for a particular prime
r
.

Let
r\nmid 2\ell{\rm{N}}_{K/{\mathbb{Q}}}(\mathrm{cond^{r}}{\lambda})
be a prime
13
13

13

It exists since
\ell>3
.
inert in
K
such that

\ell\nmid r(r^{2}-1)
.
By Theorem
6.5
(i) for
\chi=1
, the assertion follows.

∎

6.2.4.

We summarise consequences for Hecke
L
-values.

Corollary 6.8
.

Theorem
1.1
holds.

Proof.

For
p
split in
K
, the result is due to Finis (cf.
[
27
, Thm. 1.1]
). The inert case is the content of Theorems
6.4
and
6.7
.
∎

Corollary 6.9
.

Theorem
1.3
holds.

Proof.

If
p
splits in
K
, then the result follows from the interpolation formula for the
p
-adic
L
-function
\mathscr{L}_{p}(\pi_{\lambda})
(cf. Theorem
4.4
), the vanishing of its
\mu
-invariant (cf. Theorem
5.15
) and the comparison of periods (cf. Theorems
6.4
and
6.7
).

Likewise, the inert case follows from Theorems
4.9
and
5.17
, and the period comparison. ∎

6.3.
Rubin’s
p
-adic
L
-function

Theorem 6.10
.

Let
\lambda
be a Hecke character over an imaginary quadratic field
K
of infinity type
(1,0)

such that

\lambda\circ{\rm{N}}_{H/K}

is associated to a
{\mathbb{Q}}
-curve
E
over
H
with good reduction at a prime
p\nmid 6h_{K}
inert in
K
.
Let
\mathscr{L}_{p}(\lambda)
be an associated Rubin
p
-adic
L
-function.
Then

\mu(\mathscr{L}_{p}(\lambda))=0.

Proof.

By Proposition
4.18
and Theorem
5.17
,

\mu(\mathscr{L}_{p}(\lambda))=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\text{$q|{\rm N}_{K/{\mathbb{Q}}}(\mathrm{cond^{r}}{\lambda})$ inert}\end{subarray}}\mu_{p}(\lambda_{q}).

As explained below, the right hand side vanishes.

We have

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\text{$q|{\rm{N}}_{K/{\mathbb{Q}}}(\mathrm{cond^{r}}{\lambda})$ inert}\end{subarray}}\mu_{p}(\widehat{\lambda}_{q})=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\text{$q|{\rm{N}}_{K/{\mathbb{Q}}}(\mathrm{cond^{r}}{\lambda})$ inert}\end{subarray}}\mu_{p}(\lambda_{q})

for
\widehat{\lambda}
the
p
-adic avatar of
\lambda

and
\mu_{p}(\widehat{\lambda}_{q}):=\inf_{x\in O_{K_{q}}^{\times}}v_{p}(\widehat{\lambda}_{q}(x)-1)
.

Since
H/K
is unramified, for each
q|{\rm N}_{K/{\mathbb{Q}}}(\mathrm{cond^{r}}\lambda)
inert in
K
and
{\mathfrak{q}}
a prime of
H
above
q
, the norm map
O_{H_{\mathfrak{q}}}^{\times}\rightarrow O_{K_{q}}^{\times}
is surjective
14
14

14

In fact the norm map is identity as
q
splits completely in
H
.
. Thus
\mu_{p}(\widehat{\lambda}_{q})=\mu_{p}((\widehat{\lambda}\circ{\rm N}_{H/K})_{{\mathfrak{q}}})
, where the latter is similarly defined.

Note that
\widehat{\lambda}\circ{\rm N}_{H/K}
factors through
{\mathrm{Gal}}(H(E[p^{\infty}])/H)\subset{\mathrm{Aut}}_{O_{K_{p}}}(E[p^{\infty}])=O_{K_{p}}^{\times}
. Write
{\mathrm{Gal}}(H(E[p^{\infty}])/H)
as
{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}^{2}\times\Delta
, for which
p\nmid\#\Delta
. Hence, we have

(\widehat{\lambda}\circ{\rm N}_{H/K})|_{O_{H_{\mathfrak{q}}}^{\times}}\subset(\widehat{\lambda}\circ{\rm N}_{H/K})|_{\Delta}

with order coprime to
p
. It follows that

\mu_{p}((\widehat{\lambda}\circ{\rm N}_{H/K})_{{\mathfrak{q}}})=0
.

∎

7.
Newforms as test vectors for supercuspidal representations

In this section we show that newform is a test vector for certain self-dual pairs
(\pi,1)
with
\pi
supercuspidal, and calculate the associated toric period.
For a given prime
\ell
, it is also shown that the latter is an
\ell
-adic unit
under some
conditions. The main result is Theorem
7.1
.

The explicit study of such toric periods is a key to arithmetic applications, such as Theorems
1.1
,
1.3
and
1.5
(see also
[
83
,
48
,
49
,
47
]
).

The setting and notation of this section are independent from the rest.

7.1.
Main result

7.1.1.
Setting

Let
q
be an odd prime and
K/{\mathbb{Q}}_{q}
an unramified quadratic extension. Let
\eta_{K}
be the associated quadratic character of
{\mathbb{Q}}_{q}^{\times}
.

Let
\lambda
be a character of
K^{\times}
of exponential conductor
m\geq 2
such that
\lambda|_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{q}^{\times}}=\eta_{K}
.

Let
\pi=\pi_{\lambda}
be the associated representation of
{\mathrm{PGL}}_{2}({\mathbb{Q}}_{q})
, which has exponential conductor
n=2m
. Note that
(\pi,1)
is a self-dual pair. Moreover, the Tunnell–Saito condition is satisfied as seen in the proof of Lemma
3.2
. The primary goal of this section is to consider
K^{\times}
-toric period of newforms in
\pi
with respect to the following embedding
K\hookrightarrow M_{2}({\mathbb{Q}}_{q})
.

Put

\text{$M_{0}(q^{2m})=\left\{\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{pmatrix}\in M_{2}({\mathbb{Z}}_{q})\ |\ \ q^{2m}|c\right\}$ and $U_{0}(q^{2m})=M_{0}(q^{2m})\cap{\mathrm{GL}}_{2}({\mathbb{Z}}_{q})$}.

The following family of embeddings
\iota:K\hookrightarrow M_{2}({\mathbb{Q}}_{q})
satisfy
\iota K\cap M_{0}(q^{2m})=\iota O_{K,q^{m}}
for
O_{K,q^{m}}:={\mathbb{Z}}_{q}+q^{m}O_{K}
.

Let
\theta\in K
be a unit so that
\overline{\theta}=-\theta
, where
\overline{\cdot}
denotes the action of non-trivial element in
{\mathrm{Gal}}(K/{\mathbb{Q}}_{q})
.
Pick
u\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{q}^{\times}
such that
15
15

15

It suffices to solve
u^{2}\theta^{2}-1\equiv x^{2}\pmod{q}
for
u,x\in{\mathbb{F}}_{q}^{\times}
. Consider the surjective map
{\mathbb{F}}_{q^{2}}^{\times}\rightarrow{\mathbb{F}}_{q}^{\times},x+u\theta\mapsto x^{2}-u^{2}\theta^{2}
. Then
x^{2}-u^{2}\theta^{2}=-1\pmod{q}
has
q+1
solutions
x+u\theta
with
u,x\in{\mathbb{F}}_{q}
, and at most
\begin{cases}4,&\quad q>3,\\
2,&\quad q=3\end{cases}
solutions with
x
or
u=0
in
{\mathbb{F}}_{q}
.

u^{2}\theta^{2}-1\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{q}^{\times 2}.

Define an embedding
\iota:K\hookrightarrow M_{2}({\mathbb{Q}}_{q})
by

\displaystyle\theta\mapsto

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}q^{-m}&\\
&1\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}1&-u\\
&1\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}&1\\
\theta^{2}&\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}1&u\\
&1\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}q^{m}&\\
&1\end{pmatrix}=

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}-u\theta^{2}&\frac{1-u^{2}\theta^{2}}{q^{m}}\\
q^{m}\theta^{2}&u\theta^{2}\end{pmatrix}.

We emphasise that the embedding of the unramified
torus in
{\mathrm{PGL}}_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{q})
depends only on the conductor of the representation.

Let

f\in\pi^{U_{0}(q^{2m})}

be a newform in
\pi
.
Denote by
(\ ,\ )
a
{\mathrm{PGL}}_{2}({\mathbb{Q}}_{q})
-invariant non-degenerate Hermitian pairing on
\pi
.
The primary object of this section is the toric period

\gamma_{\theta,u}:=\frac{1}{{\mathrm{vol}}(K^{\times}/{\mathbb{Q}}_{q}^{\times})(f,f)}\int_{K^{\times}/{\mathbb{Q}}_{q}^{\times}}(\pi(\iota(t))f,f)d^{\times}t.

7.1.2.
Results

Theorem 7.1
.

Let the setting be as in §
7.1.1
. Then for
\gamma_{\theta,u}
the toric period of a newform
f\in\pi
, we have

\displaystyle\gamma_{\theta,u}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(1-q^{-2})q^{m}}\left(2+\epsilon(\pi)(\lambda^{-1}(a_{0}+\theta u)+\lambda^{-1}(-a_{0}+\theta u))\right).

Here
a_{0}\in({\mathbb{Z}}/q^{m}{\mathbb{Z}})^{\times}
is a solution of
1+(a^{2}-\theta^{2}u^{2})\equiv 0\pmod{q^{m}}
.

To discuss non-vanishing of the toric period, consider the decomposition

O_{K}^{\times}=\mu_{K}(1+qO_{K})

with
\mu_{K}\subset O_{K}^{\times}
the torsion subgroup and let
{\rm pr}:O_{K}^{\times}\rightarrow 1+qO_{K}
be the projection map.

Corollary 7.2
.

Let the setting be as in §
7.1.1
.

(i)

Any newform
f\in\pi
is a test vector for the pair
(\pi,1)
, i.e.

\gamma_{\theta,u}\neq 0,

except in the case that
a_{0}+u\theta\in\mu_{K}\cdot{\rm pr}(\ker(\lambda))
and
\lambda((a_{0}+u\theta))=-\epsilon(\pi)
. Moreover, for a given
\lambda
and
\theta
, there exists
u
such that
\gamma_{\theta,u}\neq 0
.

(ii)

Let
\ell\nmid q
be a prime.
Then for a given
\theta
, there exists
u
such that

v_{\ell}((q^{2}-1)\gamma_{\theta,u})=0.

Proof.

(i)

Suppose that the toric period
\gamma_{\theta,u}
vanishes. In view of Theorem
7.1
, we then have

\lambda(a_{0}+u\theta)=-\epsilon(\pi).

This implies that
{\rm pr}(a_{0}+u\theta)\in\ker(\lambda)
, otherwise
\lambda({\rm pr}(a_{0}+u\theta))
would be a non-trivial
q
-th power root of unity
since
\lambda|_{(1+qO_{K})/(1+q{\mathbb{Z}}_{q})}
is primitive with
{(1+qO_{K})}/(1+q{\mathbb{Z}}_{q})\simeq{\mathbb{Z}}/q^{m-1}{\mathbb{Z}}
.

The furthermore part follows from the fact that: As
u
varies,
\lambda({\rm pr}(a_{0}+\theta u))
can be any
q^{m-1}
-th root of unity,
m\geq 2
. (Note that here
a_{0}
is determined by
u
and
\theta
.)

Indeed, suppose that

a^{2}-\theta^{2}u^{2}=-1
with
a,u\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{q}^{\times}
.
Then for any norm
1
element
\alpha
in
O_{K}^{1}\cap(1+qO_{K})
,
a^{\prime}+\theta u^{\prime}:=({a}+\theta u)\alpha
also satisfies the equation

a^{\prime 2}-\theta^{2}u^{\prime 2}=-1
with
a^{\prime},u^{\prime}\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{q}^{\times}
. On the other hand, the norm map
1+qO_{K}\rightarrow 1+q{\mathbb{Z}}_{q}
restricted to
