1+q{\mathbb{Z}}_{q}
is surjective, and then so is
O_{K}^{1}\cap(1+qO_{K})\rightarrow(1+qO_{K})/(1+q{\mathbb{Z}}_{q})
. Since the map

({a}+\theta u)O_{K}^{1}\cap(1+qO_{K})\rightarrow(1+qO_{K})/(1+q{\mathbb{Z}}_{q})

is surjective
and
\lambda|_{1+qO_{K}}
is a primitive character on
(1+qO_{K})/(1+q{\mathbb{Z}}_{q})\simeq{\mathbb{Z}}/q^{m-1}{\mathbb{Z}}
, the fact follows.

(ii)

Take
u
and
\theta
such that
\lambda({\rm pr}(a_{0}+u\theta))\neq 1
. Such an
u
exists by the analysis in (i).

We rely on the following:

Fact 7.3
.

Let
\zeta
be a primitive
k
-th root of unity with
k\neq 2
. Then
{\rm N}_{{\mathbb{Q}}(\zeta)^{+}/{\mathbb{Q}}}(\pm 2+\zeta+\overline{\zeta})
divides either any odd prime factor of
k
or divides
2
if
k
is a power of
2
.

Proof of Fact
7.3
.

Note that

\begin{aligned} x^{\varphi(k)}\prod_{s\in({\mathbb{Z}}/k{\mathbb{Z}})^{\times}}(x+x^{-1}+\zeta^{s}+\overline{\zeta}^{s})=&\prod_{s\in({\mathbb{Z}}/k{\mathbb{Z}})^{\times}}(x^{2}+1+x\zeta^{s}+x\overline{\zeta}^{s})=&\Phi_{k}(-x)^{2}\end{aligned},

where
\varphi
is the Euler function and
\Phi_{k}
the
k
-th cyclotomic polynomial.
Therefore,

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle N}_{{\mathbb{Q}}(\zeta)^{+}/{\mathbb{Q}}}(\pm 2+\zeta+\overline{\zeta})^{2}=

\displaystyle\Phi_{k}(\mp 1)^{2}(\pm 1)^{\varphi(k)}.

Recall that

\Phi_{k}(x)|\frac{x^{k}-1}{x^{d}-1}
for any proper divisor
d|k
.
Thus if
r|k
is an odd prime, we have

\Phi_{k}(\pm 1)\ |\ \frac{(\pm 1)^{k}-1}{(\pm 1)^{k/r}-1}\ |\ r.

If
k=2^{s}
with
s\geq 2
, then
\Phi_{k}(\pm 1)|\ \frac{(\pm 1)^{k}-1}{(\pm 1)^{k/2}-1}\ |\ 2
.
∎

The assertion is a consequence of Fact
7.3
: we apply it for
k
being the order of
\epsilon(\pi)\lambda(a_{0}+u\theta)
. Note that
q|k
and so the proof concludes.

∎

Remark 7.4
.

An elementary argument shows the existence of an embedding
K\hookrightarrow M_{2}({\mathbb{Q}}_{q})
such that the toric period associated to
(\pi,1)
is non-zero. In contrast, the above result gives the existence with respect to which the toric period is an
\ell
-adic unit for a given prime
\ell
. The latter is crucial for our application.

7.1.3.
Strategy

The Kirillov model is integral to our method.

We first obtain an expression for the matrix coefficients of the newform under toric action in terms of a linear combination of twist epsilon factors, without assuming supercuspidality (cf. Theorem
7.9
). This relies on the action of Atkin–Lehner operator on twists of the newform (cf. Proposition
7.8
).
In our supercuspidal case, the epsilon factor of a
{\mathrm{GL}}_{2}({\mathbb{Q}}_{q})
-representation equals that of the associated character of
K^{\times}
(cf. Lemma
7.24
).
We explicitly calculate the latter using an approach of Murase and Sugano
[
63
]
.
This transforms the toric period into a twisted sum of a Jacobi sum and values of
\lambda
(cf. Lemma
7.25
). The former turns out to be elementary (cf. Lemma
7.28
), leading to an expression for the toric period in terms of values of
\lambda
(cf. Proposition
7.29
).

We begin with preliminaries on the Kirillov model in §
7.2
. Then §
7.3
presents the connection with epsilon factors, and §
7.4
of the latter with values of
\lambda
.
The proof of Theorem
7.1
concludes in §
7.4.4
.

Remark 7.5
.

(i)

Our approach perhaps applies to self-dual pairs
(\pi,\chi)
over
K
with
\mathrm{cond^{r}}{\chi}\leq\mathrm{cond^{r}}{\pi}
.

(ii)

For
m
even, one may also resort to the compact induction model (cf.
[
49
,
47
]
).

7.2.
Preliminaries on Kirillov model

7.2.1.
The model

Let
\pi
be an irreducible admissible representation of
{\mathrm{PGL}}_{2}({\mathbb{Q}}_{q})
. Let
\psi
be a non-trivial character of
{\mathbb{Q}}_{q}
.

Recall that Kirillov model
{\mathcal{K}}(\pi,\psi)
of
\pi
with respect to
\psi
is a model of
\pi
in the space of locally constant functions such that upon restriction to the upper triangle subgroup
B({\mathbb{Q}}_{q})
the action is given by

\pi\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
&d\end{pmatrix}f(x)=\psi(bx/d)f(ax/d),\quad a,d,x\in{\mathbb{Q}}_{q}^{\times},b\in{\mathbb{Q}}_{q}.

The space of Schwartz functions is a finite codimensional subspace of
{\mathcal{K}}(\pi,\psi)
, and it equals
{\mathcal{K}}(\pi,\psi)
if and only if
\pi
is supercuspidal (cf.
[
52
, §2]
).

7.2.2.
Newforms and twists

Denote by
q^{n}
the conductor of
\pi
.
Recall that the space of newforms in
\pi
is the subspace fixed by
U_{0}(q^{n})
.

In the following we choose
\psi
to be unramified and identify
\pi
with its Kirillov model
{\mathcal{K}}(\pi,\psi)
,
and assume that
n\geq 2
.

We have the following explicit description of newforms (cf.
[
80
, p. 23]
).

Lemma 7.6
.

Suppose that
n\geq 2
. Then the space of newform is
{\mathbb{C}}\cdot 1_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{q}^{\times}}
.

Corollary 7.7
.

Suppose that
n\geq 2
.
For
k\geq 1
, we have

\sum_{a\in{\mathbb{Z}}/q^{k}{\mathbb{Z}}}\pi\begin{pmatrix}1&\frac{a}{q^{k}}\\
&1\end{pmatrix}f=0.

In particular, the action of
U_{q}=\sum_{a\in{\mathbb{Z}}/q{\mathbb{Z}}}\begin{pmatrix}q&a\\
&1\end{pmatrix}
on newforms is with eigenvalue
0
.

Proof.

Note that the unipotent action does not change the support of a newform.
For
x\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{q}^{\times}
, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{a\in{\mathbb{Z}}/q^{k}{\mathbb{Z}}}\pi\begin{pmatrix}1&\frac{a}{q^{k}}\\
&1\end{pmatrix}f(x)=

\displaystyle\sum_{a\in{\mathbb{Z}}/q^{k}{\mathbb{Z}}}\psi\left(\frac{ax}{q^{k}}\right)f(x)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{a\in{\mathbb{Z}}/q^{k}{\mathbb{Z}}}\psi\left(\frac{a}{q^{k}}\right)f(x).

Here the last equality follows by taking
f=1_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{q}^{\times}}
, which also implies that

\sum_{a\in{\mathbb{Z}}/q^{k}{\mathbb{Z}}}\pi\begin{pmatrix}1&\frac{a}{q^{k}}\\
&1\end{pmatrix}f(x)=0
since the sum of the
q^{k}
-th root of unity is zero.

∎

In the following,
we consider action of the Atkin-Lehner operator
w_{\pi}=\begin{pmatrix}&1\\
q^{n}&\end{pmatrix}
on vectors of the form
\chi 1_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{q}^{\times}}
for
\chi
a character of
{\mathbb{Z}}_{q}^{\times}
.

For
f\in\pi
, and
\chi
a character of
{\mathbb{Z}}_{q}^{\times}
, write

\widehat{f}(\chi,t)=\sum_{n\in{\mathbb{Z}}}\widehat{f}_{n}(\chi)t^{n},

where
\widehat{f}_{n}(\chi)=\int_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{q}^{\times}}\chi(x)f(q^{n}x)dx
. The action of
w:=\begin{pmatrix}&1\\
-1&\end{pmatrix}
on
f
is given by

\widehat{\pi(w)f}(\chi,t)=C(\chi,t)\widehat{f}(\chi^{-1},t^{-1})

for

C(\chi,q^{s-1/2})=\frac{L(1-s,{\pi}\otimes\chi)\epsilon(s,\pi\otimes\chi^{-1},\psi)}{L(s,\pi\otimes\chi^{-1})}.

Here
\chi
is viewed as a character of
{\mathbb{Q}}_{q}^{\times}
by
\chi(q)=1
,
L(s,\pi\otimes\chi^{-1})
and

\epsilon(s,\pi\otimes\chi^{-1},\psi)
are the
L
- and epsilon-factors associated to
\pi\otimes\chi^{-1}
respectively. (cf.
[
52
]
lines above Corollary 2.19.)

Proposition 7.8
.

Let
\chi
be a character of
\mathbb{Q}_{q}^{\times}
as above and
f_{\chi}=\chi 1_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{q}^{\times}}\in\pi
.
Suppose that
\mathrm{cond^{r}}(\pi\otimes\chi)=\mathrm{cond^{r}}(\pi)=q^{n}
,
n\geq 2
.
Then we have

\pi(w)f_{\chi}=\epsilon(1/2,\pi\otimes\chi^{-1},\psi)\chi^{-1}(q^{n}\cdot)1_{q^{-n}{\mathbb{Z}}_{q}^{\times}}.

In particular,
\pi(w_{\pi})f_{\chi}=\pi\left(\begin{pmatrix}1&\\
&-q^{n}\end{pmatrix}w\right)f=\epsilon(1/2,\pi\otimes\chi^{-1},\psi)\chi(-1)f_{\chi^{-1}}
.

Proof.

To determine
\pi(w)f_{\chi}
, it suffices to consider
\widehat{\pi(w)f_{\chi}}(\nu,t)
for all characters
\nu
of
{\mathbb{Z}}_{q}^{\times}
.

Since
\mathrm{cond^{r}}{(\pi\otimes\chi)}=\mathrm{cond^{r}}{(\pi\otimes\chi^{-1})}\geq q^{2}
, the associated local
L
-factor is just
1
.
Note that
C(\chi,q^{s-1/2})=\epsilon(s,\pi\otimes\chi^{-1},\psi)=\epsilon(1/2,\pi\otimes\chi^{-1},\psi)q^{-n(s-1/2)}
, where we utilise the hypothesis that
\mathrm{cond^{r}}{(\pi\otimes\chi^{-1})}=\mathrm{cond^{r}}{(\pi)}={q^{n}}
.

It follows that

\widehat{\pi(w)f_{\chi}}(\nu,t)=\epsilon(1/2,\pi\otimes\chi^{-1},\psi)t^{-n}\begin{cases}1,&\nu=\chi,\\
0,&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

Therefore

\widehat{\pi(w)f}_{\chi,m}(\nu)=\begin{cases}\epsilon(1/2,\pi\otimes\chi^{-1},\psi),\quad&m=-n,\nu=\chi,\\
0,&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

The proof concludes.
∎

Since
\pi
has trivial central character, we denote
\epsilon(1/2,\pi,\psi)
simply by
\epsilon(\pi)\in\{\pm 1\}
.

7.3.
Toric periods and epsilon factors

Let the setting be as in §
7.1.1
, except that we allow
\pi
to be any unitary irreducible admissible representation of
{\mathrm{PGL}}_{2}({\mathbb{Q}}_{q})
with exponential conductor
n=2m
,
m\geq 2
.

This subsection links the toric period
\gamma_{\theta.u}
with twists of epsilon factors.

7.3.1.
Main result

Theorem 7.9
.

Let
f\in\pi
be a newform. We have

\displaystyle\sum_{x\in O_{K}^{\times}/O_{K,q^{m}}^{\times}}(\pi(\iota(x))f,f)=\frac{q-q\eta(-1)\epsilon(\pi)\epsilon(\pi\otimes\eta)}{q-1}(f,f)

\displaystyle+q^{\lfloor\frac{m}{2}\rfloor}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\chi\in\widehat{({\mathbb{Z}}/q^{m}{\mathbb{Z}})^{\times}}\\
primitive\end{subarray}}\frac{G(\chi,\psi)^{2}}{\varphi(q^{m})^{2}}\chi^{-1}(u^{2}\theta^{2})\epsilon(1/2,\pi\otimes\chi,\psi)(f,f)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\displaystyle 2q^{(m-1)/2}\sum_{v\in 1-q^{m-1}({\mathbb{Z}}/q{\mathbb{Z}})^{\times 2}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\chi\in\widehat{({\mathbb{Z}}/q^{m}{\mathbb{Z}})^{\times}}\\
primitive\end{subarray}}\frac{G(\chi,\psi)^{2}}{\varphi(q^{m})^{2}}\chi^{-1}(u^{2}\theta^{2}v)\epsilon(1/2,\pi\otimes\chi,\psi)(f,f),&\quad\text{$m$ odd},\\
0,&\quad\text{$m$ even}.\end{cases}

Here
v
is viewed as an element in
{\mathbb{Z}}/q^{m}{\mathbb{Z}}
,

\eta
the non-trivial quadratic character
16
16

16

viewed as character of
{\mathbb{Q}}_{q}^{\times}
via
\eta(q)=1
of
{\mathbb{Z}}_{q}^{\times}
, and

G(\eta,\psi)=\sum_{a\in{\mathbb{F}}_{q}^{\times}}\psi\left(\frac{a}{q}\right)\eta(a)
,
G(\chi,\psi)=\sum_{a\in({\mathbb{Z}}/q^{m}{\mathbb{Z}})^{\times}}\psi\left(\frac{a}{q^{m}}\right)\chi(a)
are the Gauss sum.

The result is a simple consequence of the following proposition, whose formulation relies on the fact that
{O_{K}^{\times}/O_{K,q^{m}}^{\times}}
is represented by

\{a+\theta\ |\ a\in{\mathbb{Z}}/q^{m}{\mathbb{Z}}\}\sqcup\{1+b\theta\ |\ b\in q{\mathbb{Z}}/q^{m}{\mathbb{Z}}\}.

Here we view an element of
{\mathbb{Z}}/q^{k}{\mathbb{Z}}
as an element in
{\mathbb{Z}}_{q}
by choosing a lift, a convention often followed.

Proposition 7.10
.

We have the following.

(i)

\sum_{\{a\in{\mathbb{Z}}/q^{m}{\mathbb{Z}}\ |\ q\nmid a\}}(\pi(\iota(a+\theta))f,f)=0.

(ii)

For
1\leq t\leq m
,

\displaystyle\sum_{a\in q^{t}({\mathbb{Z}}/q^{m-t}{\mathbb{Z}})^{\times}}(\pi(\iota(a+\theta))f,f)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\displaystyle 0,&\quad m-2t>1,\\
\displaystyle 2q^{(m-1)/2}\sum_{v\in 1-q^{m-1}({\mathbb{Z}}/q{\mathbb{Z}})^{\times 2}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\chi\in\widehat{({\mathbb{Z}}/q^{m}{\mathbb{Z}})^{\times}}\\
primitive\end{subarray}}\frac{G(\chi,\psi)^{2}}{\varphi(q^{m})^{2}}\chi^{-1}(u^{2}\theta^{2}v)\epsilon(1/2,\pi\otimes\chi,\psi)(f,f),&\quad m=2t+1,\\
\displaystyle\varphi(q^{m-t})\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\chi\in\widehat{({\mathbb{Z}}/q^{m}{\mathbb{Z}})^{\times}}\\
primitive\end{subarray}}\frac{G(\chi,\psi)^{2}}{\varphi(q^{m})^{2}}\chi^{-1}(u^{2}\theta^{2})\epsilon(1/2,\pi\otimes\chi,\psi)(f,f),&\quad m-2t\leq 0.\end{cases}

Here we regard
1-q^{m-1}({\mathbb{Z}}/q{\mathbb{Z}})^{\times 2}\subset({\mathbb{Z}}/q^{m}{\mathbb{Z}})^{\times}
.

(iii)

For
1\leq s\leq m
,

\sum_{\{b\in q{\mathbb{Z}}/q^{m}{\mathbb{Z}}\ |\ q^{s}\parallel b\}}(\pi(\iota(1+b\theta))f,f)=(f,f)\begin{cases}\ 0,&\quad\text{ if $m-s>1$},\\
\frac{1-q\eta(-1)\epsilon(\pi)\epsilon(\pi\otimes\eta)}{q-1},&\quad\text{ if $m-s=1$},\\
f,&\quad\text{ if $m-s=0$}.\\
\end{cases}

Our approach is based on the Kirillov model, which leads to an explicit formula for matrix coefficients of a newform under the action of
\iota(K^{\times})
in terms of twist epsilon factors. Proposition
7.10
is a consequence of Propositions
7.12
,
7.13
,
7.16
,
7.17
and
7.20
below.

Throughout this subsection, identify
\pi
with it’s Kirillov model with respect to an unramified character
\psi
of
{\mathbb{Q}}_{q}
(cf. §
7.2
). Then we may choose
f
to be
1_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{q}^{\times}}
in the Kirillov model since
n\geq 2
(cf. Lemma
7.6
).

By Bruhat decomposition

{\mathrm{GL}}_{2}({\mathbb{Q}}_{q})=B({\mathbb{Q}}_{q})\begin{pmatrix}&q^{-m}\\
q^{m}&\end{pmatrix}N({\mathbb{Q}}_{q})\sqcup B({\mathbb{Q}}_{q}),

where
B
is the subgroup of upper triangle matrices and
N\subset B
the unipotent subgroup.
In view of the Bruhat decomposition and explicit action of
B({\mathbb{Q}}_{q})
on the Kirillov model, it suffices to consider matrix coefficients of

w_{q^{m}}=\begin{pmatrix}&q^{-m}\\
q^{m}&\end{pmatrix}
on the
twist newforms
\chi{\mathbb{Z}}_{q}^{\times}
in
\pi
. An analysis of the latter gives rise to twist epsilon factors (cf. Proposition
7.8
).

As for the explicit matrix coefficients, we separate the analysis into three cases, which correspond to the sub cases of Proposition
7.10
.
We first consider matrix coefficients under the action of
\iota(a+\theta)
with
a\in{\mathbb{Z}}/q^{m}{\mathbb{Z}}
.

7.3.2.
Case I:
a+\theta
with
q\nmid a

We begin with a preliminary.

Lemma 7.11
.

If
a-u\theta^{2}
is a unit, then

\displaystyle(\pi(\iota(a+\theta))f,f)=

\displaystyle\left(\pi\begin{pmatrix}1&\frac{1}{q^{m}}\\
&1\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}&q^{-m}\\
q^{m}&\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}1&\frac{-(a+\theta^{2}u)(a-u\theta^{2})}{q^{m}(a^{2}-\theta^{2})}\\
&1\end{pmatrix}f,f\right).

Proof.

Consider the Bruhat decomposition

\displaystyle\iota(a+\theta)=

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}\frac{a-u\theta^{2}}{\theta^{2}}&\\
&1\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}1&\frac{1}{q^{m}}\\
&1\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}&q^{-m}\\
q^{m}&\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}1&\frac{-(a+\theta^{2}u)(a-u\theta^{2})}{q^{m}(a^{2}-\theta^{2})}\\
&1\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}\theta^{2}&\\
&\frac{-(a^{2}-\theta^{2})}{a-u\theta^{2}}\end{pmatrix}.

Since
a-u\theta^{2}
is a unit, note that
\begin{pmatrix}\frac{a-u\theta^{2}}{\theta^{2}}&\\
&1\end{pmatrix}
and
\begin{pmatrix}\theta^{2}&\\
&\frac{-(a^{2}-\theta^{2})}{a-u\theta^{2}}\end{pmatrix}
are in
B({\mathbb{Z}}_{q})
. As
f
is
B({\mathbb{Z}}_{q})
-invariant, the lemma follows.

∎

We separate the analysis into the following sub cases.

The case
q|a+u\theta^{2}

Proposition 7.12
.

Let
1\leq r\leq m
, and
C_{r}=\{a\in{\mathbb{Z}}/q^{m}{\mathbb{Z}}\ |\ q^{r}\parallel a+u\theta^{2}\}
.
Then

\sum_{a\in C_{r}}\pi\begin{pmatrix}1&\frac{-(a+\theta^{2}u)(a-u\theta^{2})}{q^{m}(a^{2}-\theta^{2})}\\
&1\end{pmatrix}f=\begin{cases}\ 0,&\quad\text{ if $m-r>1$},\\
-f,&\quad\text{ if $m-r=1$},\\
f,&\quad\text{ if $m-r=0$}.\\
\end{cases}

In particular,

\sum_{\{a\in{\mathbb{Z}}/q^{m}{\mathbb{Z}}\ |\ q|a+u\theta^{2}\}}(\pi(\iota(a+\theta))f,f)=0.

Proof.

For
a\in{\mathbb{Z}}/q^{m}{\mathbb{Z}}
, put
r=v_{q}(a+u\theta^{2})
with
0\leq r\leq m
. Write
a+u\theta^{2}=q^{r}v
.

Note that the unipotent group action does not change the support of newform

f=1_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{q}^{\times}}
.
We have

\sum_{a\in C_{r}}\pi\begin{pmatrix}1&\frac{-(a+\theta^{2}u)(a-u\theta^{2})}{q^{m}(a^{2}-\theta^{2})}\\
&1\end{pmatrix}f(x)=\sum_{v\in({\mathbb{Z}}/q^{m-r}{\mathbb{Z}})^{\times}}\psi\left(\frac{-xv(a-u\theta^{2})}{q^{m-r}(a^{2}-\theta^{2})}\right)f(x),\quad x\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{q}^{\times}.

As
a
runs over
C_{r}
,
v=(a+u\theta^{2})/q^{r}
runs over
({\mathbb{Z}}/q^{m-r}{\mathbb{Z}})^{\times}
.
For a fixed
x\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{q}^{\times}
, consider

\delta:({\mathbb{Z}}/q^{m-r}{\mathbb{Z}})^{\times}\rightarrow({\mathbb{Z}}/q^{m-r}{\mathbb{Z}})^{\times},\quad v\mapsto\frac{-v(a-u\theta^{2})}{(a^{2}-\theta^{2})}=\frac{-v(q^{r}v-2u\theta^{2})}{q^{r}v(q^{r}v-2u\theta^{2})+u^{2}\theta^{4}-\theta^{2}}.

Note that
\delta
is an isomorphism since
a-u\theta^{2}
and
a^{2}-\theta^{2}
are units.

Thus

\begin{aligned} \sum_{a\in C_{r}}\pi\begin{pmatrix}1&\frac{-(a+\theta^{2}u)(a-u\theta^{2})}{q^{m}(a^{2}-\theta^{2})}\\
&1\end{pmatrix}f(x)=&\left(\sum_{v\in({\mathbb{Z}}/q^{m-r}{\mathbb{Z}})^{\times}}\psi\left(\frac{\delta(v)x}{q^{m-r}}\right)\right)f(x)\quad(w=\delta(v))\\
=&\left(\sum_{w\in({\mathbb{Z}}/q^{m-r}{\mathbb{Z}})^{\times}}\psi\left(\frac{w}{q^{m-r}}\right)\right)f(x).\end{aligned}.

Since
\psi
is an unramified character of
{\mathbb{Q}}_{q}
,
\psi(\frac{w}{q^{m-r}})
runs over all
q^{m-r}
-th primitive roots of unity, concluding the proof. ‘In particular’ part follows from Lemma
7.11
.
∎

The case
q|a-u\theta^{2}

Proposition 7.13
.

We have

\sum_{\{a\in{\mathbb{Z}}/q^{m}{\mathbb{Z}}\ |\ q|a-u\theta^{2}\}}(\pi(\iota(a+\theta))f,f)=0.

Proof.

Note that
(a+\theta)^{-1}=\frac{-1}{a^{2}-\theta^{2}}(-a+\theta)
. So we have

\displaystyle\sum_{\{a\in{\mathbb{Z}}/q^{m}{\mathbb{Z}}\ |\ q|a-u\theta^{2}\}}(\pi(\iota(a+\theta))f,f)=

\displaystyle\sum_{\{a\in{\mathbb{Z}}/q^{m}{\mathbb{Z}}\ |\ q|a-u\theta^{2}\}}(f,\pi(\iota(a+\theta)^{-1})f)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\{a\in{\mathbb{Z}}/q^{m}{\mathbb{Z}}\ |\ q|a+u\theta^{2}\}}(f,\pi(\iota({a}+\theta)){f}).\quad

The latter vanishes by Proposition
7.12
.
∎

The case
q\nmid a(a^{2}-u^{2}\theta^{4})

For this remaining case, the main result is Proposition
7.16
below.

In view of Lemma
7.11
we consider the map

\kappa:\{a\in{\mathbb{Z}}/q^{m}{\mathbb{Z}}\ \Big|\ a\equiv\hskip-10.0pt/\ \pm u\theta^{2}\pmod{q}\}\rightarrow({\mathbb{Z}}/q^{m}{\mathbb{Z}})^{\times},\quad a\mapsto\frac{-(a+\theta^{2}u)(a-u\theta^{2})}{a^{2}-\theta^{2}}=\frac{(u\theta^{2})^{2}-\theta^{2}}{a^{2}-\theta^{2}}-1.

For
c\in\{1,\cdots,q-1\}
not congruent to
\pm u\theta^{2}
modulo
{q}
, its restriction to

S_{c}=\{a\in{\mathbb{Z}}/q^{m}{\mathbb{Z}}\ \Big|\ a\equiv c\pmod{q}\}

is given by the following.

Fact 7.14
.

\kappa(S_{c})
is a fiber of the natural projection map
({\mathbb{Z}}/q^{m}{\mathbb{Z}})^{\times}\rightarrow({\mathbb{Z}}/q{\mathbb{Z}})^{\times}
.

Proof.

Note that
\kappa(a)=\kappa(a^{\prime})
if and only if
a^{2}\equiv a^{\prime 2}\pmod{q^{m}}
, thus
\kappa|_{S_{c}}
is injective. Moreover, the image
\kappa(S_{c})
is constant modulo
q
. Therefore
\kappa(S_{c})
is the fiber of the projection map
({\mathbb{Z}}/q^{m}{\mathbb{Z}})^{\times}\rightarrow({\mathbb{Z}}/q{\mathbb{Z}})^{\times}
by comparing the cardinality.
∎

The following fact will also be useful.

Fact 7.15
.

Let
k\geq 1
be an integer and
\zeta
a
q^{k}
-th primitive root of unity, and
s\leq k
an integer. Then for
a\in{\mathbb{Z}}/q^{k}{\mathbb{Z}}
,

\sum_{b\in{\mathbb{Z}}/q^{k-s}{\mathbb{Z}}}\zeta^{a+q^{s}b}=\zeta^{a}\sum_{b\in{\mathbb{Z}}/q^{k-s}{\mathbb{Z}}}(\zeta^{q^{s}})^{b}=\begin{cases}0,\quad&\text{$k>s$},\\
\zeta^{a},\quad&\text{$k=s$}.\\
\end{cases}

Proposition 7.16
.

For each
c\in\{1,\cdots,q-1\}
not congruent to
\pm u\theta^{2}
modulo
{q}
, we have

\sum_{a\in S_{c}}(\pi(\iota(a+\theta))f,f)=0

Proof.

In view of Lemma
7.11
, it suffices to consider

\displaystyle\sum_{a\in S_{c}}\pi\begin{pmatrix}1&\frac{-(a+\theta^{2}u)(a-u\theta^{2})}{q^{m}(a^{2}-\theta^{2})}\\
&1\end{pmatrix}f(x)=

\displaystyle\sum_{a\in S_{c}}\pi\begin{pmatrix}1&\frac{\kappa(a)}{q^{m}}\\
&1\end{pmatrix}f(x)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{a\in S_{c}}\psi\left(\frac{\kappa(a)x}{q^{m}}\right)f(x),

where
x\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{q}^{\times}
.
The latter vanishes
17
17

17

by taking
m=k
,
s=1
and
\zeta=\psi(x/q^{m})
by Fact
7.15
since
\kappa(S_{c})
is a fiber of the projection map
({\mathbb{Z}}/q^{m}{\mathbb{Z}})^{\times}\rightarrow({\mathbb{Z}}/q{\mathbb{Z}})^{\times}
in view of Fact
7.14
.

Hence, the assertion follows from Lemma
7.11
.
∎

7.3.3.
Case II:
a+\theta
with
q\mid a

Proposition 7.17
.

For
1\leq t\leq m
, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{a\in q^{t}({\mathbb{Z}}/q^{m-t}{\mathbb{Z}})^{\times}}(\pi(\iota(a+\theta))f,f)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\displaystyle 0,&\quad m-2t>1,\\
\displaystyle 2q^{(m-1)/2}\sum_{v\in 1-q^{m-1}({\mathbb{Z}}/q{\mathbb{Z}})^{\times 2}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\chi\in\widehat{({\mathbb{Z}}/q^{m}{\mathbb{Z}})^{\times}}\\
primitive\end{subarray}}\frac{G(\chi,\psi)^{2}}{\varphi(q^{m})^{2}}\chi^{-1}(u^{2}\theta^{2}v)\epsilon(1/2,\pi\otimes\chi,\psi)(f,f),&\quad m=2t+1,\\
\displaystyle\varphi(q^{m-t})\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\chi\in\widehat{({\mathbb{Z}}/q^{m}{\mathbb{Z}})^{\times}}\\
primitive\end{subarray}}\frac{G(\chi,\psi)^{2}}{\varphi(q^{m})^{2}}\chi^{-1}(u^{2}\theta^{2})\epsilon(1/2,\pi\otimes\chi,\psi)(f,f),&\quad m-2t\leq 0.\end{cases}

Here we regard
1-q^{m-1}({\mathbb{Z}}/q{\mathbb{Z}})^{\times 2}\subset({\mathbb{Z}}/q^{m}{\mathbb{Z}})^{\times}
.

We begin with some preliminaries.

First, note that Lemma
7.11
still applies.
Put
t=v_{q}(a)\in\{1,\cdots,m\}
and consider the map

\kappa_{t}:q^{t}({\mathbb{Z}}/q^{m-t}{\mathbb{Z}})^{\times}\rightarrow({\mathbb{Z}}/q^{m}{\mathbb{Z}})^{\times},\quad a\mapsto\frac{(u\theta^{2})^{2}-\theta^{2}}{a^{2}-\theta^{2}}-1,

(7.1)

where we regard
q^{t}({\mathbb{Z}}/q^{m-t}{\mathbb{Z}})^{\times}\subset({\mathbb{Z}}/q^{m}{\mathbb{Z}})
.

Fact 7.18
.

(i)

For
t\in\{1,\cdots,m\}
with
m\leq 2t
, we have
\kappa_{t}=-\theta^{2}u^{2}
.

(ii)

For
t\in\{1,\cdots,m\}
with
m\geq 2t+1
and
r\in\{1,\cdots,q-1\}
, put

S_{t,r}=q^{t}r(1+q{\mathbb{Z}}_{q})/(1+q^{m-t}{\mathbb{Z}}_{q}).

(a)

If
m-2t=1
, then
\kappa_{t}|_{S_{t,r}}=\frac{(u\theta^{2})^{2}-\theta^{2}}{q^{2t}r^{2}-\theta^{2}}-1
.

(b)

If
m>2t+1
, then

\kappa_{t}({S_{t,r}})=\{y\in({\mathbb{Z}}/q^{m}{\mathbb{Z}})^{\times}\ |\ y\equiv\frac{(u\theta^{2})^{2}-\theta^{2}}{q^{2t}r^{2}-\theta^{2}}-1\pmod{q^{2t+1}}\},

which is a fiber of the projection map
({\mathbb{Z}}/q^{m}{\mathbb{Z}})^{\times}\rightarrow({\mathbb{Z}}/q^{2t+1}{\mathbb{Z}})^{\times}.
Furthermore, the function

\kappa_{t}|_{S_{t,r}}
is exactly
q^{t}
to
1
.

Proof.

The assertions in parts (i) and (a) are apparent.
The following considers (b).

Note that
\kappa_{t}
can be written as a composite

\kappa_{t}:q^{t}({\mathbb{Z}}/q^{m-t}{\mathbb{Z}})^{\times}{\xrightarrow{j}}({\mathbb{Z}}/q^{m+t}{\mathbb{Z}})^{\times}\rightarrow({\mathbb{Z}}/q^{m}{\mathbb{Z}})^{\times},

where the first map
j
is
a\mapsto\frac{(u\theta^{2})^{2}-\theta^{2}}{a^{2}-\theta^{2}}-1
and the second natural quotient.
It is enough to show that
j
restricted to
S_{t,r}
is injective and its image is

\left\{y\in({\mathbb{Z}}/q^{m+t}{\mathbb{Z}})^{\times}\ |\ y\equiv\frac{(u\theta^{2})^{2}-\theta^{2}}{q^{2t}r^{2}-\theta^{2}}-1\pmod{q^{2t+1}}\right\},

which is a fiber of the projective map
({\mathbb{Z}}/q^{m+t}{\mathbb{Z}})^{\times}\rightarrow({\mathbb{Z}}/q^{2t+1}{\mathbb{Z}})^{\times}.

Now we prove the claim. The image of
j
modulo
q^{2t+1}
is the constant
\frac{(u\theta^{2})^{2}-\theta^{2}}{q^{2t}r^{2}-\theta^{2}}-1
. Note that if
a,a^{\prime}\in S_{t,r}
have the same image then
a^{2}=a^{\prime 2}\pmod{q^{m+t}}
and so
a=a^{\prime}\in S_{t,r}
.
Hence
j
restricted to
S_{t,r}
is injective, and the claim follows by comparing the cardinality.

∎

Lemma 7.19
.

For
1\leq t\leq m
, we have

\sum_{a\in q^{t}({\mathbb{Z}}/q^{m-t}{\mathbb{Z}})^{\times}}\pi\begin{pmatrix}1&\frac{-(a+\theta^{2}u)(a-u\theta^{2})}{q^{m}(a^{2}-\theta^{2})}\\
&1\end{pmatrix}f=\begin{cases}0,&\quad m-2t>1,\\
\displaystyle 2q^{(m-1)/2}\sum_{v\in 1-q^{m-1}({\mathbb{Z}}/q{\mathbb{Z}})^{\times 2}}\pi\begin{pmatrix}1&\frac{-u^{2}\theta^{2}v}{q^{m}}\\
&1\end{pmatrix}f,&\quad m-2t=1,\\
\varphi(q^{m-t})\pi\begin{pmatrix}1&\frac{-u^{2}\theta^{2}}{q^{m}}\\
&1\end{pmatrix}f,&\quad m-2t\leq 0.\\
\end{cases}

Proof.

Fix
t\geq 1
.
As before, we may take
f=1_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{q}^{\times}}
.

For
x\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{q}^{\times}
and
\kappa_{t}
as in (
7.1
),

\displaystyle\sum_{a\in q^{t}({\mathbb{Z}}/q^{m-t}{\mathbb{Z}})^{\times}}\pi\begin{pmatrix}1&\frac{-(a+\theta^{2}u)(a-u\theta^{2})}{q^{m}(a^{2}-\theta^{2})}\\
&1\end{pmatrix}f(x)=

\displaystyle\sum_{a\in q^{t}({\mathbb{Z}}/q^{m-t}{\mathbb{Z}})^{\times}}\psi\left(\frac{\kappa_{t}(a)x}{q^{m}}\right)f(x).

In view of Fact
7.18
the following holds.

(i)

If
m-2t\leq 0
, the image of

q^{t}({\mathbb{Z}}/q^{m-t}{\mathbb{Z}})^{\times}\rightarrow({\mathbb{Z}}/q^{m}{\mathbb{Z}})^{\times},\quad a\mapsto\frac{-(a+\theta^{2}u)(a-u\theta^{2})}{(a^{2}-\theta^{2})}

is the constant

-\theta^{2}u^{2},
and the third case follows.

(ii)

If
m-2t=1
, then
\kappa
on each
S_{t,r}
is the constant
18
18

18

To see this congruence, note that

\frac{1}{q^{m-1}r^{2}-\theta^{2}}\equiv-\frac{(\theta^{2}+q^{m-1}r^{2})}{\theta^{4}}\pmod{q^{m}}
,
where
m\geq 2
and
q^{m-1}r^{2}-\theta^{2}\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{q}^{\times}
, and so

\displaystyle\frac{(u\theta^{2})^{2}-\theta^{2}}{q^{m-1}r^{2}-\theta^{2}}-1\equiv

\displaystyle-\theta^{2}u^{2}\left(1-q^{m-1}r^{2}\left(\frac{1}{\theta^{2}}-\frac{1}{u^{2}\theta^{4}}\right)\right)\pmod{q^{m}}.

\frac{(u\theta^{2})^{2}-\theta^{2}}{q^{m-1}r^{2}-\theta^{2}}-1\equiv-\theta^{2}u^{2}\left(1-q^{m-1}\frac{r^{2}(u^{2}\theta^{2}-1)}{\theta^{4}u^{2}}\right)\pmod{q^{m}},

where
S_{t,r}
is as in Fact
7.18
.

As
r
varies in
\{1,\cdots q-1\}
, note that
\frac{r^{2}(u^{2}\theta^{2}-1)}{\theta^{4}u^{2}}
varies over
({\mathbb{Z}}/q{\mathbb{Z}})^{\times 2}
. Thus,

\displaystyle\sum_{a\in q^{t}({\mathbb{Z}}/q^{m-t}{\mathbb{Z}})^{\times}}\pi\begin{pmatrix}1&\frac{-(a+\theta^{2}u)(a-u\theta^{2})}{q^{m}(a^{2}-\theta^{2})}\\
&1\end{pmatrix}f

\displaystyle=\sum_{r=1}^{q-1}\sum_{a\in S_{t,r}}\pi\begin{pmatrix}1&\frac{-(a+\theta^{2}u)(a-u\theta^{2})}{q^{m}(a^{2}-\theta^{2})}\\
&1\end{pmatrix}f

\displaystyle=\sum_{r=1}^{q-1}\#S_{t,r}\cdot\pi\begin{pmatrix}1&\frac{-\theta^{2}u^{2}(1-q^{m-1}\frac{r^{2}(u^{2}\theta^{2}-1)}{\theta^{4}u^{2}})}{q^{m}}\\
&1\end{pmatrix}f

\displaystyle=q^{(m-1)/2}\cdot\sum_{w\in({\mathbb{Z}}/q{\mathbb{Z}})^{\times}}\pi\begin{pmatrix}1&\frac{-\theta^{2}u^{2}(1-q^{m-1}w^{2})}{q^{m}}\\
&1\end{pmatrix}f

\displaystyle=2q^{(m-1)/2}\cdot\sum_{v\in 1-q^{m-1}({\mathbb{Z}}/q{\mathbb{Z}})^{\times 2}}\pi\begin{pmatrix}1&\frac{-\theta^{2}u^{2}v}{q^{m}}\\
&1\end{pmatrix}f.

(iii)

Suppose that
m-2t>1
. Then the map
\kappa_{t}
on each
S_{t,r}
is
q^{t}
to
1
and the image
\kappa_{t}(S_{t,r})
is a fiber of the projection
({\mathbb{Z}}/q^{m}{\mathbb{Z}})^{\times}\rightarrow({\mathbb{Z}}/q^{2t+1}{\mathbb{Z}})^{\times}

by Fact
7.18
. Thus it follows from Fact
7.15

that

\sum_{a\in S_{t,r}}\psi\left(\frac{\kappa_{t}(a)x}{q^{m}}\right)f(x)=0

for each
r
, concluding the proof of first case.

∎

Proof of Proposition
7.17
.

In light of Lemmas
7.11
and
7.19
, it suffices to show:
for
v\in({\mathbb{Z}}/q^{m}{\mathbb{Z}})^{\times}
,

\displaystyle\left(\pi\begin{pmatrix}1&\frac{1}{q^{m}}\\
&1\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}&q^{-m}\\
q^{m}&\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}1&\frac{-\theta^{2}u^{2}v}{q^{m}}\\
&1\end{pmatrix}f,f\right)=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\chi\in\widehat{({\mathbb{Z}}/q^{m}{\mathbb{Z}})^{\times}}\\
primitive\end{subarray}}\frac{G(\chi,\psi)^{2}}{\varphi(q^{m})^{2}}\chi^{-1}(u^{2}\theta^{2}v)\epsilon(1/2,\pi\otimes\chi,\psi)(f,f).

We have

\displaystyle\pi\begin{pmatrix}1&\frac{-\theta^{2}u^{2}v}{q^{m}}\\
&1\end{pmatrix}f=

\displaystyle\psi\left(\frac{-\theta^{2}u^{2}v\cdot}{q^{m}}\right)1_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{q^{\times}}}(\cdot)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\chi\in\widehat{({\mathbb{Z}}/q^{m}{\mathbb{Z}})^{\times}}\end{subarray}}\frac{G(\chi^{-1},\psi)}{\varphi(q^{m})}\chi(-u^{2}\theta^{2}v)f_{\chi}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\chi\in\widehat{({\mathbb{Z}}/q^{m}{\mathbb{Z}})^{\times}}\\
primitive\end{subarray}}\frac{G(\chi^{-1},\psi)}{\varphi(q^{m})}\chi(-u^{2}\theta^{2}v)f_{\chi}.

Here the second equality just amounts to Fourier expansion
19
19

19

For functions
f,h
on a finite abelian group
G
, let
(\ ,\ )_{G}
be the natural Hermitian pairing on
G
given by
(f,h)_{G}=\sum_{g\in G}f(g)\overline{h}(g)
. Then we have

f=\sum_{\chi\in\widehat{G}}{\frac{(f,\chi)_{G}}{(\chi,\chi)_{G}}}\chi.

, and
the last equality follows from the fact that the Gauss sum

G(\chi^{-1},\psi):=\sum_{u\in({\mathbb{Z}}/q^{m}{\mathbb{Z}})^{\times}}\chi^{-1}(u)\psi(u/q^{m})

is non-zero only for primitive
\chi\in\widehat{({\mathbb{Z}}/q^{m}{\mathbb{Z}})^{\times}}
.
Similarly,

\pi\begin{pmatrix}1&\frac{-1}{q^{m}}\\
&1\end{pmatrix}f=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\chi\in\widehat{({\mathbb{Z}}/q^{m}{\mathbb{Z}})^{\times}}\\
primitive\end{subarray}}\frac{G(\chi^{-1},\psi)}{\varphi(q^{m})}\chi(-1)f_{\chi}.

Now we have

\displaystyle\left(\pi\begin{pmatrix}1&\frac{1}{q^{m}}\\
&1\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}&q^{-m}\\
q^{m}&\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}1&\frac{-\theta^{2}u^{2}v}{q^{m}}\\
&1\end{pmatrix}f,f\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\pi(w_{q^{m}})\pi\begin{pmatrix}1&\frac{-\theta^{2}u^{2}v}{q^{m}}\\
&1\end{pmatrix}f,\pi\begin{pmatrix}1&\frac{-1}{q^{m}}\\
&1\end{pmatrix}f\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\pi(w_{q^{m}})\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\chi\in\widehat{({\mathbb{Z}}/q^{m}{\mathbb{Z}})^{\times}}\\
primitive\end{subarray}}\frac{G(\chi^{-1},\psi)}{\varphi(q^{m})}\chi(-u^{2}\theta^{2}v)f_{\chi},\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\chi\in\widehat{({\mathbb{Z}}/q^{m}{\mathbb{Z}})^{\times}}\\
primitive\end{subarray}}\frac{G(\chi^{-1},\psi)}{\varphi(q^{m})}\chi(-1)f_{\chi}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\chi\in\widehat{({\mathbb{Z}}/q^{m}{\mathbb{Z}})^{\times}}\\
primitive\end{subarray}}\frac{G(\chi,\psi)^{2}}{\varphi(q^{m})^{2}}\chi^{-1}(u^{2}\theta^{2}v)\epsilon(1/2,\pi\otimes\chi,\psi)(f,f).

Here the last equality just follows from Proposition
7.8
, and the facts that
G(\chi^{-1},\psi)\chi^{-1}(-1)=\overline{G(\chi,\psi)}
and
(\ ,\ )
is a Hermitian pairing on
\pi
.
∎

7.3.4.
Case III:
1+b\theta

This subsection considers
\iota(1+b\theta)
for
b\in q{\mathbb{Z}}/q^{m}{\mathbb{Z}}
.

The main result:

Proposition 7.20
.

For
1\leq s\leq m
, we have

\sum_{\{b\in q{\mathbb{Z}}/q^{m}{\mathbb{Z}}\ |\ q^{s}\parallel b\}}(\pi(\iota(1+b\theta))f,f)=(f,f)\begin{cases}\ 0,&\quad\text{ if $m-s>1$},\\
\frac{1-q\eta(-1)\epsilon(\pi)\epsilon(\pi\otimes\eta)}{q-1},&\quad\text{ if $m-s=1$},\\
f,&\quad\text{ if $m-s=0$}.\\
\end{cases}

We begin with some preliminaries.

Lemma 7.21
.

Let
b\in q{\mathbb{Z}}/q^{m}{\mathbb{Z}}
. Write
b=q^{s}w
with
1\leq s\leq m
and
w
in
({\mathbb{Z}}/q^{m-s}{\mathbb{Z}})^{\times}
. Then

(\pi(\iota(1+b\theta))f,f)=\epsilon(\pi)\left(\pi\begin{pmatrix}1&\frac{1}{q^{m-s}}\\
&1\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}&q^{-m}\\
q^{m}&\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}1&\frac{\theta^{2}w^{2}(1-u^{2}\theta^{2})}{q^{m-s}(1-b^{2}\theta^{2})}\\
&1\end{pmatrix}f,f\right).

Proof.

Consider the Bruhat decomposition

\iota(1+b\theta)w_{q^{m}}=\begin{pmatrix}\frac{w(1-u^{2}\theta^{2})}{(1+b\theta^{2}u)}&\\
&1\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}1&\frac{1}{q^{m-s}}\\
&1\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}&q^{-m}\\
q^{m}&\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}1&\frac{\theta^{2}w^{2}(1-u^{2}\theta^{2})}{q^{m-s}(1-b^{2}\theta^{2})}\\
&1\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}1+b\theta^{2}u&\\
&\frac{1-b^{2}\theta^{2}}{w(1-u^{2}\theta^{2})}\end{pmatrix}.

Note that
f
is fixed by
B({\mathbb{Z}}_{q})
and

\pi(w_{q^{m}})f=\epsilon(\pi)f
, concluding the proof.
∎

In view of Lemma
7.21
we are led to the following.

Lemma 7.22
.

For
1\leq s\leq m
, we have

\sum_{\{b\in q{\mathbb{Z}}/q^{m}{\mathbb{Z}}\ |\ q^{s}\parallel b\}}\pi\begin{pmatrix}1&\frac{\theta^{2}w^{2}(1-u^{2}\theta^{2})}{q^{m-s}(1-b^{2}\theta^{2})}\\
&1\end{pmatrix}f=\begin{cases}\ 0,&\quad\text{ if $m-s>1$},\\
-f-\eta(-1)G(\eta,\psi)f_{\eta},&\quad\text{ if $m-s=1$},\\
f,&\quad\text{ if $m-s=0$}.\\
\end{cases}

Here
w=b/q^{s}
with
w\in({\mathbb{Z}}/q^{m-s})^{\times}
,
\eta
is the non-trivial quadratic character of
{\mathbb{Z}}_{q}^{\times}
,
and
f_{\eta}=\eta 1_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{q^{\times}}}
in the Kirillov model of
\pi
.

Proof.

For
q^{s}\parallel b
, write
b=q^{s}w
with
w\in({\mathbb{Z}}/q^{m-s}{\mathbb{Z}})^{\times}
.

We have

\pi\begin{pmatrix}1&\frac{\theta^{2}w^{2}(1-u^{2}\theta^{2})}{q^{m-s}(1-b^{2}\theta^{2})}\\
&1\end{pmatrix}f(x)=\psi\left(\frac{w^{2}}{q^{m-s}(1-q^{2s}w^{2}\theta^{2})}\cdot{x\theta^{2}(1-u^{2}\theta^{2})}\right)f(x).

Since
\theta^{2}(1-u^{2}\theta^{2})\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{q}^{\times}
,
the third case follows.

Suppose that
m-s=1
. For
x\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{q}^{\times}
, put
k=\frac{\theta^{2}w^{2}(u^{2}\theta^{2}-1)}{1-b^{2}\theta^{2}}
. We have

\displaystyle\sum_{\{b\in q{\mathbb{Z}}/q^{m}{\mathbb{Z}}\ |\ q^{m-1}\parallel b\}}\pi\begin{pmatrix}1&\frac{\theta^{2}w^{2}(1-u^{2}\theta^{2})}{q^{m-s}(1-b^{2}\theta^{2})}\\
&1\end{pmatrix}f(x)=

\displaystyle 2\sum_{k\in{\mathbb{F}}_{q}^{\times}\backslash{\mathbb{F}}_{q}^{\times 2}}\psi\left(\frac{-xk}{q}\right)f(x)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{k\in{\mathbb{F}}_{q}^{\times}}\psi\left(\frac{-xk}{q}\right)(1-\eta(k))f(x)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-f-\eta(-1)G(\eta,\psi)f_{\eta}.

Here the first equality relies on
u^{2}\theta^{2}-1\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{q}^{\times 2}
,
\theta^{2}\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{q}^{\times}\backslash{\mathbb{Z}}_{q}^{\times 2}
and
1-b^{2}\theta^{2}\equiv 1\pmod{q^{m}}
under the assumption
q^{m-1}\parallel b
and
m\geq 2
.

Now suppose that
m-s\geq 2
.
Consider the map

\delta:({\mathbb{Z}}/q^{m-s}{\mathbb{Z}})^{\times}\rightarrow({\mathbb{Z}}/q^{m-s}{\mathbb{Z}})^{\times},\quad w\mapsto\frac{w^{2}}{1-q^{2s}w^{2}\theta^{2}}.

Note that the map
\delta
is
2
to
1
and its image is a disjoint union of fiber of the natural projection map

({\mathbb{Z}}/q^{m-s}{\mathbb{Z}})^{\times}\rightarrow({\mathbb{Z}}/q{\mathbb{Z}})^{\times}
. To see this claim, note that
\delta(w)=\delta(w^{\prime})
if and only if
w^{2}=w^{\prime 2}
in
({\mathbb{Z}}/q^{m-s}{\mathbb{Z}})^{\times}
. On the other hand, the image of the projection map

({\mathbb{Z}}/q^{m-s}{\mathbb{Z}})^{\times}\rightarrow({\mathbb{Z}}/q{\mathbb{Z}})^{\times},\quad w\mapsto\frac{w^{2}}{1-q^{2s}w^{2}\theta^{2}}

is
{\mathbb{F}}_{q}^{\times 2}
. Comparing the cardinality, the claim follows.

If
m-s>1
, then the first case follows form the preceding paragraph and Fact
7.15
: for each fiber
S
of the projection map, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{w\in\delta^{-1}(S)}\psi\left(\frac{w^{2}}{q^{m-s}(1-q^{2s}w^{2}\theta^{2})}\cdot{x\theta^{2}(1-u^{2}\theta^{2})}\right)=

\displaystyle 2\sum_{v\in S}\psi\left(\frac{v}{q^{m-s}}\cdot{x\theta^{2}(1-u^{2}\theta^{2})}\right),

which vanishes by Fact
7.15
.

∎

Proof of Proposition
7.20
.

While the first case is just a consequence of
Lemmas
7.21
and
7.22
,
the third follows from Lemmas
7.21
, and
7.22
and the fact that

\pi\begin{pmatrix}&q^{-m}\\
q^{m}&\end{pmatrix}f=\epsilon(\pi)f.

Now we consider the second case. In view of Lemmas
7.21
and
7.22
it suffices to show that

\displaystyle\left(\pi\begin{pmatrix}1&{q^{-1}}\\
&1\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}&q^{-m}\\
q^{m}&\end{pmatrix}(-f-\eta(-1)G(\eta,\psi)f_{\eta}),f\right)=

\displaystyle\frac{\epsilon(\pi)-q\eta(-1)\epsilon(\pi\otimes\eta)}{q-1}\left(f,f\right).

(7.2)

By Proposition
7.8
,

\pi\begin{pmatrix}&q^{-m}\\
q^{m}&\end{pmatrix}f_{\eta}=\epsilon(\pi\otimes\eta)\eta(-1)f_{\eta},

since
\eta=\eta^{-1}
, and so

\displaystyle\left(\pi\begin{pmatrix}1&q^{-1}\\
&1\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}&q^{-m}\\
q^{m}&\end{pmatrix}(-f-\eta(-1)G(\eta,\psi)f_{\eta}),f\right)=

\displaystyle\left(\pi\begin{pmatrix}1&q^{-1}\\
&1\end{pmatrix}(-\epsilon(\pi)f-G(\eta,\psi)\epsilon(\pi\otimes\eta)f_{\eta}),f\right).

Note that

\displaystyle{\pi}\left(\begin{pmatrix}1&q^{-1}\\
&1\end{pmatrix}f_{\eta}(x),f\right)=

\displaystyle\left(\frac{1}{q-1}\sum_{u\in({\mathbb{Z}}/q{\mathbb{Z}})^{\times}}{\pi}\begin{pmatrix}u&uq^{-1}\\
&1\end{pmatrix}f_{\eta}(x),f\right)=

\displaystyle\frac{{G(\eta,\psi)}}{q-1}(f,f)

and that

\displaystyle\left({\pi}\begin{pmatrix}1&q^{-1}\\
&1\end{pmatrix}f,f\right)=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{q-1}\sum_{u\in{\mathbb{F}}_{q}^{\times}}\left({\pi}\begin{pmatrix}u&\\
&1\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}1&\frac{1}{q}\\
&1\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}u^{-1}&\\
&1\end{pmatrix}f,f\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{q-1}\sum_{u\in{\mathbb{F}}_{q}^{\times}}\left({\pi}\begin{pmatrix}1&\frac{u}{q}\\
&1\end{pmatrix}f,f\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{q-1}(f,f).

Here the last equality follows from the defining action of unipotent elements, and the fact that summation of the
q
-th primitive root of unity is
-1
.

Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\left(\pi\begin{pmatrix}1&q^{-1}\\
&1\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}&q^{-m}\\
q^{m}&\end{pmatrix}(-f-\eta(-1)G(\eta,\psi)f_{\eta}),f\right)=

\displaystyle\left(\pi\begin{pmatrix}1&q^{-1}\\
&1\end{pmatrix}(-\epsilon(\pi)f-G(\eta,\psi)\epsilon(\pi\otimes\eta)f_{\eta}),f\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\epsilon(\pi)-q\eta(-1)\epsilon(\pi\otimes\eta)}{q-1}\left(f,f\right).

Here the last equality uses
G(\eta,\psi)^{2}=q\eta(-1)
for
\eta
quadratic, concluding the proof of (
7.2
).

∎

7.4.
Explicit toric period formula

Let the setting be as in §
7.1.1
.
This subsection concludes the proof of Theorem
7.1
for toric period of newform. It is a consequence of
Theorem
7.9
in combination with Lemma
7.23
and Proposition
7.29
below, the latter being an explicit formula for twisted epsilon factors appearing in Theorem
7.1
. It crucially relies on
\pi=\pi_{\lambda}
being supercuspidal.

7.4.1.
Epsilon factors

Lemma 7.23
.

For
\eta
the non-trivial quadratic character of
{\mathbb{Z}}_{q}^{\times}
,
we have

\epsilon(\pi\otimes\eta)=-\eta(-1)\epsilon(\pi).

Proof.

Recall that
\pi=\pi_{\lambda}
. Since
K
and
\psi
are unramified and
q
odd,

\epsilon(\pi)=\epsilon(1/2,\pi,\psi)=\lambda_{K}(\psi)\epsilon(1/2,\lambda,\psi_{K})=(-1)^{m}\lambda(\theta)

for
\lambda_{K}(\psi)=\frac{\int_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{q}^{\times}}\eta_{K}(u)\psi(u)du}{|\int_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{q}^{\times}}\eta_{K}(u)\psi(u)du|}=1
.
Here the second equality follows from
[
52
, Thm. 4.7]
, and the last from
[
63
, Prop. 3.7]
.

In particular,
comparing the root number of
\epsilon(\pi)
with
\epsilon(\pi\otimes\eta)
,
we have

\epsilon(\pi\otimes\eta)=\eta(N_{K/{\mathbb{Q}}_{q}}(\theta))\epsilon(\pi)=-\eta(-1)\epsilon(\pi).

∎

Lemma 7.24
.

For any
\chi\in\widehat{({\mathbb{Z}}/q^{m}{\mathbb{Z}})^{\times}}
,
the character
\lambda\chi_{K}
also has conductor
q^{m}
and

\epsilon(1/2,\pi\otimes\chi,\psi)=(-1)^{m}\lambda(\theta)\frac{G((\lambda\chi_{K})^{-1},\psi_{K})}{G(\lambda^{-1},\psi_{K})}.

Here
\chi_{K}=\chi\circ{{\rm{N}}_{K/{\mathbb{Q}}}}
,
G({\lambda^{\prime}},\psi_{K}):=\sum_{O_{K}^{\times}/O_{K,q^{m}}^{\times}}{\lambda^{\prime}}(x)\psi_{K}\left(\frac{x}{q^{m}}\right)
with
\psi_{K}={\mathrm{tr}}_{K/{\mathbb{Q}}_{q}}\psi
and
\lambda^{\prime}
a character with conductor
q^{m}
.

Proof.

Note that
\chi_{K}
has conductor
q^{m}
and that
\lambda|_{1+q^{m-1}O_{K}}
does not factor through norm, and so
\lambda\chi_{K}
has conductor
q^{m}
. In particular,
G((\lambda\chi_{K})^{-1},\psi_{K})
is well defined and non-zero.

We have
\pi\otimes\chi=\pi_{\lambda}\otimes\chi=\pi_{\lambda\chi_{K}}
(cf.
[
52
, Thm. 4.7]
).
As in the proof of Lemma
7.23
,

\frac{\epsilon(1/2,\pi_{\lambda\chi_{K}},\psi)}{\epsilon(1/2,\pi,\psi)}=\frac{\epsilon(1/2,\lambda\chi_{K},\psi_{K})}{\epsilon(1/2,\lambda,\psi_{K})}.

In view of the definition of epsilon factor in terms of Gauss sum (cf.
[
63
, p. 281]
) we have

\epsilon(1/2,\pi\otimes\chi,\psi)=\epsilon(1/2,\pi,\psi)\frac{G((\lambda\chi_{K})^{-1},\psi_{K})}{G(\lambda^{-1},\psi_{K})}.

Therefore
[
63
, Prop. 3.7]
concludes the proof.
∎

7.4.2.
Analysis of twist Gauss sum

In this subsection we obtain explicit formulas for the twist Gauss sums appearing in Proposition
7.10
.

Lemma 7.25
.

For
\chi\in\widehat{({\mathbb{Z}}/q^{m}{\mathbb{Z}})^{\times}}
primitive and
v\in({\mathbb{Z}}/q^{m}{\mathbb{Z}})^{\times}
, we have

G(\chi,\psi)^{2}\chi^{-1}(u^{2}\theta^{2}v)\frac{G((\lambda\chi_{K})^{-1},\psi_{K})}{G(\lambda^{-1},\psi_{K})}=\chi(-4{v^{-1}})J(\chi,\chi)\sum_{a\in{\mathbb{Z}}/q^{m}{\mathbb{Z}}}(\lambda\chi_{K})^{-1}(a+\theta u)

and

J(\chi,\chi)=\sum_{x\in({\mathbb{Z}}/q^{m}{\mathbb{Z}})^{\times}}\chi(x)\chi(1-x).

Proof.

Simply denote
\theta u
by
\theta^{\prime}
.
Note that
\overline{\theta^{\prime}}=-\theta^{\prime}
, and
so

\displaystyle\chi^{-1}(u^{2}\theta^{2}v)\frac{G((\lambda\chi_{K})^{-1},\psi_{K})}{G(\lambda^{-1},\psi_{K})}=

\displaystyle\chi(-4{v^{-1}})\frac{G\left((\lambda\chi_{K})^{-1},\psi_{K}\left(\frac{\cdot}{2\theta^{\prime}}\right)\right)}{G\left(\lambda^{-1},\psi_{K}\left(\frac{\cdot}{2\theta^{\prime}}\right)\right)}.

(7.3)

In the following we analyse the Gauss sum in the numerator based on the fact that

O_{K}^{\times}={\mathbb{Z}}_{q}^{\times}\oplus{\mathbb{Z}}_{q}\theta^{\prime}\sqcup q{\mathbb{Z}}_{q}\oplus{\mathbb{Z}}_{q}^{\times}\theta^{\prime}

and
{\mathrm{tr}}_{K/{\mathbb{Q}}_{q}}\left(\frac{a+b\theta^{\prime}}{q^{m}2\theta^{\prime}}\right)=\frac{b}{q^{m}}
.

To begin
G((\lambda\chi_{K})^{-1},\psi_{K}(\frac{\cdot}{2\theta^{\prime}}))=I+J
, where

I=\sum_{a\in({\mathbb{Z}}/q^{m}{\mathbb{Z}})^{\times}}\sum_{b\in{\mathbb{Z}}/q^{m}{\mathbb{Z}}}(\lambda\chi_{K})^{-1}(a+b\theta^{\prime})\psi\left(\frac{b}{q^{m}}\right)

J=\sum_{a\in q{\mathbb{Z}}/q^{m}{\mathbb{Z}}}\sum_{b\in({\mathbb{Z}}/q^{m}{\mathbb{Z}})^{\times}}(\lambda\chi_{K})^{-1}(a+b\theta^{\prime})\psi\left(\frac{b}{q^{m}}\right).

Note that

\displaystyle I=

\displaystyle\sum_{b\in{\mathbb{Z}}/q^{m}{\mathbb{Z}}}(\lambda\chi_{K})^{-1}(1+b\theta^{\prime})\left(\sum_{a\in({\mathbb{Z}}/q^{m}{\mathbb{Z}})^{\times}}\chi^{-2}(a)\psi\left(\frac{ba}{q^{m}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{b\in({\mathbb{Z}}/q^{m}{\mathbb{Z}})^{\times}}(\lambda\chi_{K})^{-1}(1+b\theta^{\prime})\left(\sum_{a\in({\mathbb{Z}}/q^{m}{\mathbb{Z}})^{\times}}\chi^{-2}(a)\psi\left(\frac{ba}{q^{m}}\right)\right).

The last equality follows from:
\sum_{a\in({\mathbb{Z}}/q^{m}{\mathbb{Z}})^{\times}}\chi^{-2}(a)\psi\left(\frac{ba}{q^{m}}\right)
is non-zero only for
b\in({\mathbb{Z}}/q^{m}{\mathbb{Z}})^{\times}
since
\chi^{-2}
is still a primitive character modulo
q^{m}
.
Thus

\displaystyle I=

\displaystyle G(\chi^{-2},\psi)\sum_{b\in({\mathbb{Z}}/q^{m}{\mathbb{Z}})^{\times}}(\lambda\chi_{K})^{-1}(1+b\theta^{\prime})\chi_{K}(b)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle G(\chi^{-2},\psi)\sum_{b\in({\mathbb{Z}}/q^{m}{\mathbb{Z}})^{\times}}(\lambda\chi_{K})^{-1}(b^{-1}+\theta^{\prime})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle G(\chi^{-2},\psi)\sum_{b\in({\mathbb{Z}}/q^{m}{\mathbb{Z}})^{\times}}(\lambda\chi_{K})^{-1}(b+\theta^{\prime}).

Similarly, we have

\displaystyle J=

\displaystyle G(\chi^{-2},\psi)\sum_{a\in q{\mathbb{Z}}/q^{m}{\mathbb{Z}}}(\lambda\chi_{K})^{-1}(a+\theta^{\prime}).

Hence

I+J=G(\chi^{-2},\psi)\sum_{a\in{\mathbb{Z}}/q^{m}{\mathbb{Z}}}(\lambda\chi_{K})^{-1}(a+\theta^{\prime}).

(7.4)

Note that

G(\chi,\psi)^{2}G(\chi^{-2},\psi)=q^{m}J(\chi,\chi)

(7.5)

since

G(\chi,\psi)^{2}=G(\chi^{2},\psi)J(\chi,\chi)

and
G(\chi^{2},\psi)G(\chi^{-2},\psi)=q^{m}

as
\chi,\chi^{2}
are primitive modulo
q^{m}
.

Now we have

\displaystyle G(\chi,\psi)^{2}\chi^{-1}(u^{2}\theta^{2}v)\frac{G((\lambda\chi_{K})^{-1},\psi_{K})}{G(\lambda^{-1},\psi_{K})}=

\displaystyle G(\chi,\psi)^{2}\chi(-4{v^{-1}})\frac{G\left((\lambda\chi_{K})^{-1},\psi_{K}\left(\frac{\cdot}{2\theta^{\prime}}\right)\right)}{G\left(\lambda^{-1},\psi_{K}\left(\frac{\cdot}{2\theta^{\prime}}\right)\right)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\chi(-4{v^{-1}})}{G(\lambda^{-1},\psi_{K}(\frac{\cdot}{2\theta^{\prime}}))}G(\chi,\psi)^{2}G(\chi^{-2},\psi)\sum_{a\in{\mathbb{Z}}/q^{m}{\mathbb{Z}}}(\lambda\chi_{K})^{-1}(a+\theta^{\prime})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\chi(-4{v^{-1}})J(\chi,\chi)\sum_{a\in{\mathbb{Z}}/q^{m}{\mathbb{Z}}}(\lambda\chi_{K})^{-1}(a+\theta^{\prime})\right).

Here the first equality follows from (
7.3
), the second from (
7.4
) and the third from (
7.5
).

As seen in the proof of
[
63
, Prop. 3.7]

G\left(\lambda^{-1},\psi_{K}\left(\frac{\cdot}{2\theta^{\prime}}\right)\right)=q^{m}
, and so the proof concludes.
∎

In view of Theorem
7.9
and Lemma
7.25
it is natural to consider the following function on
{\mathbb{Z}}_{q}
:

F_{v}(a)=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\chi\in\widehat{({\mathbb{Z}}/q^{m}{\mathbb{Z}})^{\times}}\\
primitive\end{subarray}}\chi(-4v)J(\chi,\chi)\chi^{-1}(a^{2}-\theta^{\prime 2})

(7.6)

for
v\in({\mathbb{Z}}/q^{m}{\mathbb{Z}})^{\times}
. Note that
F_{v}
depends on
\theta^{\prime}=\theta u
.

To explicitly describe
F_{v}
, we recall the following elementary facts.

Fact 7.26
.

Let
m\geq 2
be an integer.
For
a\in({\mathbb{Z}}/q^{m}{\mathbb{Z}})^{\times}
,

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\chi\in\widehat{({\mathbb{Z}}/q^{m}{\mathbb{Z}})^{\times}}\\
primitive\end{subarray}}\chi(a)=\begin{cases}(q-1)^{2}q^{m-2},\quad&a\equiv 1\pmod{q^{m}},\\
-(q-1)q^{m-2},\quad&a\equiv 1\pmod{q^{m-1}},a\equiv\hskip-10.0pt/\ 1\pmod{q^{m}},\\
0,\quad&a\equiv\hskip-10.0pt/\ 1\pmod{q^{m-1}}.\\
\end{cases}

Fact 7.27
.

Consider a quadratic equation

X^{2}+2bX+c=0

(7.7)

with
a,b\in{\mathbb{Z}}/q^{k}{\mathbb{Z}}
for an odd prime
q
and
k\geq 1
.

\bullet

If
b^{2}-c
is not a square in
{\mathbb{Z}}/q^{k}{\mathbb{Z}}
, then (
7.7
) has no solution in
{\mathbb{Z}}/q^{k}{\mathbb{Z}}
.

\bullet

If
b^{2}-c=v^{2}
is a square with
v\in({\mathbb{Z}}/q^{k}{\mathbb{Z}})^{\times}
, then (
7.7
) has exactly
2
solutions.

\bullet

If
b^{2}-c=v^{2}
is a square with
q|v
, let
t={\rm ord}_{q}(b^{2}-c)\in\{1,\cdots k\}
.

–

If
t=k
, then (
7.7
) has
q^{\lfloor k/2\rfloor}
solutions.

–

If
t<k
,write
t=2r
with
1\leq r<k/2
, then (
7.7
) has
2q^{r}
solutions.

We have the following explicit description of the function
F_{v}
in certain cases
20
20

20

In view of Theorem
7.9
and Lemma
7.25
the congruence conditions appearing in Lemma
7.28
suffice for our application.
.

Lemma 7.28
.

Let
m\geq 2
be an integer,
v\in({\mathbb{Z}}/q^{m}{\mathbb{Z}})^{\times}
with

v\equiv 1\pmod{q^{m-1}}

and
F_{v}
a function on
{\mathbb{Z}}_{q}
as in (
7.6
).
Let
a_{0}\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{q}^{\times}
be a solution of
1+(a^{2}-{\theta^{2}u^{2}})=0
.

(i)

If
m
is even and
v=1
, then

\displaystyle F_{v}=

\displaystyle q^{\frac{3m}{2}-2}(q-1)^{2}\left(1_{a_{0}(1+q^{m}{\mathbb{Z}}_{q})}+1_{-a_{0}(1+q^{m}{\mathbb{Z}}_{q})}-\frac{1}{q-1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}d\in\{a_{0}(1+q^{m-1}u)\ |\ 1\leq u\leq q-1\}\end{subarray}}(1_{d(1+q^{m}{\mathbb{Z}}_{q})}+1_{-d(1+q^{m}{\mathbb{Z}}_{q})})\right).

(ii)

If
m
is odd, write
v=1+wq^{m-1}
for
w\in{\mathbb{F}}_{q}
, then

\displaystyle F_{v}=

\displaystyle q^{\frac{3(m-1)}{2}}(q-1)\left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}d\in\{a_{0}(1+q^{m-1}u)\ |\ u=0,\cdots,q-1\}\\
w+2a_{0}^{2}u\in{\mathbb{F}}_{q}^{\times 2}\end{subarray}}(1_{d(1+q^{m}{\mathbb{Z}}_{q})}+1_{-d(1+q^{m}{\mathbb{Z}}_{q})})\right)

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle q^{\frac{3(m-1)}{2}}(q-1)\left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}d\in\{a_{0}(1+q^{m-1}u)\ |\ u=0,\cdots,q-1\}\\
w+2a_{0}^{2}u\in{\mathbb{F}}_{q}^{\times}\backslash{\mathbb{F}}_{q}^{\times 2}\end{subarray}}(1_{d(1+q^{m}{\mathbb{Z}}_{q})}+1_{-d(1+q^{m}{\mathbb{Z}}_{q})})\right).

Proof.

In the following we simply denote
\theta u
by
\theta^{\prime}
and fix
a\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{q}
.

Note that

F_{v}(a)=\sum_{x\in({\mathbb{Z}}/q^{m}{\mathbb{Z}})^{\times}}\left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\chi\in\widehat{({\mathbb{Z}}/q^{m}{\mathbb{Z}})^{\times}}\\
primitive\end{subarray}}\chi(v(4x^{2}-4x)(a^{2}-\theta^{\prime 2})^{-1})\right).

Thus in view of Fact
7.26
a necessary condition for
F_{v}(a)\neq 0
is that the equation

v(4x^{2}-4x)\equiv(a^{2}-\theta^{\prime 2})\pmod{q^{m-1}}

(7.8)

has a solution
x\in({\mathbb{Z}}/q^{m}{\mathbb{Z}})^{\times}
. (Note that
a^{2}-\theta^{\prime 2}
is a
q
-adic unit.)

Let
A
be the number of solutions
x
of

v(4x^{2}-4x)\equiv(a^{2}-\theta^{\prime 2})\pmod{q^{m-1}}

(7.9)

in
({\mathbb{Z}}/q^{m}{\mathbb{Z}})^{\times}
and
B
that of

v(4x^{2}-4x)\equiv(a^{2}-\theta^{\prime 2})\pmod{q^{m}}

(7.10)

in
({\mathbb{Z}}/q^{m}{\mathbb{Z}})^{\times}
.
By Fact
7.26
,

F_{v}(a)=B(q-1)^{2}q^{m-2}-(A-B)(q-1)q^{m-2}.

Note that the discriminant of (
7.8
) in
{\mathbb{Z}}/q^{m-1}{\mathbb{Z}}
equals

16(v^{2}+v(a^{2}-\theta^{\prime 2}))\equiv 16(1+(a^{2}-\theta^{\prime 2}))\pmod{q^{m-1}}

since
m\geq 2
and
v\equiv 1\pmod{q^{m-1}}
.
In particular,
F_{v}(a)\neq 0
implies that

1+(a^{2}-\theta^{\prime 2})\pmod{q}\in{\mathbb{F}}_{q}^{2}.

We now consider the following cases.

•

Suppose that
1+(a^{2}-\theta^{\prime 2})\pmod{q}\in{\mathbb{F}}_{q}^{\times 2}
.

Then the discriminant of (
7.8
) is a square and a unit in
{\mathbb{Z}}/q^{m-1}{\mathbb{Z}}
. Hence
(
7.9
)
has
2
solutions in
({\mathbb{Z}}/q^{m-1}{\mathbb{Z}})^{\times}
by Fact
7.27
and
2q
solutions in
({\mathbb{Z}}/q^{m}{\mathbb{Z}})^{\times}
and exactly two of such
x
satisfy
(
7.10
)
by Fact
7.27
again. So
F_{v}(a)=0
.

•

Suppose that
1+(a^{2}-\theta^{\prime 2})\equiv 0\pmod{q}
. Then
a
is a unit. We consider the following three subcases.

(a)

Moreover, suppose that
v^{2}+v(a^{2}-\theta^{\prime 2})\equiv 0\pmod{q^{m}}
, and so
1+(a^{2}-\theta^{\prime 2})\equiv 0\pmod{q^{m-1}}
.
Then
(
7.9
)
has

q^{\lfloor{(m-1)}/2\rfloor}
solutions in
({\mathbb{Z}}/q^{m-1}{\mathbb{Z}})^{\times}
by Fact
7.27
and hence
q\cdot q^{\lfloor{(m-1)}/2\rfloor}
solutions in
({\mathbb{Z}}/q^{m}{\mathbb{Z}})^{\times}
.
On the other hand,
(
7.10
)
has
q^{\lfloor{m}/2\rfloor}
solutions in
({\mathbb{Z}}/q^{m}{\mathbb{Z}})^{\times}
by Fact
7.27
.
So

F_{v}(a)=\begin{cases}q^{m/2}(q-1)^{2}q^{m-2},\quad&\text{if $m$ is even}\\
0,\quad&\text{if $m$ is odd.}\\
\end{cases}

(b)

Suppose that
1+(a^{2}-\theta^{\prime 2})\equiv 0\pmod{q^{m-1}}
but
v^{2}+v(a^{2}-\theta^{\prime 2})\equiv\hskip-10.0pt/\ 0\pmod{q^{m}}
.

Then
(
7.9
) has

q^{\lfloor{(m-1)}/2\rfloor}
solutions in
({\mathbb{Z}}/q^{m-1}{\mathbb{Z}})^{\times}

and

q\cdot q^{\lfloor{(m-1)}/2\rfloor}
solutions in
({\mathbb{Z}}/q^{m}{\mathbb{Z}})^{\times}
.

Note that (
7.10
)
has a solution only if
m
is odd
21
21

21

Only then the discriminant has even
q
-adic valuation.
, and in this case, it has

\begin{cases}2q^{{(m-1)}/2},\quad&\text{if $v^{2}+v(a^{2}-\theta^{\prime 2})\in({\mathbb{Z}}/q^{m}{\mathbb{Z}})^{2}$}\\
0,\quad&\text{if $v^{2}+v(a^{2}-\theta^{\prime 2})\notin({\mathbb{Z}}/q^{m}{\mathbb{Z}})^{2}$}\end{cases}

solutions. So

F_{v}(a)=\begin{cases}-q\cdot q^{\lfloor{(m-1)}/2\rfloor}(q-1)q^{m-2},\quad&\text{if $m$ is even}\\
q\cdot q^{\lfloor{(m-1)}/2\rfloor}(q-1)q^{m-2},\quad&\text{if $m$ is odd and if $v^{2}+v(a^{2}-\theta^{\prime 2})\in({\mathbb{Z}}/q^{m}{\mathbb{Z}})^{2}$.}\\
-q\cdot q^{\lfloor{(m-1)}/2\rfloor}(q-1)q^{m-2}\quad&\text{if $m$ is odd and if $v^{2}+v(a^{2}-\theta^{\prime 2})\notin({\mathbb{Z}}/q^{m}{\mathbb{Z}})^{2}$.}\\
\end{cases}

The following calculation gives criterion for
v^{2}+v(a^{2}-\theta^{\prime 2})\in({\mathbb{Z}}/q^{m}{\mathbb{Z}})^{2}
. Write
a=a_{0}(1+q^{m-1}u)
for
u\in{\mathbb{F}}_{q}
and
v=1+wq^{m-1}
for
w\in{\mathbb{F}}_{q}
.
Then

\displaystyle v^{2}+v(a^{2}-\theta^{\prime 2})\equiv

\displaystyle(w+2a_{0}^{2}u)q^{m-1}\pmod{q^{m}}.

(c)

Suppose that
1+(a^{2}-\theta^{\prime 2})\equiv\hskip-10.0pt/\ 0\pmod{q^{m-1}}
, and put

t=v_{q}(1+(a^{2}-\theta^{\prime 2}))=v_{q}(v^{2}+v(a^{2}-\theta^{\prime 2}))\in\{1,\cdots,m-2\}.

If
t
is odd, then
(
7.9
) has no solution, and hence

F_{v}(a)=0.

Suppose that
t
is even with
t=2r
.
If
1+(a^{2}-\theta^{\prime 2})
is not a square in
{\mathbb{Z}}/q^{m}{\mathbb{Z}}
, then neither (
7.9
) nor (
7.10
) have a solution in
{\mathbb{Z}}/q^{m}{\mathbb{Z}}
. It follows that
F_{v}(a)=0
.
On the other hand, if
1+(a^{2}-\theta^{\prime 2})
is a square in
{\mathbb{Z}}/q^{m}{\mathbb{Z}}
and then so is
v^{2}+v(a^{2}-\theta^{\prime 2})
.
Consequently
(
7.9
)
has
2q^{r}
solution in
({\mathbb{Z}}/q^{m-1}{\mathbb{Z}})
by Fact
7.27
and in turn has
2q^{r+1}
solutions in
({\mathbb{Z}}/q^{m}{\mathbb{Z}})
.
Note that (
7.10
)
has
2q^{r}
solutions in
({\mathbb{Z}}/q^{m}{\mathbb{Z}})
by Fact
7.27
. Thus we still have

F_{v}(a)=0.

∎

7.4.3.
Explicit formulas for sum of twist epsilon factors

Proposition 7.29
.

In our setting the following holds.

(i)

If
m
is even, then

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\chi\in\widehat{({\mathbb{Z}}/q^{m}{\mathbb{Z}})^{\times}}\\
primitive\end{subarray}}G(\chi,\psi)^{2}\chi^{-1}(u^{2}\theta^{2})\epsilon(1/2,\pi\otimes\chi,\psi)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle q^{\frac{3m}{2}-1}(q-1)\lambda(\theta)\left(\lambda^{-1}(a_{0}+{\theta u})+\lambda^{-1}(-a_{0}+\theta u)\right).

(ii)

If
m
is odd, then

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\chi\in\widehat{({\mathbb{Z}}/q^{m}{\mathbb{Z}})^{\times}}\\
primitive\end{subarray}}G(\chi,\psi)^{2}\chi^{-1}(u^{2}\theta^{2})\epsilon(1/2,\pi\otimes\chi,\psi)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle 2\sum_{v\in 1-q^{m-1}({\mathbb{Z}}/q{\mathbb{Z}})^{\times 2}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\chi\in\widehat{({\mathbb{Z}}/q^{m}{\mathbb{Z}})^{\times}},\\
primitive\end{subarray}}G(\chi,\psi)^{2}\chi^{-1}(u^{2}\theta^{2}v)\epsilon(1/2,\pi\otimes\chi,\psi)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(-1)^{m}\lambda(\theta)q^{\frac{3m-1}{2}}(q-1)\left(\lambda^{-1}(a_{0}+\theta^{\prime})+\lambda^{-1}(-a_{0}+\theta^{\prime})\right).

Proof.

In the following we simply denote
\theta u
by
\theta^{\prime}
.

We first consider the case (i).
Note that

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\chi\in\widehat{({\mathbb{Z}}/q^{m}{\mathbb{Z}})^{\times}}\\
primitive\end{subarray}}G(\chi,\psi)^{2}\chi^{-1}(u^{2}\theta^{2})\epsilon(1/2,\pi\otimes\chi,\psi)\quad

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(-1)^{m}\lambda(\theta)\left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\chi\in\widehat{({\mathbb{Z}}/q^{m}{\mathbb{Z}})^{\times}}\\
primitive\end{subarray}}\chi(-4)J(\chi,\chi)\sum_{a\in{\mathbb{Z}}/q^{m}{\mathbb{Z}}}(\lambda\chi_{K})^{-1}(a+\theta^{\prime})\right)\quad

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(-1)^{m}\lambda(\theta)q^{\frac{3m}{2}-2}(q-1)^{2}

\displaystyle\left(\lambda^{-1}(a_{0}+\theta^{\prime})+\lambda^{-1}(-a_{0}+\theta^{\prime})-\frac{1}{q-1}\left(\sum_{v=1}^{q-1}\lambda^{-1}(a_{0}(1+q^{m-1}v)+\theta^{\prime})+\lambda^{-1}(-a_{0}(1+q^{m-1}v)+\theta^{\prime})\right)\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(-1)^{m}\lambda(\theta)q^{\frac{3m}{2}-1}(q-1)\left(\lambda^{-1}(a_{0}+\theta^{\prime})+\lambda^{-1}(-a_{0}+\theta^{\prime})\right).

Here the first equality follows from Lemmas
7.24
, and
7.25
, the second from Lemma
7.28
, and the last from:

\displaystyle\sum_{v=1}^{q-1}\lambda^{-1}(a_{0}(1+q^{m-1}v)+\theta^{\prime})+\lambda^{-1}(-a_{0}(1+q^{m-1}v)+\theta^{\prime})

(7.11)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{v=1}^{q-1}\left(\lambda^{-1}(a_{0}+\theta^{\prime})\lambda^{-1}\left(1+\frac{q^{m-1}v}{a_{0}+\theta^{\prime}}\right)+\lambda^{-1}(-a_{0}+\theta^{\prime})\lambda^{-1}\left(1+\frac{q^{m-1}v}{-a_{0}+\theta^{\prime}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\lambda^{-1}(a_{0}+\theta^{\prime})-\lambda^{-1}(-a_{0}+\theta^{\prime}).

Now consider the case (ii). Just as above, by Lemmas
7.24
,
7.25
and
7.28
, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\chi\in\widehat{({\mathbb{Z}}/q^{m}{\mathbb{Z}})^{\times}}\\
primitive\end{subarray}}G(\chi,\psi)^{2}\chi^{-1}(u^{2}\theta^{2})\epsilon(1/2,\pi\otimes\chi,\psi)\quad

(7.12)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(-1)^{m}\lambda(\theta)q^{\frac{3(m-1)}{2}}(q-1)\left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a=a_{0}(1+q^{m-1}u)\in a_{0}(1+q^{m-1}({\mathbb{Z}}/q{\mathbb{Z}})^{\times})\\
\left(\frac{2u}{q}\right)=+1\end{subarray}}(\lambda^{-1}(a+\theta^{\prime})+\lambda^{-1}(-a+\theta^{\prime}))\right.

\displaystyle-\left.\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a=a_{0}(1+q^{m-1}u)\in a_{0}(1+q^{m-1}({\mathbb{Z}}/q{\mathbb{Z}})^{\times})\\
\left(\frac{2u}{q}\right)=-1\end{subarray}}(\lambda^{-1}(a+\theta^{\prime})+\lambda^{-1}(-a+\theta^{\prime}))\right).

Again by Lemmas
7.24
,
7.25
and
7.28
, and an analysis similar to (
7.12
), we have

\displaystyle\sum_{v\in 1-q^{m-1}({\mathbb{Z}}/q{\mathbb{Z}})^{\times 2}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\chi\in\widehat{({\mathbb{Z}}/q^{m}{\mathbb{Z}})^{\times}}\\
primitive\end{subarray}}G(\chi,\psi)^{2}\chi^{-1}(u^{2}\theta^{2}v)\epsilon(1/2,\pi\otimes\chi,\psi)\quad

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\displaystyle(-1)^{m}\lambda(\theta)}\sum_{v\in 1+q^{m-1}({\mathbb{Z}}/q{\mathbb{Z}})^{\times 2}}\left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\chi\in\widehat{({\mathbb{Z}}/q^{m}{\mathbb{Z}})^{\times}}\\
primitive\end{subarray}}\chi(-4v)J(\chi,\chi)\sum_{a\in{\mathbb{Z}}/q^{m}{\mathbb{Z}}}(\lambda\chi_{K})^{-1}(a+\theta^{\prime})\right)\quad

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\displaystyle(-1)^{m}\lambda(\theta)}q^{\frac{3(m-1)}{2}}(q-1)\left(\sum_{v\in 1+q^{m-1}({\mathbb{Z}}/q{\mathbb{Z}})^{\times 2}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a\in a_{0}(1+q^{m-1}({\mathbb{Z}}/q{\mathbb{Z}}))\\
w+2a_{0}^{2}u\in{\mathbb{F}}_{q}^{\times 2}\end{subarray}}(\lambda^{-1}(a+\theta^{\prime})+\lambda^{-1}(-a+\theta^{\prime}))\right)\quad

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle{\displaystyle(-1)^{m}\lambda(\theta)}q^{\frac{3(m-1)}{2}}(q-1)\left(\sum_{v\in 1+q^{m-1}({\mathbb{Z}}/q{\mathbb{Z}})^{\times 2}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a\in a_{0}(1+q^{m-1}({\mathbb{Z}}/q{\mathbb{Z}}))\\
w+2a_{0}^{2}u\in{\mathbb{F}}_{q}^{\times}\backslash{\mathbb{F}}_{q}^{\times 2}\end{subarray}}(\lambda^{-1}(a+\theta^{\prime})+\lambda^{-1}(-a+\theta^{\prime}))\right),

where
v=1+q^{m-1}w
and
a=a_{0}(1+q^{m-1}u)
.

Put

S_{1}(u)=\{w\in{\mathbb{F}}_{q}^{\times 2}\ |\ 2a_{0}^{2}u+w\in{\mathbb{F}}_{q}^{\times 2}\},

and

S_{2}(u)=\{w\in{\mathbb{F}}_{q}^{\times 2}\ |\ 2a_{0}^{2}u+w\in{\mathbb{F}}_{q}^{\times}\backslash{\mathbb{F}}_{q}^{\times 2}\}.

Fact 7.30
.

Let
q
be an odd prime.

Given
k\in{\mathbb{F}}_{q}
, the cardinality of solutions of
x^{2}-y^{2}=k
in
{\mathbb{F}}_{q}^{2}
is given by
\begin{cases}q-1,&\quad k\neq 0,\\
2q-1,&\quad k=0.\end{cases}

Proof of Fact
7.30
.

First consider the case
k\neq 0
. Consider the projective curve

x^{2}-y^{2}=kz^{2}

which has
q+1
solutions in
{\mathbb{P}}^{2}({\mathbb{F}}_{q})
and has
2
solutions in
{\mathbb{P}}^{2}({\mathbb{F}}_{q})
with
z=0
. Thus for
k\neq 0
, the equation
x^{2}-y^{2}=k
has
q-1
solutions in
{\mathbb{F}}_{q}^{2}
.

If
k=0
, then the equation becomes
x^{2}-y^{2}=0
, which has
2(q-1)
non-zero solutions and
1
zero solutions in
{\mathbb{F}}_{q}^{2}
.
∎

As a consequence, we have

\#S_{1}(u)=\begin{cases}\frac{q-1}{4},&\quad u\neq 0,q\equiv 1\pmod{4},\eta(2u)=-1,\\
\frac{q-5}{4},&\quad u\neq 0,q\equiv 1\pmod{4},\eta(2u)=+1,\\
\frac{q-3}{4},&\quad u\neq 0,q\equiv 3\pmod{4},\\
\frac{q-1}{2},&\quad u=0.\end{cases}

If
u\neq 0
, then

\#S_{1}(u)+\#S_{2}(u)=\frac{q-1}{2}-\frac{\eta(-2u)+1}{2}.

From the above analysis, note that the substraction

\displaystyle{\displaystyle(-1)^{m}\lambda(\theta)}q^{\frac{3(m-1)}{2}}(q-1)\left(\sum_{v\in 1+q^{m-1}({\mathbb{Z}}/q{\mathbb{Z}})^{\times 2}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a\in a_{0}(1+q^{m-1}({\mathbb{Z}}/q{\mathbb{Z}}))\\
w+2a_{0}^{2}u\in{\mathbb{F}}_{q}^{\times 2}\end{subarray}}(\lambda^{-1}(a+\theta^{\prime})+\lambda^{-1}(-a+\theta^{\prime}))\right)\quad

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle{\displaystyle(-1)^{m}\lambda(\theta)}q^{\frac{3(m-1)}{2}}(q-1)\left(\sum_{v\in 1+q^{m-1}({\mathbb{Z}}/q{\mathbb{Z}})^{\times 2}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a\in a_{0}(1+q^{m-1}({\mathbb{Z}}/q{\mathbb{Z}}))\\
w+2a_{0}^{2}u\in{\mathbb{F}}_{q}^{\times}\backslash{\mathbb{F}}_{q}^{\times 2}\end{subarray}}(\lambda^{-1}(a+\theta^{\prime})+\lambda^{-1}(-a+\theta^{\prime}))\right)

equals

{(-1)^{m}\lambda(\theta)}q^{\frac{3(m-1)}{2}}(q-1)\left(\frac{q-1}{2}(\lambda^{-1}(a_{0}+\theta^{\prime})+\lambda^{-1}(-a_{0}+\theta^{\prime}))-\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a\in a_{0}(1+q^{m-1}({\mathbb{Z}}/q{\mathbb{Z}}))\\
\eta(2u)=+1\end{subarray}}(\lambda^{-1}(a+\theta^{\prime})+\lambda^{-1}(-a+\theta^{\prime}))\right).

(7.13)

Combining (
7.12
) and (
7.13
), we have

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\chi\in\widehat{({\mathbb{Z}}/q^{m}{\mathbb{Z}})^{\times}}\\
primitive\end{subarray}}G(\chi,\psi)^{2}\chi^{-1}(u^{2}\theta^{2})\epsilon(1/2,\pi\otimes\chi,\psi)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle 2\sum_{v\in 1-q^{m-1}({\mathbb{Z}}/q{\mathbb{Z}})^{\times 2}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\chi\in\widehat{({\mathbb{Z}}/q^{m}{\mathbb{Z}})^{\times}},\\
primitive\end{subarray}}G(\chi,\psi)^{2}\chi^{-1}(u^{2}\theta^{2}v)\epsilon(1/2,\pi\otimes\chi,\psi)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(-1)^{m}\lambda(\theta)q^{\frac{3(m-1)}{2}}(q-1)\left((q-1)(\lambda^{-1}(a_{0}+\theta^{\prime})+\lambda^{-1}(-a_{0}+\theta^{\prime}))\right.

\displaystyle\left.-\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a\in a_{0}(1+q^{m-1}({\mathbb{Z}}/q{\mathbb{Z}})^{\times})\end{subarray}}(\lambda^{-1}(a+\theta^{\prime})+\lambda^{-1}(-a+\theta^{\prime}))\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(-1)^{m}\lambda(\theta)q^{\frac{3m-1}{2}}(q-1)\left(\lambda^{-1}(a_{0}+\theta^{\prime})+\lambda^{-1}(-a_{0}+\theta^{\prime})\right).

Here the last equality proceeds as follows:
For
a=a_{0}(1+q^{m-1}u)
, we have

(a_{0}(1+q^{m-1}u))+\theta^{\prime}\equiv(a_{0}+\theta^{\prime})(1+q^{m-1}\frac{ua_{0}}{a_{0}+\theta^{\prime}})\pmod{p^{m}}

and hence
\lambda^{-1}(a+\theta^{\prime})=\lambda^{-1}(a_{0}+\theta^{\prime})\lambda^{-1}(1+q^{m-1}\frac{2ua_{0}}{2(a_{0}+\theta^{\prime})})
.
Note that
\lambda^{-1}(1+q^{m-1}\frac{a_{0}}{2(a_{0}+\theta^{\prime})})=\zeta_{q}
for

\zeta_{q}
a
q
-th primitive root of unity, and
as
u
runs over
{\mathbb{F}}_{q}^{\times}
, then
\lambda^{-1}(1+q^{m-1}\frac{a_{0}u}{2(a_{0}+\theta^{\prime})})
runs over all
q
-th primitive root of unity.
Since

\sum_{a\in{\mathbb{F}}_{q}^{\times}}\zeta_{q}^{a}=-1,

the equality follows.

∎

7.4.4.
Proof of Theorem
7.1

The following is a combination of the previous results of this section.

By Lemma
7.23
, we have

\frac{q-q\eta(-1)\epsilon(\pi)\epsilon(\pi\otimes\eta)}{q-1}=\frac{2q}{q-1}.

By Proposition
7.29
for
m
even, we have

q^{\lfloor\frac{m}{2}\rfloor}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\chi\in\widehat{({\mathbb{Z}}/q^{m}{\mathbb{Z}})^{\times}}\\
primitive\end{subarray}}\frac{G(\chi,\psi)^{2}}{\varphi(q^{m})^{2}}\chi^{-1}(u^{2}\theta^{2})\epsilon(1/2,\pi\otimes\chi,\psi)=\frac{q}{q-1}\lambda(\theta)\left(\lambda^{-1}(a_{0}+\theta u)+\lambda^{-1}(-a_{0}+{\theta u})\right),

and for
m
odd,

\displaystyle q^{\lfloor\frac{m}{2}\rfloor}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\chi\in\widehat{({\mathbb{Z}}/q^{m}{\mathbb{Z}})^{\times}}\\
primitive\end{subarray}}\frac{G(\chi,\psi)^{2}}{\varphi(q^{m})^{2}}\chi^{-1}(u^{2}\theta^{2})\epsilon(1/2,\pi\otimes\chi,\psi)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle 2q^{(m-1)/2}\sum_{v\in 1-q^{m-1}({\mathbb{Z}}/q{\mathbb{Z}})^{\times 2}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\chi\in\widehat{({\mathbb{Z}}/q^{m}{\mathbb{Z}})^{\times}}\\
primitive\end{subarray}}\frac{G(\chi,\psi)^{2}}{\varphi(q^{m})^{2}}\chi^{-1}(u^{2}\theta^{2}v)\epsilon(1/2,\pi\otimes\chi,\psi)\quad

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{-q}{q-1}\lambda(\theta)(\lambda^{-1}(a_{0}+\theta u)+\lambda^{-1}(-a_{0}+\theta u)).

Recall that
\epsilon(\pi)=(-1)^{m}\lambda(\theta)
, and so the result follows from Theorem
7.9
.
